| §4.4 / FV Thm 3.21 | **General FKG for arbitrary monotone observables** (nonnegativity hypothesis removed) | **Done (3 linearity lemmas + main theorem)** | `Inequalities/FKGGeneral.lean`. `fkg_ising` (inheriting Mathlib `fkg`'s interface) requires `0 ≤ f`, `0 ≤ g`; the genuine FV Theorem 3.21 holds for **any** monotone `f, g` of arbitrary sign. `fkg_ising_of_monotone`: for ferromagnetic `p` and monotone nondecreasing `f, g : Config ι → ℝ`, `⟨f⟩⟨g⟩ ≤ ⟨fg⟩` — no nonnegativity assumption. Proof by covariance shift-invariance: the Gibbs covariance `⟨fg⟩ − ⟨f⟩⟨g⟩` is unchanged under adding constants to `f, g` (constant terms cancel by linearity + normalisation `⟨1⟩ = 1`); subtracting the finite minima `a = min f`, `b = min g` gives nonnegative monotone `f' = f − a ≥ 0`, `g' = g − b ≥ 0` with the same covariance, and `fkg_ising` applied to `f', g'` closes it (`nlinarith`). Supporting Gibbs-expectation linearity lemmas `gibbsExpectation_const` (`⟨c⟩ = c`), `gibbsExpectation_add` (`⟨F+H⟩ = ⟨F⟩+⟨H⟩`), `gibbsExpectation_const_mul` (`⟨cF⟩ = c⟨F⟩`) are proved alongside. |
| Refactor | **Generic weighted-expectation linearity** (dedup of 9 lemmas) | **Done (3 generic helpers; refactor)** | `Inequalities/WeightedExpectation.lean`. The constant / additivity / scalar linearity of a normalised weighted average `Z⁻¹ ∑_σ F(σ)·w(σ)` is purely algebraic, so it is proved **once** as `weightedExpectation_const` / `weightedExpectation_add` / `weightedExpectation_const_mul` (generic in the weight `w` and partition `Z = ∑ w`). The nine duplicated proofs of `gibbsExpectation_*` (FKGGeneral), `gibbsExpectationJ_*` (FKGInhomogeneous), and `gibbsExpectationBC_*` (FKGBoundaryCondition) now delegate to these helpers (single source of truth; faster builds). Public lemma names/signatures unchanged. |
| FV Thm 3.21 | **FKG with inhomogeneous per-edge couplings** (free boundary, arbitrary field) | **Done (Gibbs stack + log-supermodularity + 2 FKG theorems; Part of #3558)** | `Inequalities/FKGInhomogeneous.lean`. FV Theorem 3.21 allows a **collection of nonnegative couplings** `J = (J_{ij})` (one per edge) and an **arbitrary field** `h`, not just the uniform scalar `IsingParams.J`. New inhomogeneous Gibbs stack: `interactionEnergyJ` (`-∑_e J(e)·s(σ_i)s(σ_j)`), `hamiltonianJ`, `boltzmannWeightJ` (`exp(-βH)`), `boltzmannWeightJ_pos`, `partitionFunctionJ` (+`_pos`/`_ne_zero`), `gibbsExpectationJ` (+linearity `_const`/`_add`/`_const_mul`). Key lattice condition `boltzmannWeightJ_log_supermodular`: for `β ≥ 0` and `J(e) ≥ 0` (field `h` **arbitrary** — its term is modular), `w(σ)w(σ') ≤ w(σ⊔σ')w(σ⊓σ')`; per-edge supermodularity (`spin_edge_supermodularJ`, 16-case) summed with nonnegative weights `J(e)`. FKG theorems: `fkg_isingJ` (nonnegative monotone `f,g`, via Mathlib `fkg`) and `fkg_isingJ_of_monotone` (**arbitrary**-sign monotone `f,g`, via covariance shift). This matches FV 3.21 exactly for the free-boundary case (nonnegative per-edge `J`, arbitrary `h`); arbitrary boundary conditions remain follow-up (#3558). |
| FV Thm 3.21 (full) | **FKG with arbitrary boundary conditions** (conditioning route) | **Done (BC measure + log-supermodularity + 2 FKG theorems; Part of #3558)** | `Inequalities/FKGBoundaryCondition.lean`. Completes FV Theorem 3.21 in full generality (nonnegative inhomogeneous `J`, arbitrary field `h`, **arbitrary boundary condition** `η`). The boundary-condition Gibbs measure is the inhomogeneous Boltzmann weight (#3559) **conditioned** on configurations agreeing with `η` outside `Λ` — no new Hamiltonian, since the boundary interactions are already in `boltzmannWeightJ`. Key insight: the set `{σ \| agreesOff Λ η σ}` is a **sublattice** (`agreesOff_sup`/`agreesOff_inf`: off `Λ` both equal `η i`, so `⊔`/`⊓` stay `η i` via `sup_idem`/`inf_idem`), so `boltzmannWeightBC := Set.indicator {σ \| agreesOff Λ η σ} boltzmannWeightJ` is again log-supermodular (`boltzmannWeightBC_log_supermodular`: agreeing case = inhomogeneous log-supermodularity, disagreeing case = `0 ≤` nonneg). `partitionFunctionBC` (`+_pos` via `η` itself in the conditioned set), `gibbsExpectationBC` (`+_const`/`_add`/`_const_mul`). FKG theorems `fkg_isingBC` (nonnegative) and `fkg_isingBC_of_monotone` (arbitrary-sign monotone, via covariance shift). With #3559 this realizes the complete FV 3.21 statement (collection of nonnegative couplings + arbitrary `h` + arbitrary boundary condition). |
| §4.1 / FV §3.6.2 | **Field monotonicity of the Gibbs expectation** (Holley), free and boundary-condition versions | **Done (2 cross-supermodularity + 4 monotonicity theorems)** | `Inequalities/MonotonicityField.lean`. Increasing the external field `h` increases the Gibbs expectation of every monotone observable: `h ≤ h' ⟹ ⟨φ⟩_h ≤ ⟨φ⟩_{h'}` for nondecreasing `φ`. Foundational for the stochastic ordering of Ising measures, the `±` extremal states, and the infinite-volume limit. Proved via Mathlib's **`holley`** (`Combinatorics.SetFamily.FourFunctions`): the two normalised Boltzmann weights at fields `h ≤ h'` satisfy the Holley domination condition `boltzmannWeight_field_cross_supermodular`: `w_h(a)·w_{h'}(b) ≤ w_h(a⊓b)·w_{h'}(a⊔b)` — interaction part = edge log-supermodularity (`spin_edge_supermodular_F`, weight `βJ ≥ 0`); field part = per-site `field_site_mono` (`h·s(a)+h'·s(b) ≤ h·s(a⊓b)+h'·s(a⊔b)` for `h ≤ h'`, weight `β ≥ 0`). `gibbsExpectation_field_mono_of_nonneg` (nonnegative `φ`, via `holley` on the normalised measures) and `gibbsExpectation_field_mono` (**arbitrary** monotone `φ`, via the constant shift since `⟨φ⟩_h ≤ ⟨φ⟩_{h'}` is shift-invariant). The same file also carries the **boundary-condition** version (generic in the graph, no `ℤ^d` dependence; relocated here from `Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/MagnetizationFieldMonotone.lean` by Issue #4857): `boltzmannWeightBC_field_cross_supermodular` — field cross-supermodularity of the `η`-boundary Boltzmann weights (the boundary-agreement sublattice closure `agreesOff_inf`/`agreesOff_sup` reduces the both-agree case to the unconditioned `boltzmannWeight_field_cross_supermodular`; otherwise the left side vanishes) — and `gibbsExpectationBC_field_mono_of_nonneg` / `gibbsExpectationBC_field_mono`, field monotonicity of the `η`-boundary expectation of a monotone observable (Holley + subtract-the-minimum, mirroring the unconditioned pair). Reference for the boundary-condition version: Friedli–Velenik (Cambridge, 2017), §3.6 (Holley's inequality, Theorem 3.50). |
| FV §3.6.2 / §6 | **Boundary-condition monotonicity** (Holley) | **Done (cross-supermodularity + 2 monotonicity theorems)** | `Inequalities/MonotonicityBoundary.lean`. Raising the boundary condition raises the Gibbs expectation of every monotone observable: `η ≤ η' ⟹ ⟨φ⟩^η_Λ ≤ ⟨φ⟩^{η'}_Λ` for nondecreasing `φ`. **Cornerstone of the extremal `±` Gibbs states and the infinite-volume limit** (the `+` boundary gives the maximal state, the `−` the minimal). Proved via Mathlib's `holley` on the conditioned boundary-condition weights (#3560). The order-aware conditioning closure is the key: `agreesOff_inf_of_le`/`agreesOff_sup_of_le` — if `a` agrees with `η` off `Λ` and `b` with `η'`, then (since `η ≤ η'`) `a⊓b` agrees with `η` (`η⊓η'=η`) and `a⊔b` agrees with `η'` (`η⊔η'=η'`). Hence `boltzmannWeightBC_cross_supermodular`: `w^η(a)·w^{η'}(b) ≤ w^η(a⊓b)·w^{η'}(a⊔b)` (agreeing case = `boltzmannWeightJ_log_supermodular`, else `0 ≤` nonneg). `gibbsExpectationBC_boundary_mono_of_nonneg` (nonnegative `φ`) and `gibbsExpectationBC_boundary_mono` (arbitrary monotone `φ`, via constant shift). |
| FV §6 | **Extremal `±` boundary states + magnetization ordering** | **Done (extremal configs + bracketing + magnetization sandwich)** | `Inequalities/MonotonicityExtremal.lean`. The boundary-condition monotonicity (#3562) is sharpest at the extremal configurations `plusConfig` (all up, maximal) and `minusConfig` (all down, minimal): `le_plusConfig`/`minusConfig_le` (`σ ≤ plusConfig`, `minusConfig ≤ σ` via `Spin.le_up`/`Spin.down_le`). For any boundary condition `η` and monotone `φ`, `gibbsExpectationBC_extremal_sandwich`: `⟨φ⟩^−_Λ ≤ ⟨φ⟩^η_Λ ≤ ⟨φ⟩^+_Λ` — the `±` boundary conditions bracket all boundary conditions — at this finite volume the `+` boundary condition gives the largest expectation of every monotone observable, the `−` the smallest (the foundation for the extremal infinite-volume `±` Gibbs states constructed as thermodynamic limits). Concrete application: `spin_sign_monotone` (`Spin.sign ℝ` monotone) + `singleSpinObs_monotone` (`σ ↦ s(σ_i)` monotone) give `magnetizationBC_extremal_sandwich`: the local magnetization `⟨σ_i⟩^η_Λ` is squeezed between the `±` states. A finite-volume bracketing, foundational for the extremal infinite-volume `±` states. |
| FV Lemma 3.22 | **Volume monotonicity of the `+` state** (conditioning + FKG) | **Done (shell indicator + weight identity + antitone theorem)** | `Inequalities/VolumeMonotonicity.lean`. The `+` boundary expectation of a monotone observable **decreases as the volume grows**: `Λ₁ ⊆ Λ₂ ⟹ ⟨φ⟩^+_{Λ₂} ≤ ⟨φ⟩^+_{Λ₁}` (`gibbsExpectationBC_plus_volume_antitone`). With the dual `−`-state bound this exhibits the `±` states as **monotone limits over an exhaustion** — the construction of the extremal infinite-volume Gibbs states (the project's central goal). Proof by the conditioning structure: the `Λ₁` `+` state is the `Λ₂` `+` state conditioned on the shell `Λ₂∖Λ₁` being all `+` (`agreesOff_plus_iff_shell`: `agreesOff Λ₁ + = (+ on shell) ∧ agreesOff Λ₂ +`). The shell event is an **up-set**, so its indicator `upShellIndicator` is monotone (`upShellIndicator_monotone`), and the weight identity `boltzmannWeightBC_plus_eq_shell_mul` (`w^+_{Λ₁} = 1_A·w^+_{Λ₂}`) gives `⟨φ·1_A⟩^+_{Λ₂} = ⟨φ⟩^+_{Λ₁}·⟨1_A⟩^+_{Λ₂}`; the FKG inequality `⟨φ·1_A⟩ ≥ ⟨φ⟩⟨1_A⟩` (`fkg_isingBC_of_monotone`, both monotone) then yields `⟨φ⟩^+_{Λ₂} ≤ ⟨φ⟩^+_{Λ₁}` after cancelling `⟨1_A⟩^+_{Λ₂} > 0`. |
| §6 / Issue #3565 | **`+` boundary expectation on a cubic box of ℤ^d** (PR 1) | **Done (definition + inner-region antitone)** | `Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/CubicBoxPlusState.lean`. First step toward the infinite-volume `+` Gibbs state on ℤ^d (Issue #3565). `plusBoxExpectation d n m J h β φ` = `gibbsExpectationBC` over the induced cubic-lattice graph on the ambient box `cubicBox d m`, with inner region `plusBoxInterior d n m` (= sites of `cubicBox d m` in `cubicBox d n`) and the annulus frozen to `plusConfig` — the `+` boundary condition on the inner box. `plusBoxInterior_subset` (`n₁ ≤ n₂ ⟹` inner regions nest, via `cubicBox_mono`). `plusBoxExpectation_antitone_interior`: within a fixed ambient box `m`, `n₁ ≤ n₂ ⟹ plusBoxExpectation d n₂ m … ≤ plusBoxExpectation d n₁ m …` for monotone `φ` — the cubic-box instance of FV Lemma 3.22 (`gibbsExpectationBC_plus_volume_antitone`). Lifting to a genuine thermodynamic limit (growing the ambient `m`) is the subject of the later PRs of #3565. |
| §6 / Issue #3565 | **Bounded BC observables + single-site `+` box spin** (PR 2a) | **Done (2 generic bounds + single-site def + range + antitone)** | `Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/CubicBoxPlusStateBounds.lean`. Generic reusable bounds for the boundary-condition Gibbs expectation: `gibbsExpectationBC_le_of_forall_le` / `gibbsExpectationBC_ge_of_forall_ge` (the expectation of a bounded observable is bounded by the pointwise bound — it is a nonnegative-weight average). `spin_sign_mem_Icc` (`Spin.sign ℝ s ∈ [-1,1]`). `plusBoxSpin d n m J h β x` — the single-site `+` box spin (the `+` boundary expectation of `s(σ_x)`), with `plusBoxSpin_mem_Icc` (`∈ [-1,1]`, the bounded-below input for the eventual monotone-convergence limit) and `plusBoxSpin_antitone_interior` (single-site cubic-box instance of FV Lemma 3.22). The remaining ingredient — the nearest-neighbour **screening** lemma (ambient-box independence) — is the next PR of #3565. |
| Issue #3565 | **Cubic-box adjacency geometry** (screening foundation) | **Done (3 geometric lemmas)** | `Concrete/CubicBoxAdjacencyGeometry.lean`. The geometric facts underlying the nearest-neighbour **screening** of the `+` box state. `latticeGraph_adj_abs_le_one` (adjacent sites differ by `≤ 1` in each coordinate). `cubicBox_adj_mem_succ` (a neighbour of a `cubicBox d n` site lies in `cubicBox d (n+1)` — the inner free region reaches only one box further out, so it is shielded from the outer shell). `cubicBox_shell_adj_not_mem_inner` (for `n+1 ≤ m`, a shell site `∈ cubicBox d (m+1) ∖ cubicBox d m` and any of its neighbours lie outside `cubicBox d n` — every shell-touching interaction is between frozen `+` spins). These feed the constant-factor cancellation in the screening proof (next PR of #3565). |
| Issue #3565 | **Induced-graph energy split over the extension graph** (screening energy core) | **Done (3 lemmas)** | `AmbientLattice/Monotonicity/InducedEnergySplit.lean`. The energy-level heart of the `+`-state screening, reusing the existing extension-graph factoring (`EdgeSiteSums.lean`). `interactionEnergy_inducedGraph_extendGraph_split`: `interactionEnergy (inducedGraph G Λ₂) = interactionEnergy (extendGraphFromΛ₁ G Λ₁ Λ₂) + (-J)·∑_{extra edges} edgeSpin` where the extra edges are `(inducedGraph G Λ₂).edgeFinset ∖ (extendGraphFromΛ₁ G Λ₁ Λ₂).edgeFinset` (via `edgeFinset_mono` + `Finset.sum_sdiff`). `hamiltonian_inducedGraph_extendGraph_split` (full Hamiltonian; field terms coincide). `hamiltonian_inducedGraph_restrict_add`: composing with `hamiltonian_extendGraph_factor` gives `hamiltonian (inducedGraph G Λ₂) σ = hamiltonian (inducedGraph G Λ₁)(restrictConfig σ) + complement-field + extra-edge interaction`. On the cubic box the extra edges all touch the frozen `+` shell (#3569), so the extra-edge interaction is a constant cancelling in the normalised expectation — the screening. |
| Issue #3565 | **Boltzmann-weight factoring of an induced graph** (screening weight core) | **Done (2 lemmas)** | `AmbientLattice/Monotonicity/InducedWeightFactor.lean`. The Boltzmann-weight counterpart of the energy split (#3570). `boltzmannWeight_inducedGraph_restrict_factor`: `boltzmannWeight (inducedGraph G Λ₂) p σ = boltzmannWeight (inducedGraph G Λ₁) p (restrictConfig σ) · exp(-β·(complement-field + extra-edge interaction))` (exponentiating `hamiltonian_inducedGraph_restrict_add`). `boltzmannWeight_inducedGraph_restrict_factor_const`: when the complement sites are all `up` and the extra edges all have `edgeSpin = 1` (the frozen `+` case), the exponential factor collapses to a **constant** depending only on the complement-site count and extra-edge count — the constant that cancels in the normalised `+` boundary-condition expectation (the screening). |
| Issue #3565 | **Cubic-box screening conditions** (BC↔uniform bridge + extra-edge geometry) | **Done (2 lemmas)** | `Inequalities/FKGInhomogeneous.lean` + `Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/CubicBoxScreening.lean`. The frozen-`+` ingredients turning `boltzmannWeight_inducedGraph_restrict_factor_const` (#3571) into the cubic-box screening. `boltzmannWeightJ_uniform_eq` (in `Inequalities/FKGInhomogeneous.lean`, beside the `boltzmannWeightJ` it specialises; relocated there by #4833): at a uniform coupling `fun _ => J`, `boltzmannWeightJ G β (fun _ => J) h = boltzmannWeight G ⟨J,h,β⟩` — bridges the boundary-condition framework (`boltzmannWeightBC = indicator · boltzmannWeightJ`) to the uniform-coupling factoring lemmas. `cubicBox_extra_edge_endpoints_not_mem_inner` (in `Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/CubicBoxScreening.lean`): for `n+1 ≤ m`, both endpoints of an extra edge (in `inducedGraph (latticeGraph d) (cubicBox d (m+1))` but not in `extendGraphFromΛ₁ … (cubicBox d m)`) lie outside `cubicBox d n` (Sym2 case analysis: not in the extension graph ⟹ an endpoint is in the shell, then `cubicBox_shell_adj_not_mem_inner` #3569 places both outside `cubicBox d n`). Hence for a `+`-off-`box n` configuration, every extra edge has `edgeSpin = 1` — the `hextra` input to the screening. |

| §6 / Issue #3565 | **★ Infinite-volume `+` expectation of a single spin on ℤ^d ★** | **Done (antitone + convergence)** | `Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/InfiniteVolumePlusState.lean`. **The culmination of the cubic-box `+`-state programme**: the infinite-volume `+` boundary expectation of a single spin exists as the monotone limit along the cubic exhaustion. `plusBoxSpin_infiniteVolume_antitone`: for a fixed `x ∈ cubicBox d N`, the screened single-site `+` box spins `k ↦ plusBoxSpin d (N+k) (N+k+1) … x` (free inner box `cubicBox d (N+k)`, immediate `+` boundary layer) are **antitone** (FV Lemma 3.22 `plusBoxSpin_antitone_interior` + the ambient screening `plusBoxSpin_screening_succ`). `tendsto_plusBoxSpin_infiniteVolume`: this antitone, `[-1,1]`-bounded sequence **converges** (`tendsto_atTop_ciInf`) to `⨅ k, plusBoxSpin d (N+k) (N+k+1) … x` — the infinite-volume `+` expectation of `σ_x`, a contribution to the project's central long-term goal (the thermodynamic limit / extremal `+` state). Extending the limit to all local observables (the full state functional) is follow-up. |
| §6 / Issue #3565 | **★ Infinite-volume `+` expectation of a monotone local observable on ℤ^d ★** | **Done (structure + convergence)** | `Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/LocalObservableLimit.lean`. Generalises the single-spin limit to an arbitrary **monotone local observable** — the standard `+`-state API. `restrictConfig_monotone` / `restrictConfig_trans` (the configuration restriction map is monotone and transitive). `LocalMonotoneObservable d` — a structure bundling a finite support `S : Finset (Fin d → ℤ)`, a function `φ : Config ↑S → ℝ`, and `Monotone φ`. `LocalMonotoneObservable.lift hS` (`φ ∘ restrictConfig hS`, with `lift_monotone`). `plusBoxLocalExpectation n m J h β O hS` — its `+` box expectation. `plusBoxLocalExpectation_screening_succ` (ambient-box independence: the lifted observable depends only on the inner config via `restrictConfig_trans`). `plusBoxLocalExpectation_ge` (bounded below by `inf' φ` over the finite support configs — the bounded-below input replacing `[-1,1]`). `plusBoxLocalExpectation_infiniteVolume_antitone` and `tendsto_plusBoxLocalObservable_infiniteVolume`: for `O.S ⊆ cubicBox d N`, the screened `+` local expectations converge (decreasingly, `tendsto_atTop_ciInf`) to `⨅ k, plusBoxLocalExpectation (N+k) (N+k+1) … O` — the infinite-volume `+` expectation of any monotone local observable. (Any local observable is a difference of monotone ones, so this is the building block of the full `+`-state functional.) |
| §3.4 / Issue #3565 | **★ The cubic-exhaustion `+`-state functional on ℤ^d (towards FV Thm 3.17) ★** | **Done (functional + same-support linearity + positivity + normalisation; sequence-independence / translation-invariance / `−` state / cross-support union are follow-up)** | `Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/LocalObservableState.lean`. Extends the cubic-screened `+` limit to **all** local observables (not just monotone), giving the cubic-exhaustion `+`-state functional. `LocalObservable d` — a real function `φ : Config ↑S → ℝ` on a finite support (no monotonicity). `configUpRank` / `configUpRank_mono` / `configUpRank_lt_of_lt` — the up-spin count, (strictly) monotone. The **monotone-difference decomposition** (the elementary role of FV Lemma 3.19): `LocalObservable.upper` (`K·rank + φ`) and `lower` (`K·rank`), `K = 2·max|φ|`, both monotone (the large `K·rank` dominates `φ`'s variation across any covering step), with `phi_eq_upper_sub_lower` (`φ = upper − lower`). `plusBoxObsExpectation` — the `+` box expectation of an arbitrary observable, with `plusBoxObsExpectation_{eq_sub,add,const_mul,const,mono}` (linearity from `gibbsExpectationBC_add`/`const_mul`/`const`). `plusStateExpectation` — the infinite-volume `+`-state functional (the difference of the two monotone limits), with `tendsto_plusStateExpectation` (the screened box expectations converge to it). The functional is **linear** (`plusStateExpectation_add`, `_const_mul`, *same support*), **normalised** (`plusStateExpectation_const` = `c`), **positive** (`plusStateExpectation_nonneg`), **monotone** (`plusStateExpectation_mono`), and **consistent** with the monotone-observable limit (`plusStateExpectation_of_monotone` = `⨅`). All via `tendsto_nhds_unique` / `le_of_tendsto`. (Sequence independence, translation invariance, the `−` state, and cross-support union linearity are follow-up before the full FV Thm 3.17 `+` state.) |
| §3.4 / Issue #3565 | **★ The `−` state and `±` extremal ordering on ℤ^d (FV Lemma 3.23) ★** | **Done (flip bridge + cubic-exhaustion `−` functional + `μ⁻ ≤ μ⁺`)** | `Inequalities/BoundaryFlip.lean` + `Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/MinusStateExtremal.lean`. The global spin-flip `σ ↦ σ.flip` maps the `−` boundary state to the `+` state with `h ↦ −h`. **Flip bridge** (general inhomogeneous `J`, any `h`): `interactionEnergyJ_flip` / `hamiltonianJ_neg_h_flip` / `boltzmannWeightJ_neg_h_flip` (the Hamiltonian/weight under `h ↦ −h` + flip), `agreesOff_minusConfig_flip_iff` (`σ` agrees with `−` off `Λ` iff `σ.flip` agrees with `+`), `boltzmannWeightBC_minus_eq_plus_neg_h_flip`, `partitionFunctionBC_minus_eq_plus_neg_h_flip`, and the headline `gibbsExpectationBC_minus_eq_plus_neg_h_flip` (`⟨F⟩^−_Λ(h) = ⟨F ∘ flip⟩^+_Λ(−h)`, via the `flipEquiv` reindex). **`−` state**: `LocalObservable.flipObs` (`σ ↦ φ(σ.flip)`), `minusStateExpectation J h β O := plusStateExpectation J (−h) β O.flipObs` with `tendsto_minusStateExpectation` and the transferred `minusStateExpectation_{add,const_mul,const,nonneg}`. **Extremal ordering**: `plusBoxObsExpectation_flipObs_neg_h_le` (finite `−` ≤ `+` from the flip bridge + `gibbsExpectationBC_minus_le`) and `minusStateExpectation_le_plusStateExpectation` (`μ⁻(φ) ≤ μ⁺(φ)` for monotone `φ`, `β, J ≥ 0`). |
| §3.4 / Issue #3565 | **Cross-support union linearity of the `±`-state functional on ℤ^d** | **Done (extend invariance + union add)** | `Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/LocalObservableUnion.lean`. Extends the *same-support* linearity (`plusStateExpectation_add`) to observables on **different** supports. `LocalObservable.extend hSS'` — enlarge the support `S ⊆ S'` (the observable ignores the new coordinates, `φ ∘ restrictConfig`). `plusBoxObsExpectation_extend_eq` (the box expectation is unchanged, `restrictConfig_trans`, `rfl`); `plusStateExpectation_extend_eq` / `minusStateExpectation_extend_eq` (the `±`-state functional is unchanged, via `tendsto_nhds_unique`). Headline `plusStateExpectation_union_add` / `minusStateExpectation_union_add`: for `φ₁` on `S₁` and `φ₂` on `S₂` both lifted to `S₁ ∪ S₂`, `μ^±(φ₁ + φ₂) = μ^±(φ₁) + μ^±(φ₂)` (lift both via extension to the common support `S₁ ∪ S₂ ⊆ cubicBox d N`, then same-support additivity). |
| §3.4 / Issue #3581 | **Boundary-condition translation covariance (towards `±`-state translation invariance)** | **Done (PR 1: BC covariance + plusConfig covariance)** | `Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/PlusStateTranslation.lean`. The boundary-condition analogue of `correlationΛ_vaddFinset_eq`, the foundation for translation invariance of the cubic-exhaustion `±`-state functional (Issue #3581). `plusConfig_configVaddEquiv` (the all-`+` configuration is translation-invariant). `agreesOff_map_configVaddEquiv_iff` (boundary agreement transports under translation). `boltzmannWeightBC_vaddFinset_eq` / `partitionFunctionBC_vaddFinset_eq` / `gibbsExpectationBC_vaddFinset_eq`: for a `IsTranslationInvariant` graph, uniform coupling, and constant field, `⟨F ∘ τ⁻¹⟩^{τη}_{τΛ} = ⟨F⟩^η_Λ` where `τ = configVaddEquiv t Ω` (uniform Hamiltonian invariance `hamiltonian_configVaddEquiv_symm` + `configVaddEquiv` reindexing of the conditioned sum). |
| §3.4 / Issue #3581 | **Cubic-box interlacing geometry + `+` boundary covariance (towards `±`-state translation invariance)** | **Done (PR 2: interlacing + plus covariance)** | `Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/CubicBoxInterlacing.lean`. The translation toolkit for the `±`-state squeeze. `latticeRadius a = max_i |a i|` (`latticeRadius_zero`, `latticeRadius_neg`, `abs_coord_le_latticeRadius`). The centered cubic boxes interlace with their translates up to the index shift `R = latticeRadius a`: `vaddFinset_cubicBox_subset` (`a +ᵥ cubicBox d n ⊆ cubicBox d (n + R)`) and `cubicBox_subset_vaddFinset` (`cubicBox d n ⊆ a +ᵥ cubicBox d (n + R)`). `gibbsExpectationBC_plus_vaddFinset_eq`: the `plusConfig` specialisation of `gibbsExpectationBC_vaddFinset_eq` (#3582) — the `+` boundary expectation on the translated volume equals that on the original at the pulled-back observable (the all-`+` boundary is translation-invariant). Next (#3581): generalized-ambient screening + the interlacing squeeze for the limit. Reference: Friedli–Velenik (Cambridge, 2017), §3.4 Theorem 3.17 (statement p. 95, proof pp. 102–103). |
| §3.4 / Issue #3581 | **General-ambient `+` boundary screening (towards `±`-state translation invariance)** | **Done (PR 3: general ambient independence)** | `AmbientLattice/Monotonicity/PlusScreening.lean`. The cubic-box `+` screening (`gibbsExpectationBC_cubicBox_succ`) generalised to an arbitrary ambient pair `Λ₁ ⊆ Λ₂` with an inner region `I : Finset ↑Λ₁` whose extra-edge shell is separated from `I` (hypothesis `hsep`). `subtypeInclEmb` (the inclusion `↑Λ₁ ↪ ↑Λ₂`). `agreesOff_plus_configEquivSubtypeProd_iff` (boundary agreement splits along the config split). `edgeSpin_eq_one_of_agreesOff_extra_general` (frozen extra-edge spin). `plusShellConst` (the shell constant) with `plusShellConst_pos`. `boltzmannWeightBC_extendGraph_pointwise` / `bcSum_extendGraph_factor` / `partitionFunctionBC_extendGraph_factor` (the per-config / sum / partition-function factoring). Headline `gibbsExpectationBC_extendGraph_screening`: for an observable depending only on the inner configuration, the `+` boundary expectation is the same on `Λ₂` as on `Λ₁` (the shell is frozen `+` and cancels). This is the ambient-independence input for the translation-invariance squeeze (the translated cubic box is not cubic, so the cubic screening does not apply directly). Reference: Friedli–Velenik (Cambridge, 2017), §3.4 Theorem 3.17, pp. 100–104. |
| §3.4 / Issue #3581 | **Translated observable + inner-region interlacing antitone sandwich (towards `±`-state translation invariance)** | **Done (PR 4: squeeze ingredients)** | `Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/PlusStateTranslationInvariance.lean`. The concrete ingredients of the translation squeeze. `LocalMonotoneObservable.vadd a O` (the lattice translation of a monotone observable, via `configVaddEquiv`; monotonicity preserved) with `vadd_support_subset` (`(O.vadd a).S ⊆ cubicBox d (N + latticeRadius a)`). `translatedInner a n M` (the sites of the common ambient `cubicBox d M` with value in `a +ᵥ cubicBox d n`). The inner-region interlacing `translatedInner_subset_plusBoxInterior` (`translatedInner a n M ⊆ plusBoxInterior d (n+R) M`) and `plusBoxInterior_subset_translatedInner` (`plusBoxInterior d k M ⊆ translatedInner a (k+R) M`). The **antitone sandwich** `gibbsExpectationBC_translatedInner_sandwich`: for a monotone observable and `latticeRadius a ≤ n`, `E⁺_{n+R} ≤ E⁺_{a+ᵥn} ≤ E⁺_{n−R}` on the common ambient (via `gibbsExpectationBC_plus_volume_antitone`). Next (#3581): recentering the middle term via the covariance + screening, then the limit squeeze → translation invariance `μ⁺(τ_a φ) = μ⁺(φ)`. Reference: Friedli–Velenik (Cambridge, 2017), §3.4 Theorem 3.17, pp. 100–104. |
| §3.4 / Issue #3581 | **Recentering covariance for the translation squeeze (towards `±`-state translation invariance)** | **Done (PR 4b: recentering)** | `Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/PlusStateTranslationCapstone.lean`. The recentering step of the translation squeeze. `innerOf Ω I` (the inner region of ambient `Ω` given by a site set `I`); `translatedInner_eq_innerOf`. `translatedInner_map_vaddSubtypeEquiv` (the translated inner region pulls back to the centered inner region on the translated ambient `vaddFinset (-a) (cubicBox d M)`). `vadd_lift_comp_configVaddEquiv_symm` (the translated observable's lift composed with the inverse translation equals `O`'s lift on the translated ambient). Headline `gibbsExpectationBC_translatedInner_recenter`: the `+` expectation of `O.vadd a` with the translated inner region on the centered cubic ambient equals the `+` expectation of `O` with the centered inner region on the translated ambient — via the boundary-condition covariance applied with `t = −a` (the cubic box stays as `Ω`, so no ambient-equality transport is needed). Next (#3581): the general screening connecting the translated ambient back to the cubic exhaustion + the limit squeeze → `μ⁺(τ_a φ) = μ⁺(φ)`. Reference: Friedli–Velenik (Cambridge, 2017), §3.4 Theorem 3.17, pp. 100–104. |
| §3.4 / Issue #3581 | **Screening the translated ambient back to the cubic exhaustion (towards `±`-state translation invariance)** | **Done (PR 4c: middle term = cubic local expectation)** | `Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/PlusStateTranslationFinal.lean`. Brings the recentered sandwich middle term back onto the cubic exhaustion via the general-ambient screening (#3584). `innerOf_eq_plusBoxInterior_map` (the centered inner region is the lifted cubic inner region). `hsep_translated` (the shell-separation hypothesis: a `cubicBox d n` site's neighbours lie in `cubicBox d (n+1)`, via `cubicBox_adj_mem_succ`). `cubicBox_succ_subset_vaddFinset_neg` (the geometric inclusion). `gibbsExpectationBC_innerOf_translated_eq_plusBoxLocal` (the screening identity: `+` expectation of `O` with centered inner region on the translated ambient = cubic `plusBoxLocalExpectation`). Headline `gibbsExpectationBC_translatedInner_vadd_eq_plusBoxLocal`: composing the recentering covariance (#3586) with the screening, the translated sandwich middle term `E⁺_{translatedInner, cubicBox d (n+1+R)}((O.vadd a).lift)` equals `plusBoxLocalExpectation n (n+1) O`. Next (#3581): the bounds screening + the limit squeeze → `μ⁺(τ_a φ) = μ⁺(φ)`. Reference: Friedli–Velenik (Cambridge, 2017), §3.4 Theorem 3.17, pp. 100–104. |
| §3.4 / Issue #3581 | **★ Translation invariance of the cubic-exhaustion `+`-state functional on ℤ^d (FV Thm 3.17) ★** | **Done (monotone observable)** | `Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/PlusStateTranslationHeadline.lean`. **The culmination of the translation-invariance arc**: the cubic-exhaustion `+`-state functional is invariant under lattice translations on a monotone observable, `μ⁺(τ_a φ) = μ⁺(φ)`. `plusBoxLocalExpectation_screening_eq` (multi-step ambient independence of the `+` local expectation, iterating `plusBoxLocalExpectation_screening_succ`). `plusBoxLocal_vadd_squeeze`: the per-stage squeeze — for `R = latticeRadius a` and `n = N+2R+k`, `E⁺_{n+R}(O.vadd a) ≤ E⁺_n(O) ≤ E⁺_{n-R}(O.vadd a)` (the interlacing antitone sandwich #3585 with the recentered middle term #3586/#3587, the bounds at their natural ambient or reduced by the screening). `plusBoxLocalExpectation_congr` (index congruence). Headline `plusStateExpectation_vadd_monotone`: the translated `+` local expectations are bracketed between two shifted subsequences of the original (`plusBoxLocal_vadd_squeeze`), all converging to the same `+`-state limit (`tendsto_plusBoxLocalObservable_infiniteVolume`, `Tendsto.comp`), so by `le_of_tendsto_of_tendsto'` and `le_antisymm`, `μ⁺(τ_a φ) = μ⁺(φ)`. Reference: Friedli–Velenik (Cambridge, 2017), §3.4 Theorem 3.17 (statement p. 95, proof pp. 102–103). |
| §3.4 / Issue #3581 | **Translation-invariance infrastructure for general local observables on ℤ^d** | **Done (compatibility lemmas)** | `Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/PlusStateTranslationGeneral.lean`. The infrastructure to extend translation invariance from monotone observables to **all** local observables (via the monotone-difference decomposition). `configUpRank_configVaddEquiv_symm` (the up-spin count is translation-invariant). `LocalObservable.vadd` (the lattice translation of an arbitrary local observable) with `monoBound_vadd` (the monotone bound is translation-invariant). `vadd_upper_phi_eq` / `vadd_lower_phi_eq` (the upper/lower monotone parts commute with translation: `(O.vadd a).upper.φ = (O.upper.vadd a).φ`). `plusStateExpectation_congr_phi` (the `+`-state respects observable-function equality). These reduce the general-observable translation invariance to the monotone case `plusStateExpectation_vadd_monotone` (#3588). Reference: Friedli–Velenik (Cambridge, 2017), §3.4 Theorem 3.17 (statement p. 95, proof pp. 102–103). |
| §3.4 / Issue #3581 | **★ Translation invariance of the `+`-state functional on all local observables on ℤ^d (FV Thm 3.17) ★** | **Done (all local observables)** | `Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/PlusStateTranslationGeneralHeadline.lean`. The final assembly: `μ⁺(τ_a φ) = μ⁺(φ)` for **any** local observable. `plusStateExpectation_eq_upper_sub_lower` (the `+`-state as a difference of its upper/lower monotone `+`-states, via `plusStateExpectation_of_monotone`). Headline `plusStateExpectation_vadd`: reduces the general case to the monotone case `plusStateExpectation_vadd_monotone` (#3588) through the monotone-difference decomposition — translation commutes with the upper/lower parts (`vadd_upper_phi_eq` / `vadd_lower_phi_eq`, #3589), so `μ⁺(τ_a φ) = μ⁺(φ_upper) − μ⁺(φ_lower) = μ⁺(τ_a φ_upper) − μ⁺(τ_a φ_lower) = μ⁺(φ)`. Reference: Friedli–Velenik (Cambridge, 2017), §3.4 Theorem 3.17 (statement p. 95, proof pp. 102–103). |
| §3.4 / Issue #3581 | **Translation invariance of the `−`-state functional on ℤ^d (FV Thm 3.17)** | **Done** | `Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/MinusStateTranslation.lean`. `μ⁻(τ_a φ) = μ⁻(φ)` for any local observable, from the `+`-state invariance (#3590) via the spin-flip bridge: `minusStateExpectation J h β O = plusStateExpectation J (−h) β O.flipObs`, and translation commutes with the flip (`flipObs_vadd_phi_eq` — `configVaddEquiv` commutes with `Config.flip`, both coordinatewise). Headline `minusStateExpectation_vadd`. Reference: Friedli–Velenik (Cambridge, 2017), §3.4 Theorem 3.17 (statement p. 95, proof pp. 102–103). |
| §3.4 / Issue #3581 | **General shell separation + ambient independence (towards exhaustion independence)** | **Done (foundation)** | `Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/PlusStateExhaustion.lean`. Towards exhaustion independence of the cubic-exhaustion `+`-state functional. `hsep_of_neighbors_subset` — the general shell-separation criterion (if every inner-region site has all its lattice neighbours in `Λ₁`, every extra edge has both endpoints outside the inner region), generalising the cubic-specific `hsep_translated` (#3587). `gibbsExpectationBC_screening_of_neighbors` — ambient independence for a general inner region: for an observable depending only on the inner configuration whose inner region's neighbours lie in `Λ₁ ⊆ Λ₂`, the `+` boundary expectation on `Λ₂` equals that on `Λ₁`. Next: the directed inf over all finite regions + cofinality (cubic and any exhaustion both cofinal) → exhaustion independence. Reference: Friedli–Velenik (Cambridge, 2017), §3.4 Theorem 3.17 (statement p. 95, proof pp. 102–103). |
| §3.4 / Issue #3581 | **Ambient independence of the cubic `+` local expectation** | **Done** | `Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/PlusStateAmbientCubic.lean`. A step towards exhaustion independence: `plusBoxLocalExpectation_eq_on_ambient` — for any ambient `Ω ⊇ cubicBox d (n+1)`, the `+` boundary expectation of `O` with the cubic inner region `cubicBox d n` on `Ω` equals the natural cubic `plusBoxLocalExpectation n (n+1) O` (via `gibbsExpectationBC_screening_of_neighbors` #3592, since a `cubicBox d n` site's neighbours lie in `cubicBox d (n+1)`). Reference: Friedli–Velenik (Cambridge, 2017), §3.4 Theorem 3.17 (statement p. 95, proof pp. 102–103). |
| §3.4 / Issue #3581 | **General two-ambient independence of the `+` boundary expectation** | **Done** | `Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/PlusStateAmbientIndep.lean`. The compatibility lemma making the `+` state ambient-free: `gibbsExpectationBC_ambient_indep` — for a free region `I` whose lattice neighbours lie in `Λ₁`, and an observable `O` supported in `Λ₁`, the `+` boundary expectation of `O` (inner region `I`) on `Ω₁` equals that on `Ω₂` for **any** two ambients `Ω₁, Ω₂ ⊇ Λ₁` (both reduce to the value on `Λ₁` by two applications of `gibbsExpectationBC_screening_of_neighbors` #3592). This is the key compatibility for the directed-infimum formulation: different finite free regions can be compared by routing each through its own ambient. Reference: Friedli–Velenik (Cambridge, 2017), §3.4 Theorem 3.17 (statement p. 95, proof pp. 102–103). |
| §3.4 / Issue #3581 | **Region `+` expectation `μ⁺_A` and its nearest-neighbour closure** | **Done** | `Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/PlusRegionExpectation.lean`. Models the FV finite-volume `+` measure `μ⁺_A` (Ising in `A` with the configuration outside `A` frozen to `+1`) for any finite region `A ⊆ ℤ^d`, on the way to exhaustion independence. `nnClosure d A = A ∪ ∂A` — the nearest-neighbour closure (`subset_nnClosure`, `nnClosure_neighbors_subset`, `nnClosure_mono`, `nnClosure_cubicBox_subset_succ`). `regionLift d A` — `A` viewed inside `↑(nnClosure d A)`. `plusRegionExpectation A J h β O hSA` — the region `+` expectation (`gibbsExpectationBC` on `nnClosure d A`, free region `A`, boundary layer frozen `+`). `plusRegionExpectation_eq_on_ambient` — ambient independence (realised on any `Ω ⊇ nnClosure d A`, via `gibbsExpectationBC_screening_of_neighbors` #3592). `plusRegionExpectation_antitone` — antitone in the region (FV Lemma 3.22, growing `A` decreases the monotone expectation, compared on the common ambient `nnClosure d A'`). `plusRegionExpectation_cubicBox_eq` — agrees with the cubic `plusBoxLocalExpectation n (n+1)` on `A = cubicBox d n`, connecting the region functional to the cubic-exhaustion `+`-state. Reference: Friedli–Velenik (Cambridge, 2017), §3.4 Theorem 3.17 (statement p. 95, proof pp. 102–103). |
| §3.4 / Issue #3581 | **Cubic-radius convergence of the region `+` expectation** | **Done** | `Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/PlusRegionCubicConvergence.lean`. The region `+` expectation `plusRegionExpectation (cubicBox d r)` of a monotone observable converges, as the radius `r → ∞`, to the cubic-exhaustion `+`-state `plusStateExpectation` — the radius-reindexed form of the monotone-convergence limit, packaged as a **total** sequence ready to feed the general-exhaustion squeeze. `plusRegionExpectation_cubicBox_antitone` — antitone in the cubic radius (FV Lemma 3.22). `regionCubicValue` / `regionCubicValue_eq` — the total cubic-radius sequence `r ↦ plusRegionExpectation (cubicBox d (max N r))` (the `max N r` keeps the support hypothesis available for every `r`). `plusRegionExpectation_cubicBox_index_congr` — equal-radius congruence (proof-irrelevant supports). `tendsto_plusRegionExpectation_cubicBox` — `N+k`-indexed convergence (via `plusRegionExpectation_cubicBox_eq` + `tendsto_plusBoxLocalObservable_infiniteVolume`). `tendsto_regionCubicValue` — radius-reindexed convergence (reindex `r ↦ r-N`, `tendsto_sub_atTop_nat`). Reference: Friedli–Velenik (Cambridge, 2017), §3.4 Theorem 3.17 (statement p. 95, proof pp. 102–103). |
| §3.4 / Issue #3581 | **★ Exhaustion independence of the cubic-exhaustion `+`-state functional on ℤ^d (FV Thm 3.17 headline) ★** | **Done (monotone observable, `d ≥ 1`)** | `Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/PlusRegionExhaustion.lean`. **The headline of the exhaustion-independence programme**: for **any** exhaustion `Λ` of `ℤ^d` (`d ≥ 1`) and a monotone local observable `O`, the region `+` expectations `plusRegionExpectation (Λ.volume (N₀+k))` converge to the cubic-exhaustion `+`-state `plusStateExpectation` as `k → ∞` — the finite-volume `+` measures `μ⁺_{Λ_k}` converge to the **same** infinite-volume `+` state regardless of the exhaustion. `cubicBox_not_subset_of_lt` / `cubicBox_subset_iff_le` — strict monotonicity of cubic boxes (`d ≥ 1`, via a `Pi.single` separating vertex). `exhaustionOuterRadius` / `_spec` / `_tendsto` — the smallest enclosing cubic radius (`→ ∞`). `exhaustionInnerRadius` / `_spec` / `_tendsto` — the largest enclosed cubic radius (`Nat.findGreatest`, `→ ∞`). Headline `tendsto_plusRegionExpectation_exhaustion`: the **squeeze** `cubicBox d (innerRadius) ⊆ Λ.volume ⊆ cubicBox d (outerRadius)` with both radii `→ ∞`; region antitonicity (FV Lemma 3.22) turns the inclusions into inequalities and both cubic bounds converge to `plusStateExpectation` (`tendsto_regionCubicValue`), so `tendsto_of_tendsto_of_tendsto_of_le_of_le'` closes it. Reference: Friedli–Velenik (Cambridge, 2017), §3.4 Theorem 3.17 (statement p. 95, proof pp. 102–103). |
| §3.4 / Issue #3581 | **★ Exhaustion independence for general observables and the `−` state (FV Thm 3.17 complete, `d ≥ 1`) ★** | **Done** | `Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/PlusRegionObsExhaustion.lean`. Extends the monotone-observable exhaustion independence (`tendsto_plusRegionExpectation_exhaustion`) to **all** local observables and the `−` state, completing FV Theorem 3.17 (`d ≥ 1`) for both cubic-exhaustion `±`-state functionals on an arbitrary exhaustion. `plusRegionObsExpectation` / `plusRegionObsExpectation_eq_sub` — the region `+` expectation of a general observable via the monotone-difference decomposition `φ = upper − lower`. `tendsto_plusRegionObsExpectation_exhaustion` — for any exhaustion `Λ` and any local observable `O`, the region `+` expectations converge to `plusStateExpectation` (difference of the two monotone headlines, via `plusStateExpectation_eq_upper_sub_lower`). `minusRegionObsExpectation` / `tendsto_minusRegionObsExpectation_exhaustion` — the `−`-state analogue, the `(−h, flipObs)` specialisation (the global spin-flip bridge `minusStateExpectation = plusStateExpectation` at `(−h, flipObs)`). Reference: Friedli–Velenik (Cambridge, 2017), §3.4 Theorem 3.17 (statement p. 95, proof pp. 102–103). |
| §3.4–§3.6 / Issue #3599 | **Infinite-volume magnetization `m^±(β,h)` (order parameter)** | **Done (definition + bounds + ordering + convergence)** | `Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/InfiniteVolumeMagnetization.lean`. The order parameter `m^±(β,h) = μ^±(σ_x)` — the single-spin expectation in the cubic-exhaustion `±`-state. `singleSpinMonoObs x` — the monotone single-spin observable (support `{x}`, `φ = s(σ_x)`, via `singleSpinObs_monotone`); `singleSpinMonoObs_support_subset` (`{x} ⊆ cubicBox d (latticeRadius x)`, `mem_cubicBox_latticeRadius`); `singleSpinMonoObs_phi_le_one` / `neg_one_le_singleSpinMonoObs_phi`. `plusMagnetization` / `minusMagnetization` — `m^±`. `tendsto_plusMagnetization` / `tendsto_minusMagnetization` — the finite-volume `±` box magnetizations converge to `m^±`. `plusMagnetization_le_one` / `neg_one_le_plusMagnetization` / `minusMagnetization_le_one` / `neg_one_le_minusMagnetization` — the `m^± ∈ [-1,1]` bounds (`plusStateExpectation_mono` + `_const`; the `−` bounds via the flip form). `minusMagnetization_le_plusMagnetization` — the extremal ordering `m⁻ ≤ m⁺` (FV Lemma 3.23 / Theorem 3.17, from `minusStateExpectation_le_plusStateExpectation`). Reference: Friedli–Velenik (Cambridge, 2017), §3.4 (Theorem 3.17), §3.6 (magnetization, phase transition). |
| §3.6 / Issue #3599 | **Spin-flip symmetry of the magnetization `m⁻(β,h) = −m⁺(β,−h)`** | **Done** | `Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/MagnetizationFlipSymmetry.lean`. The global spin-flip (`h ↦ −h`) exchanges the `±` states and negates the single spin. `singleSpinMonoObs_phi_flip` — the single spin negates under the flip (`Spin.sign_flip`). `minusMagnetization_eq_neg_plusMagnetization_neg_h` / `plusMagnetization_eq_neg_minusMagnetization_neg_h` — `m⁻(β,h) = −m⁺(β,−h)` (and reverse), via the `−`-state-as-flipped-`+`-state definition + `plusStateExpectation_congr_phi` + `plusStateExpectation_const_mul`. `minusMagnetization_zero_eq_neg_plusMagnetization_zero` / `plusMagnetization_zero_eq_neg_minusMagnetization_zero` — the `h = 0` antisymmetry `m⁻(β,0) = −m⁺(β,0)`. Reference: Friedli–Velenik (Cambridge, 2017), §3.6 (the `±` magnetizations and spin-flip symmetry). |
| §3.6 / Issue #3599 | **Field monotonicity of the magnetization `m^±(β,·)`** | **Done** | `Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/MagnetizationFieldMonotone.lean`. Increasing the field increases the magnetization (Holley's inequality lifted to the boundary-condition Gibbs expectation, then to the infinite-volume magnetization). The generic boundary-condition ingredients (`boltzmannWeightBC_field_cross_supermodular`, `gibbsExpectationBC_field_mono_of_nonneg`, `gibbsExpectationBC_field_mono`) live in `Inequalities/MonotonicityField.lean` (relocated by Issue #4857); this file supplies the lattice half. `plusMagnetization_mono_h` — `m⁺(β,·)` is nondecreasing in the field (each finite-volume `+` box magnetization is monotone in `h`, and `m⁺` is the limit, `le_of_tendsto_of_tendsto'`). `minusMagnetization_mono_h` — `m⁻(β,·)` is nondecreasing (via the flip symmetry `m⁻(β,h) = −m⁺(β,−h)`). Reference: Friedli–Velenik (Cambridge, 2017), §3.6 (magnetization, monotonicity in the field; Holley's inequality, Theorem 3.50). |
| §3.6 / Magnetization | **Spontaneous magnetization `m*(β) = m⁺(β,0)` (order parameter, `+`-state route)** | **Done** | `Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/SpontaneousMagnetization.lean`. The zero-field `+`-state magnetization at the origin, the order parameter of the phase transition (the `+`-state-route spontaneous magnetization, distinct from the existing GKS-route `Ambient.spontaneousMagnetization`). `plusStateSpontaneousMagnetization` — `m*(β) = m⁺(β,0)`. `plusStateSpontaneousMagnetization_nonneg` — `0 ≤ m*(β)` (the key non-negativity: from the zero-field antisymmetry `m⁻(β,0) = −m⁺(β,0)` and `m⁻ ≤ m⁺`, so `−m⁺(β,0) ≤ m⁺(β,0)`). `plusStateSpontaneousMagnetization_le_one` — `m*(β) ≤ 1`. `minusStateSpontaneousMagnetization` / `_eq_neg` / `_nonpos` / `_le_plusStateSpontaneousMagnetization` — the `−` order parameter `= −m*(β) ≤ 0 ≤ m*(β)`. Reference: Friedli–Velenik (Cambridge, 2017), §3.6 (spontaneous magnetization, the order parameter). |
| §3.6 / Magnetization | **Sign of the magnetization** | **Done** | `Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/MagnetizationSign.lean`. The sign of the magnetization from field monotonicity + zero-field non-negativity. `plusMagnetization_zero_field_nonneg` — `0 ≤ m⁺(β,0)` at any site (general-site version of the spontaneous non-negativity). `plusMagnetization_nonneg_of_field_nonneg` — `0 ≤ h ⟹ 0 ≤ m⁺(β,h)` (dominates its non-negative zero-field value). `minusMagnetization_nonpos_of_field_nonpos` — `h ≤ 0 ⟹ m⁻(β,h) ≤ 0` (spin-flip symmetry). `minusMagnetization_zero_field_nonpos` — `m⁻(β,0) ≤ 0`. Reference: Friedli–Velenik (Cambridge, 2017), §3.6 (magnetization, sign and the order parameter). |
| §3.6 / Thm 3.49 / Issue #3605 | **GKS-I for the `+` boundary-condition state** | **Done** | `Inequalities/GKSBoundaryCondition.lean`. The first Griffiths inequality extended from the free Gibbs state to the `+` boundary state — the foundation for the `β`/`J`-monotonicity of the `+` magnetization. `boltzmannWeightBC_plus_eq_prod` — the `+` boundary weight is the free weight times a product of pinning factors `w⁺_Λ(σ) = w(σ)·∏_{i∈Λᶜ}(½+½·σ_i)` (each `1` if `σ_i=+1`, `0` if `σ_i=−1`, realising the boundary indicator). `boltzmannWeightBC_plus_hasNonnegCorrelations` — the `+` weight has non-negative correlations (each factor is `(a+b·σ^C)` with `a,b≥0`, so `hasNonnegCorrelations_mul_prod` applies). `gibbsExpectationBC_plus_spinProduct_nonneg` — GKS-I: `⟨σ^A⟩⁺_Λ ≥ 0`. Reference: Friedli–Velenik (Cambridge, 2017), §3.6, Theorem 3.49 (GKS-I, pp. 127–128). |
| §3.6 / Thm 3.49 / Issue #3605 | **GKS-II for the `+` boundary-condition state** | **Done** | `Inequalities/GKSBoundaryConditionII.lean`. The second Griffiths inequality for the `+` boundary state, `⟨σ^A⟩⁺_Λ · ⟨σ^B⟩⁺_Λ ≤ ⟨σ^{AΔB}⟩⁺_Λ` (`gibbsExpectationBC_plus_gks_second`), by the duplicate-variable argument applied to the pinned weight `w⁺ = w·pin`. The change of variables `ω' = φ_t ω` (`(φt)_i = ω_i·t_i`) **collapses the pinning** `pin_Λ(ω)·pin_Λ(φt) = pin_Λ(ω)·pin_Λ(t)` (`bcPin_duplicate_eq`, from `(½+½a)(½+½ab) = (½+½a)(½+½b)` since `a²=1`), so the doubled `+` weight is `w⁺(ω)·w⁺(φt) = bcModExp(t,ω)·pin_Λ(ω)·pin_Λ(t)` (`boltzmannWeightBC_plus_duplicate_eq`), which has non-negative correlations in `ω` (`bcModifiedWeight_nonneg_corr`: exp-product HNC × `pin_Λ(ω)` product × non-negative constant `pin_Λ(t)`). Mirrors the free-state `gks_second` and scaled-state `scaledCorrelation_gks_second`. Reference: Friedli–Velenik (Cambridge, 2017), §3.6, Theorem 3.49 (GKS-II, pp. 127–128). |
| §3.6 / Prop 4.2.1 / Issue #3605 | **Coupling monotonicity of the `+` boundary correlation** | **Done** | `Inequalities/MonotonicityJBoundaryCondition.lean`. `⟨σ^B⟩⁺_Λ` is monotone increasing in the uniform coupling `J` (boundary analogue of `correlation_monotone_J`). `cov_hnc_boltzmannBC_nonneg` — the `+`-state covariance bound for an HNC factor `f` (Walsh/Fourier expansion of `f` + the boundary GKS-II `gibbsExpectationBC_plus_gks_second`, #3607; mirror of `cov_hnc_boltzmann_nonneg`). `boltzmannWeightBC_plus_reweight` — the reweighting identity `w⁺_{J₂} = R·w⁺_{J₁}`, `R = exp(β(J₂−J₁)·Σ edgeSpin)` (via the indicator + `boltzmannWeightJ_uniform_eq`). `gibbsExpectationBC_plus_reweighting_nonneg` — the `J`-reweighting inequality (`R` is HNC). `gibbsExpectationBC_plus_monotone_J` — `MonotoneOn (J ↦ ⟨σ^B⟩⁺) (Ici 0)`. Reference: Friedli–Velenik (Cambridge, 2017), §3.6; Glimm–Jaffe Prop. 4.2.1. |
| §3.6 / Issue #3605 | **β-monotonicity of the `+` boundary single-spin correlation** | **Done** | `Inequalities/MonotonicityBetaBoundaryCondition.lean`. `⟨σ_x⟩⁺_Λ` is monotone increasing in the inverse temperature `β`. `boltzmannWeightBC_beta_rescale` / `gibbsExpectationBC_beta_rescale` — the rescaling `⟨F⟩⁺_{(J,h,β)} = ⟨F⟩⁺_{(βJ,βh,1)}` (β factors into the couplings; the boundary indicator is unchanged). `spinProduct_singleton_monotone` — the single spin is monotone. `gibbsExpectationBC_plus_monotone_beta_singleton` — `⟨σ_x⟩⁺_Λ` nondecreasing in `β` (for `J,h ≥ 0`), by reducing through the rescaling to the `J`-monotonicity (`gibbsExpectationBC_plus_monotone_J`, #3608) and `h`-monotonicity (`gibbsExpectationBC_field_mono`, #3602): increasing `β` moves `(β₁J,β₁h)` to `(β₂J,β₂h)` with both components non-decreasing. Reference: Friedli–Velenik (Cambridge, 2017), §3.6; Glimm–Jaffe Prop. 4.2.1. |
| §3.6 / Issue #3605 | **★ β-monotonicity of the infinite-volume magnetization `m⁺(β,h)` and `m*(β)` ★** | **Done** | `Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/MagnetizationBetaMonotone.lean`. The order parameter grows with the inverse temperature. `plusBoxObsExpectation_singleSpin_eq` — the finite-volume bridge: the `+` box expectation of the single spin equals the `+` boundary spin correlation `⟨σ_{⟨x⟩}⟩` on the cubic ambient (`Spin.sign = spinProduct {x}`, `restrictConfig`). `plusBoxObsExpectation_singleSpin_mono_beta` — its β-monotonicity (via the bridge + `gibbsExpectationBC_plus_monotone_beta_singleton`, #3609). `plusMagnetization_mono_beta` — `m⁺(β,h)` nondecreasing in `β` (`J,h ≥ 0`, `0 < β₁ ≤ β₂`, by `le_of_tendsto_of_tendsto'` on the finite-volume terms). `plusStateSpontaneousMagnetization_mono_beta` — `m*(β)` nondecreasing in `β` (the order parameter grows with `β`). This completes the β/J-monotonicity programme (Issue #3605: BC GKS-I/II → BC J-monotonicity → BC β-monotonicity → `m⁺`/`m*`). Reference: Friedli–Velenik (Cambridge, 2017), §3.6; Glimm–Jaffe Prop. 4.2.1. |
| §3.4 / Issue #3599 | **Box-independence of the cubic-exhaustion `±`-state functional** | **Done** | `Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/PlusStateCongrN.lean`. The `±`-state functional does not depend on the witnessing box `N` of the support hypothesis (well-definedness). `plusBoxObsExpectation_index_congr` — equal box radii give equal box expectations (proof-irrelevant supports). `plusStateExpectation_congr_N` — `plusStateExpectation J h β O hS₁ = plusStateExpectation J h β O hS₂` for any two witnesses `hS₁ : O.S ⊆ cubicBox d N₁`, `hS₂ : O.S ⊆ cubicBox d N₂` (both equal the limit of the screened box expectations, which are shifts of the same sequence; `tendsto_plusStateExpectation` + `tendsto_add_atTop_nat` + `tendsto_nhds_unique`). `minusStateExpectation_congr_N` — the `−`-state version (the `(−h, flipObs)` specialisation). Reference: Friedli–Velenik (Cambridge, 2017), §3.4 Theorem 3.17. |
| §3.6 / Issue #3599 | **Site-independence of the `+` magnetization `m⁺(a+ᵥx) = m⁺(x)`** | **Done** | `Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/MagnetizationSiteIndependence.lean`. The order parameter is translation-invariant. `LocalObservable.ext_of_support_eq` — extensionality with a provable support equality (`obtain` + `subst` of the support equality). `singleSpinObs_vadd_eq` — the translated single-spin observable equals the single-spin observable at the translated site (`vadd a (singleSpinObs x) = singleSpinObs (a +ᵥ x)`, via the support equality `vaddFinset a {x} = {a +ᵥ x}` + `configVaddEquiv_symm_apply` + `vaddSubtypeEquiv_apply_coe`). `plusStateExpectation_congr_obs` — observable-congruence (equal observables with possibly different witnessing boxes give the same value, via `plusStateExpectation_congr_N`). `mem_cubicBox_vadd` — `a +ᵥ x ∈ cubicBox d (latticeRadius x + latticeRadius a)`. `plusMagnetization_vadd` — `m⁺(a +ᵥ x) = m⁺(x)` (via `plusStateExpectation_vadd` + `congr_obs`). Reference: Friedli–Velenik (Cambridge, 2017), §3.6. |
| §3.7 / Thm 3.49 / Issue #3613 | **Two-point clustering bounds for the `+` boundary state** | **Done (finite-volume)** | `Inequalities/GKSBoundaryConditionTwoPoint.lean`. Finite-volume GKS consequences for two-point functions, towards the high-temperature vanishing of `m*`. `symmDiff_pair` — `{i} ∆ {j} = {i, j}` for `i ≠ j`. `gibbsExpectationBC_plus_spinProduct_pair_nonneg` — `⟨σ_i σ_j⟩⁺_Λ ≥ 0` (GKS-I, #3606). `gibbsExpectationBC_plus_two_point_ge_product` — the clustering bound `⟨σ_i⟩⁺_Λ · ⟨σ_j⟩⁺_Λ ≤ ⟨σ_i σ_j⟩⁺_Λ` (GKS-II, #3607, via `symmDiff_pair`). Reference: Friedli–Velenik (Cambridge, 2017), §3.7; Theorem 3.49 (GKS). |
| §3.7 / Issue #3613 | **Infinite-volume `+` two-point function `μ⁺(σ_i σ_j)`** | **Done** | `Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/PlusTwoPoint.lean`. The cubic-exhaustion `+`-state two-point function. `twoSpinObs i j` — the two-spin product observable (support `{i,j}`, `φ = s(σ_i)·s(σ_j)`); `twoSpinObs_support_subset`. `plusBoxObsExpectation_twoSpin_eq` — the finite-volume bridge: the `+` box expectation of the two-spin product equals the `+` boundary spin-product correlation `⟨σ_{⟨i⟩}σ_{⟨j⟩}⟩` on the cubic ambient (`Spin.sign = ↑toSign`, `Finset.prod_pair` for `i≠j`, `restrictConfig`). `plusTwoPoint i j J h β` — `μ⁺(σ_i σ_j)`. `tendsto_plusTwoPoint` — the screened two-spin `+` box expectations converge to `μ⁺(σ_i σ_j)`. Foundation for the clustering bound `m*² ≤ μ⁺(σ₀σₓ)` towards the high-temperature `m*=0`. Reference: Friedli–Velenik (Cambridge, 2017), §3.7. |
| §3.7 / Issue #3613 | **Clustering bound `m*² ≤ μ⁺(σ₀σₓ)`** | **Done** | `Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/SpontaneousMagnetizationTwoPoint.lean`. The squared `+`-state spontaneous magnetization is bounded by the infinite-volume `+` two-point function. `tendsto_plusMagnetization_atRadius` — the single-spin `+` box expectation at any box radius `M ≥ latticeRadius x` converges to `m⁺(x)` (box-independence of the limit, via `plusStateExpectation_congr_N`). `plusBoxObsExpectation_two_point_clustering` — the finite-box clustering `⟨σ_i⟩⁺_box · ⟨σ_j⟩⁺_box ≤ ⟨σ_i σ_j⟩⁺_box` (`i≠j`, from the single/two-spin bridges + GKS-II `gibbsExpectationBC_plus_two_point_ge_product`). `plusStateSpontaneousMagnetization_sq_le_plusTwoPoint` — `m*² ≤ μ⁺(σ₀σₓ)` for `x ≠ 0`, `0<β`, `0≤J`, by the box-aligned limit (`set M := latticeRadius 0 + latticeRadius x`) with `le_of_tendsto_of_tendsto'` and site-independence `m⁺(x) = m⁺(0) = m*` (`plusMagnetization_vadd`). Reference: Friedli–Velenik (Cambridge, 2017), §3.7. |
| §3.7.3 / Issue #3613 | **`+`-boundary high-temperature representation of `Z⁺_Λ` (FV (3.41)–(3.45))** | **Done** | `Conditioning/PlusHighTempRepresentation.lean`. The boundary-condition analogue of the free-state high-temperature representation, towards the high-temperature `m*(β)=0`. `boltzmannWeight_h_zero_prod` — the per-configuration product form `e^{-βH} = (cosh βJ)^{|E|}∏_e(1+tanh(βJ)σ_iσ_j)` (FV (3.42)). `boltzmannWeightBC_h_zero_prod_of_agrees` / `boltzmannWeightBC_plus_eq_indicator` — the `+`-boundary weight is the agreement-indicator of the ordinary Boltzmann weight, taking the product form on agreeing configurations. `sum_indicator_agreesOff_plus_prod_pow` — the **pinned-boundary parity collapse** (FV (3.44)): `∑_{σ : agreesOff Λ (+)} ∏_v σ_v^{k_v} = 2^{|Λ|}` iff every **interior** vertex `v ∈ Λ` has even `k_v` (exterior factors are pinned to `+1`), else `0` — the boundary analogue of `sum_prod_toSign_pow_real`, the parity condition restricted to `Λ`. `partitionFunctionBC_plus_h_zero_closed` — the headline representation (FV (3.45)): `Z⁺_Λ = 2^{|Λ|}(cosh βJ)^{|E|}∑_{X ⊆ E, even on Λ}(tanh βJ)^{|X|}`, the sum over subgraphs even at every interior vertex. Reference: Friedli–Velenik (Cambridge, 2017), §3.7.3, eqs. (3.41)–(3.45), pp. 116–117. |
| §3.7.3 / Issue #3613 | **`+`-boundary one-point function high-temperature ratio (FV (3.46)–(3.47))** | **Done** | `Conditioning/PlusOnePointRepresentation.lean`. The high-temperature representation of the `+`-boundary spin-product expectation `⟨σ_A⟩⁺_Λ`. `spinProduct_eq_prod_pow` — `σ^A = ∏_v (σ_v)^{[v∈A]}`. `spinProduct_mul_prod_edgeSpin_eq_prod_pow` — the combined vertex-power form `σ^A·∏_{e∈X}σ_iσ_j = ∏_v(σ_v)^{[v∈A]+deg_X(v)}` (the `A`-shifted degree). `numeratorBC_plus_spinProduct_h_zero_closed` — the numerator representation (FV (3.46)): `∑_σ σ^A·w⁺_Λ(σ) = 2^{|Λ|}(cosh βJ)^{|E|}∑_X(tanh βJ)^{|X|}`, summed over subgraphs with `[v∈A]+deg_X(v)` even on `Λ` (even off `A`, odd on `A∩Λ`; the set `E⁺;0` for a singleton). `gibbsExpectationBC_plus_spinProduct_h_zero_ratio` — `⟨σ_A⟩⁺_Λ = (∑_{X : A-shifted even} tanh^{|X|})/(∑_{X : even} tanh^{|X|})` (the `2^{|Λ|}(cosh βJ)^{|E|}` prefactor cancels). `evenSubgraphSum_pos` — the denominator is positive (from `Z⁺_Λ>0`). `gibbsExpectationBC_plus_singleSpin_h_zero_ratio` — the singleton `⟨σ_i⟩⁺_Λ` ratio over `E⁺;0` (odd at `i`, even elsewhere), the book's (3.46) form for the `m*=0` argument. Reference: Friedli–Velenik (Cambridge, 2017), §3.7.3, eqs. (3.46)–(3.47), pp. 116–117. |
| §3.7.3 / Issue #3613 | **Connected-component bound `⟨σ_i⟩⁺_Λ ≤ ∑_C (tanh βJ)^{\|C\|}` (FV (3.47)–(3.48))** | **Done** | `Conditioning/PlusOnePointConnectedBound.lean`. The decomposition of the `E⁺;0` numerator by the connected component of the origin, bounding the one-point ratio by the component sum. `componentOfZero X z` — the edge-connected component of vertex `z` inside an edge set (`biUnion` of `edgeComponent` over the `X`-edges at `z`); `componentOfZero_subset`, `mem_componentOfZero_of_incident`, `componentOfZero_absorbs_incident` (incidence closure), `componentOfZero_sdiff_no_shared_vertex`, `filter_card_componentOfZero_add` (degree splits), `filter_card_sdiff_eq_zero_of_mem_support`. `exists_componentOfZero_mem_of_odd` — odd degree at `i` ⟹ the origin lies in the component support. `sdiff_componentOfZero_even_on` — FV (3.47): the complement `X \ componentOfZero X i` is even on `Λ` (the parity at `i` is absorbed by the origin's component, leaving the rest even). `gibbsExpectationBC_plus_singleSpin_h_zero_le_connected` — FV (3.48): `⟨σ_i⟩⁺_Λ ≤ ∑_{C} (tanh βJ)^{\|C\|}` over the origin's components, for `i ∈ Λ` and `0 ≤ tanh βJ`. Proof: fibre the numerator over `componentOfZero · i` (`Finset.sum_fiberwise_of_maps_to`), pull out `(tanh βJ)^{\|C\|}` (card additivity over the disjoint `C ⊔ (X∖C)`), and bound each fibre's complement sum by the full even sum (the complement map `X ↦ X∖C` is injective into the even-on-`Λ` subgraphs, `Finset.sum_le_sum_of_subset_of_nonneg`) — the ratio-by-1 of FV (3.48). Reuses the cluster-expansion `edgeComponent` machinery. Reference: Friedli–Velenik (Cambridge, 2017), §3.7.3, eqs. (3.47)–(3.48), pp. 117–118. |
| §3.7.3 / Issue #3613 | **Distance bound for edge-connected edge sets (FV (3.49) geometric input)** | **Done** | `Conditioning/EdgeSetDistance.lean`. The graph-theoretic input to FV (3.49): a connected component reaching a far vertex must have many edges. `reachable_fromEdgeSet_of_mem_edge` — endpoints of an edge in `X` are reachable in `fromEdgeSet ↑X`. `reachable_fromEdgeSet_of_reflTransGen` — edge-adjacency chains (`ReflTransGen (edgeAdjacentIn X)`) lift to vertex reachability. `fromEdgeSet_dist_le_card` — `(fromEdgeSet ↑X).dist u v ≤ \|X\|` (a shortest path is a trail, so its edges are distinct and lie in `X`). `reachable_fromEdgeSet_of_edgeConnected` / `dist_le_card_of_edgeConnected` — for `IsEdgeConnected X` with `u,v` incident, `G.dist u v ≤ \|X\|` (`X ⊆ G.edgeFinset`, via `Reachable.dist_anti`). `componentOfZero_eq_edgeComponent` — when `X` has an edge at `z`, the vertex-component `componentOfZero X z` equals the edge-component `edgeComponent X e₀` (all `z`-edges share one component); `isEdgeConnected_componentOfZero`. `dist_le_card_componentOfZero` — `G.dist z v ≤ \|componentOfZero X z\|` for `v` incident to a component edge. Reference: Friedli–Velenik (Cambridge, 2017), §3.7.3, eq. (3.49), p. 118. |
| §3.7.3 / Issue #3613 | **Handshake parity and the second odd-degree vertex (FV (3.49))** | **Done** | `Conditioning/EdgeSetHandshake.lean`. The handshake lemma for edge subsets and its parity consequence — the contour-reaches-boundary mechanism. `sum_filter_card_eq_two_mul_card` — `∑_v (X.filter (v∈·)).card = 2·\|X\|` (sum of degrees = twice the edge count, for `X ⊆ G.edgeFinset`). `even_card_odd_filter_card` — the set of odd-degree vertices has even cardinality (via `Finset.even_sum_iff_even_card_odd`). `exists_ne_odd_filter_card` — odd degree at `z` forces a second odd-degree vertex `j ≠ z` (the odd-degree set is even and contains `z`, so is not `{z}`). `exists_dist_le_card_componentOfZero` — combining with the distance bound: if the origin component has odd degree at `z`, there is a `j ≠ z` of **odd component-degree** with `G.dist z j ≤ \|componentOfZero X z\|` (the contour reaches a second odd-degree site, a boundary site at distance `≥ n` in the cubic-box `+` setup; the returned oddness lets downstream identify `j` as a shell vertex). Reference: Friedli–Velenik (Cambridge, 2017), §3.7.3, eqs. (3.48)–(3.49), pp. 117–118. |
| §3.7.3 / Issue #3613 | **Subtype-to-ambient distance transfer and the lattice component bound (FV (3.49))** | **Done** | `Conditioning/InduceDistanceTransfer.lean`. The transfer converting an induced-box distance into the lattice `ℓ¹` distance. `induceValHom` — the inclusion homomorphism `G.induce s →g G`. `dist_val_le_induce_dist` — `G.dist a b ≤ (G.induce s).dist a b` (a distance-realising induced walk maps to an ambient walk of the same length, via `Walk.map` + `length_map`). `latticeDistance_le_induce_dist` — the lattice specialisation `latticeDistance d a b ≤ (inducedGraph (latticeGraph d) Λ).dist a b` (via `latticeGraph_dist_eq_latticeDistance`). `latticeDistance_le_card_componentOfZero` — composing the handshake partner (`exists_dist_le_card_componentOfZero`) with the transfer: on the induced box graph, odd degree at `z` yields a `j ≠ z` of odd component-degree with `latticeDistance d z.val j.val ≤ \|componentOfZero X z\|` (reachability from component edge-connectedness via `reachable_fromEdgeSet_of_edgeConnected` + `Reachable.mono`). The component, reaching a far lattice site, must have many edges — the FV (3.49) `\|C\| ≥ n` geometry. Reference: Friedli–Velenik (Cambridge, 2017), §3.7.3, eq. (3.49), p. 118. |
| §3.7.3 / Issue #3613 | **Origin component reaches the boundary `\|C\| ≥ n` (FV (3.49))** | **Done** | `Conditioning/CubicBoxComponentSize.lean`. The FV (3.49) geometric estimate in the cubic box. `latticeDistance_origin_ge_of_not_mem_cubicBox` — a site outside `cubicBox d n` is at lattice distance `≥ n+1` from the origin (some coordinate has `\|·\| ≥ n+1`). `even_filter_card_componentOfZero` — parity inheritance: even `X`-degree at `v` gives even `componentOfZero X z`-degree (the component-degree is the full degree on its support, else `0`). `card_componentOfZero_ge_of_E0` — `n ≤ \|componentOfZero X z\|` for an `E⁺;0` subgraph in `B(m)` with `+` boundary on inner box `B(n)` (origin `z`, `z.val = 0`): the component's second odd-degree vertex must lie outside the inner box (even degree there forbids it via `even_filter_card_componentOfZero` + `mem_plusBoxInterior`), at lattice distance `≥ n+1` from the origin, reached within `\|C\|` edges (`latticeDistance_le_card_componentOfZero`). Reference: Friedli–Velenik (Cambridge, 2017), §3.7.3, eq. (3.49), p. 118. |
| §3.7.3 / Issue #3613 | **Degree bound on the walk count (FV (3.49) `(2d)^{2ℓ}` factor)** | **Done** | `Conditioning/WalkCountDegreeBound.lean`. The counting input to FV (3.49): the number of walks of a given length is degree-bounded. `walksFromCount G u k` — the number of length-`k` walks starting at `u` (`∑_v \|finsetWalkLength k u v\|`). `walksFromCount_le_pow` — `walksFromCount u k ≤ Δ^k` for a uniform degree bound `Δ` (induction on `k`: each length-`(k+1)` walk is a first edge to a neighbour followed by a length-`k` walk, so the count multiplies by at most the degree; `card_biUnion_le` + `card_neighborSet_eq_degree`). `walksFromCount_inducedLatticeGraph_le` — the lattice specialisation `walksFromCount (inducedGraph (latticeGraph d) Λ) u k ≤ (2d)^k` (via `inducedLatticeGraph_degree_le`), the `(2d)^{2ℓ}` factor of FV (3.49). Reference: Friedli–Velenik (Cambridge, 2017), §3.7.3, eq. (3.49), p. 118. |
| §3.7.3 / Issue #3613 | **Geometric series tail and exponential decay (FV (3.49))** | **Done** | `Conditioning/GeometricTail.lean`. The analytic input to the FV (3.49) decay `⟨σ₀⟩⁺_{B(n)} ≤ ∑_{ℓ≥n}(4d²·tanh βJ)^ℓ ≤ e^{-cn}`. `tsum_geometric_tail` — `∑'_ℓ q^{n+ℓ} = q^n·(1-q)⁻¹` for `0≤q<1` (via `tsum_mul_left` + `tsum_geometric_of_lt_one`). `tendsto_geometric_tail` — the tail `q^n·(1-q)⁻¹ → 0` as `n → ∞`. `pow_eq_exp_mul_log` — `q^n = exp(n·log q)`, the `e^{-cn}` form (`c = -log q > 0` for `0<q<1`). Reference: Friedli–Velenik (Cambridge, 2017), §3.7.3, eq. (3.49), p. 118. |
| §3.7.3 / Issue #3613 | **Geometric domination of a counted sum (FV (3.49) capstone bound)** | **Done** | `Conditioning/CountGeometricCapstone.lean`. The analytic capstone of FV (3.49). `sum_pow_le_geometric_tail_of_count` — for a finite family `S` with size function `g ≥ n` and member-count `#{C : g C = ℓ} ≤ M^ℓ`, the weighted sum `∑_{C∈S} q^{g(C)} ≤ (Mq)^n/(1-Mq)` (for `0 < Mq < 1`). Proof: group `S` by size (`sum_fiberwise_of_maps_to`), bound each size class `#·q^ℓ ≤ M^ℓ q^ℓ = (Mq)^ℓ`, factor out `(Mq)^n` (each size `≥ n`), and dominate the reindexed `∑_{ℓ} (Mq)^{ℓ-n}` by the geometric series `∑'_k (Mq)^k = (1-Mq)⁻¹` (`Summable.sum_le_tsum`). With `S =` the origin components, `g = |·|`, `M = (2d)²`, `q = tanh βJ`, this gives `⟨σ₀⟩⁺_{B(n)} ≤ (4d²·tanh βJ)^n/(1-4d²·tanh βJ) → 0`, reducing the high-temperature `m*(β)=0` to the counting bound `#{C : |C|=ℓ} ≤ (2d)^{2ℓ}` (FV Lemma 3.38). Reference: Friedli–Velenik (Cambridge, 2017), §3.7.3, eq. (3.49), p. 118. |
| §3.7.3 / Issue #3613 | **FV Lemma 3.38: closed walk crossing each edge twice** | **Done** | `Conditioning/EdgeWalkExistence.lean`. The Eulerian-type construction underlying the counting injection. `exists_adj_of_mem_edge` — extract a directed adjacency `G.Adj v w` with `e = s(v,w)` from `v ∈ e ∈ G.edgeSet`. `exists_boundary_edge` — connectivity frontier: an edge-connected `X` has, across any proper nonempty subset `P`, an edge of `X∖P` incident to `P` (so the prefix can grow). `edgeAdjacentIn_mono`, `isEdgeConnected_insert` — edge-adjacency monotonicity and that inserting an incident edge preserves edge-connectedness. `exists_closed_walk_of_edgeConnected` (**FV Lemma 3.38**) — a connected edge set `X` (non-diagonal edges) admits a closed walk from any incident vertex `z` with `w.edges.toFinset = X` and `w.length = 2|X|`. Proof: grow a connected prefix `P` one incident edge at a time (`exists_boundary_edge`), splicing a `v → w → v` detour (the new edge, traversed twice) at the first visit to the shared vertex `v` (`Walk.takeUntil`/`dropUntil`/`take_spec`); the edge set gains `{e}` and the length gains `2`. Reference: Friedli–Velenik (Cambridge, 2017), §3.7.3, Lemma 3.38, p. 117. |
| §3.7.3 / Issue #3613 | **Counting injection `#{connected C ∋z, |C|=ℓ} ≤ (2d)^{2ℓ}` (FV (3.49))** | **Done** | `Conditioning/EdgeWalkCounting.lean`. The FV (3.49) counting bound, completing the inputs to the high-temperature `m*(β)=0`. `exists_closed_walk_in_supergraph` — the Lemma-3.38 walk transported (via `Walk.transfer`) into the ambient graph `G`: a connected `C ⊆ G.edgeFinset` admits a closed `G`-walk from any incident `z` with edge set `C` and length `2|C|`. `card_connected_edge_sets_le` — the counting injection: for a finite family `S` of connected edge sets of size `ℓ` containing `z`, `#S ≤ #{closed walks of length 2ℓ from z}` (each `C ↦ its Lemma-3.38 walk`, injective since `C` is the walk's edge set, via `Finset.card_le_card_of_injOn`). `card_connected_edge_sets_inducedLatticeGraph_le` — the `(2d)^{2ℓ}` lattice bound (closed walks `≤ walksFromCount z (2ℓ) ≤ (2d)^{2ℓ}` via `Finset.single_le_sum` + `walksFromCount_inducedLatticeGraph_le`). Reference: Friedli–Velenik (Cambridge, 2017), §3.7.3, eq. (3.49), p. 118. |
| §3.7.3 / Issue #3613 | **High-temperature box magnetization decay (FV (3.49) capstone)** | **Done** | `Conditioning/CubicBoxMagnetizationDecay.lean`. `gibbsExpectationBC_box_singleSpin_le` — in the cubic box `B(m)` with `+` boundary on the inner box `B(n)`, `⟨σ₀⟩⁺ ≤ (4d²·tanh βJ)^n/(1-4d²·tanh βJ)` for `0<d`, `0<tanh βJ`, `4d²·tanh βJ<1`. Composes the component bound (3.48, `gibbsExpectationBC_plus_singleSpin_h_zero_le_connected`), the component-size lower bound `|C|≥n` (`card_componentOfZero_ge_of_E0`), the counting bound `#{C:|C|=ℓ}≤(2d)^{2ℓ}=(4d²)^ℓ` (`card_connected_edge_sets_inducedLatticeGraph_le`, each origin component being connected, `⊆` box edges, `∋` origin via its odd-degree `z`-edge), and the geometric capstone (`sum_pow_le_geometric_tail_of_count` with `M=4d²`, `q=tanh βJ`). The exponential decay of `⟨σ₀⟩⁺_{B(n)}` as `n→∞`, the heart of FV's `m*(β)=0`. Reference: Friedli–Velenik (Cambridge, 2017), §3.7.3, eq. (3.49), p. 118. |
| §3.7.3 / Issue #3613 | **High-temperature `m*(β)=0` (FV §3.7.3 — COMPLETE)** | **Done** | `Conditioning/HighTempZeroMagnetization.lean`. `plusStateSpontaneousMagnetization_eq_zero_of_high_temp` — the high-temperature half of the phase transition: for `0<d`, `0<β`, `0<J`, `4d²·tanh βJ<1`, the `+`-state spontaneous magnetization vanishes, `m*(β)=0`. The infinite-volume limit of the box decay bound (`gibbsExpectationBC_box_singleSpin_le`): the screened box magnetizations converge to `m*` (`tendsto_plusMagnetization`), each bounded by `(4d²·tanh βJ)^n/(1-4d²·tanh βJ) → 0` (`tendsto_geometric_tail`, via the bridge `plusBoxObsExpectation_singleSpin_eq`), so `m* ≤ 0`; with `m* ≥ 0` (`plusStateSpontaneousMagnetization_nonneg`), `m*=0`. This completes the full FV §3.7.3 high-temperature argument (the random-graph representation (3.41)–(3.45), the one-point ratio (3.46)–(3.47), the component bound (3.48), and the geometric/Eulerian-counting estimate (3.49) + Lemma 3.38). Reference: Friedli–Velenik (Cambridge, 2017), §3.7.3, pp. 116–118. |

All four `freeEnergyInfinite_*` are now fully related in the matching-widths slice of the parameter grid. |

| §4.6 | **Prop 4.6.1 (`f_Λ` convergence)** | **Done (disjoint-tower + `BoundedEdgeDensity`) + concrete J=0 / β=0 instances** | Base form: `freeEnergyAlongExhaustion_tendsto_of_superadditive` (4 bundled hypotheses — `hcard_add`, `hsuper`, `hbdd`, `hcard_one`). Relaxed form: `freeEnergyAlongExhaustion_tendsto_of_disjoint_tower` (3 hypotheses: `hcard_add`, `hsuper`, `hcard_one` + the structural `BoundedEdgeDensity G Λ`). The explicit `hbdd` is discharged automatically via `BddAbove_freeEnergyAlongExhaustion_range`. Bundled form: `DisjointTowerHypotheses` record (`card_add`/`super`/`card_one`) + `freeEnergyAlongExhaustion_tendsto_of_disjointTowerHypotheses` wrapper. Generic builder: `DisjointTowerHypotheses.of_log_linear_card` (whenever `log Z_Λ = |Λ| · c` for some constant `c`, super-additivity is automatic equality under `hcard_add`). Concrete J=0 and β=0 instances: `DisjointTowerHypotheses.of_{J_zero,beta_zero}` via the log-linear builder; closed forms `log_partitionFunctionΛ_{J_zero,beta_zero}`. Corollaries `freeEnergyAlongExhaustion_{J_zero,beta_zero}_tendsto_of_hcard_add` give Fekete convergence from `hcard_add + hcard_one + BoundedEdgeDensity` alone. Proof (general): apply mathlib `Subadditive.tendsto_lim` to `u_n := -log Z_{Λ_n}`, translate via `card_n = n · card_1` to `freeEnergyAlongExhaustion`. The super-additivity input is provided by `log_partitionFunctionΛ_disjUnion_super_additive` in the general case. Supporting: `freeEnergy_convergent_subgraph`, `freeEnergyInfinite_eq_of_tendsto`. |

| §4.6 | Concrete translation-invariant lattice graph (groundwork for Prop 4.6.1) | **Done (`ℤ^d` instance)** | `isTranslationInvariant_latticeGraph` (`Concrete/IntLattice.lean`): for every `d : ℕ`, `Ambient.IsTranslationInvariant (Fin d → ℤ) (IsingModel.latticeGraph d)` under the canonical pointwise additive self-action of `Fin d → ℤ`. First non-trivial `IsTranslationInvariant` instance (beyond `⊥`, `⊤`). Proof reduces `(t +ᵥ u) i - (t +ᵥ v) i` to `u i - v i` coordinatewise, leaving the ℓ¹-distance-1 condition invariant. Groundwork toward the concrete Prop 4.6.1 exhaustion; a compatible `TranslationInvariantExhaustion` on `ℤ^d` is deferred. |
| §4.6 | Translation-invariant exhaustion tower and its Fekete convergence | **Done (`log Z` super-additivity supplied by the caller)** | `Ambient.TranslationInvariantExhaustion` (`IsingModel/TranslationInvariance/Tower.lean`) refines `Ambient.Exhaustion` by presenting each successive volume as the previous one together with a disjoint translate of the base volume, which is what makes the volume cardinalities additive and Fekete's subadditive argument available. Translates of a `Finset` are `Ambient.vaddFinset`, whose basic API is `vaddFinset_card`, `vaddFinset_zero` and `vaddFinset_disjoint_of_disjoint` (`IsingModel/TranslationInvariance/Finset.lean`) — the last recording that a translation, being injective, cannot merge two disjoint volumes, so disjointness may be stated on the untranslated blocks. `freeEnergyAlongExhaustion_tendsto_of_translationInvariant` is the tower's convergence statement: the free-energy density converges to `freeEnergyInfinite` given bounded edge density, a non-degenerate base volume, and `log Z` super-additivity supplied as a hypothesis. That last hypothesis is discharged rather than assumed on a translation-invariant graph with ferromagnetic parameters — `log_partitionFunctionΛ_super_of_translationInvariant` obtains it from invariance of `Z_Λ` under the shift plus disjoint-union super-additivity, and `freeEnergyAlongExhaustion_tendsto_of_translationInvariant_auto` is the resulting unconditional-in-`hsuper` form. Reference: Glimm–Jaffe *Quantum Physics* 2nd ed., §4.6 Prop 4.6.1, p. 68. |
| §4.6 | Concrete cubic `Ambient.Exhaustion` on `ℤ^d` | **Done (`cubicExhaustion d`)** | `cubicBox (d n : ℕ) : Finset (Fin d → ℤ)` as `Fintype.piFinset (fun _ => Finset.Icc (-↑n) ↑n)`, with `mem_cubicBox` (membership), `cubicBox_mono` (`m ≤ n ⇒ cubicBox d m ⊆ cubicBox d n`), `cubicBox_exhaust` (every finite `A ⊆ Fin d → ℤ` is contained in a sufficiently large cube). Witness `N := Finset.max'` of `natAbs` over coordinates of `A` in the non-degenerate case (`A.Nonempty` and `d ≠ 0`); degenerate case (`A = ∅` or `d = 0`) discharges by vacuity / exfalso. Assembled into `cubicExhaustion (d : ℕ) : Ambient.Exhaustion (Fin d → ℤ)` (`Concrete/CubicExhaustion.lean`), the first concrete exhaustion on the physical integer lattice. Makes `correlationInfinite (latticeGraph d) (cubicExhaustion d) p A` an explicit object. |

This remains post-extraction and does not convert the finite `Finset` into the existing `Fin n` compact finite real-cover package. (PR #2712.) |

On compact `K ⊆ leeYangDomain`, finite-volume Lee-Yang nonvanishing and continuity give a positive lower bound for `‖P_E(exp(-2βh))‖`. The bound depends on the finite graph, so it is not lower normalised-log control uniform in the exhaustion stage. (PR #2685.) |
| §4.6 Thm 4.6.2 | ∞-vol Vitali envelope input: finite-stage Lee-Yang `Z_ℂ` lower bound | **Done (finite-stage `Z` lower bound)** | Abstract (`IsingModel/ComplexAnalyticity.lean`): `norm_partitionFunctionComplex_ge_exp_mul_isingEdgePoly_lower`. If `|Re h| ≤ R` and the Lee-Yang polynomial factor is bounded below by `ε`, then FV factorisation gives `exp(-β R |ι|) * ε ≤ ‖Z_ℂ(J,h,β)‖`. The bound remains finite-graph dependent through `ε`; it does not supply stage-uniform lower normalised-log control. (PR #2686.) |
| §4.6 Thm 4.6.2 | ∞-vol Vitali envelope input: polynomial witnesses to lower normalised-log bridge | **Done conditionally (polynomial lower-log bridge)** | Abstract (`IsingModel/AmbientComplexAnalyticity.lean`): `real_log_norm_partitionFunctionComplexAlongExhaustion_div_card_ge_of_poly_lower_stage` and `exists_lower_log_norm_partitionFunctionComplexAlongExhaustion_of_poly_lower`. The ℤ^d wrappers are defined in `PerStageComplex/Bounds/PolynomialLower.lean`. The `PerStageComplex/Bounds.lean` group umbrella re-exports them. The witness lower-log input remains conditional. (PR #2687.) |

This composes the PR #2687 polynomial-witness lower-log bridge with the PR #2683 Lee-Yang upper/lower-log handoff to obtain `‖f_n(h)‖ ≤ C + π` on compact `K ⊆ leeYangDomain`. The ℤ^d wrappers are defined in `PerStageComplex/Bounds/PolynomialLower.lean`. The `PerStageComplex/Bounds.lean` group umbrella re-exports them. The polynomial-witness lower-log input remains conditional. (PR #2688.) |

The ℤ^d wrappers are defined in `PerStageComplex/Bounds/PolynomialLower.lean`. The `PerStageComplex/Bounds.lean` group umbrella re-exports them. The polynomial-witness lower-log input remains conditional. (PR #2689.) |

If `Metric.closedBall h₀ ρ ⊆ leeYangDomain` and the polynomial witnesses have a uniform lower normalised-log bound on that closed ball, then `‖f_n(h)‖ ≤ C + π` on `Metric.ball h₀ ρ`. The polynomial-witness lower-log input remains conditional. (PR #2690.) |
