The radius-dependent polynomial-witness lower-log input remains conditional. (PR #2691.) |
| §4.6 Thm 4.6.2 | ∞-vol Vitali envelope input: root-product Lee-Yang lower-log bound | **Done (lower-log discharged)** | Finite/root side (`IsingModel/ComplexAnalyticity.lean`): `MultilinPoly.uniformPolynomial`, `_eval`, `_natDegree_le_card`, `Polynomial.one_sub_radius_pow_natDegree_le_norm_eval_of_roots_norm_ge_one`, `isingEdgePoly_uniformPolynomial_roots_norm_ge_one`, and `one_sub_radius_pow_card_le_norm_isingEdgePoly_eval_leeYangFugacityVec`. Abstract (`IsingModel/AmbientComplexAnalyticity.lean`): `exists_poly_lower_norm_isingEdgePoly_eval_leeYangFugacityVec_on_isCompact`, `exists_lower_log_norm_partitionFunctionComplexAlongExhaustion_leeYang_of_isCompact`, and unconditional locally bounded wrappers `exists_norm_freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_le_leeYang_{of_isCompact,on_ball,around}`. The ℤ^d wrappers are defined in `PerStageComplex/Bounds/LeeYangLower.lean`. The `PerStageComplex/Bounds.lean` group umbrella re-exports them. The compact fugacity gap gives `r<1`, the root-product estimate gives `ε_n=(1-r)^|Λ_n|`, and `log ε_n / |Λ_n| = log(1-r)`, closing the previous polynomial-witness lower-log hypothesis. Remaining #628 work is the coherent locally-uniform branch-family / Montel construction and global branch patching. (PR #2692.) |
| §4.6 Thm 4.6.2 | ∞-vol Vitali envelope input: compact-field upper normalised-log bound | **Done conditionally (upper log bound)** | Abstract (`IsingModel/AmbientComplexAnalyticity.lean`): `real_log_norm_partitionFunctionComplexAlongExhaustion_div_card_le_of_re_bound_stage` and `exists_real_log_norm_partitionFunctionComplexAlongExhaustion_div_card_le_on_isCompact`. Under compact field control, nonempty stages, bounded edge density, and nonvanishing of `Z_{Λ_n}(h)` on the compact set, the normalised real logarithms `Real.log ‖Z_{Λ_n}(h)‖ / |Λ_n|` admit one stage-independent upper bound. The ℤ^d wrappers are defined in `PerStageComplex/Bounds/UpperLog.lean`. The `PerStageComplex/Bounds.lean` group umbrella re-exports them. This supplies only the upper side of the absolute-log control; PR #2692 supplies the matching lower side on compact Lee-Yang sets, while no general compact-field lower theorem is claimed. (PR #2681.) |
| §4.6 Thm 4.6.2 | ∞-vol Vitali envelope input: Lee-Yang compact upper normalised-log bound | **Done conditionally (Lee-Yang upper log bound)** | Abstract (`IsingModel/AmbientComplexAnalyticity.lean`): `exists_log_norm_partitionFunctionComplexAlongExhaustion_div_card_le_leeYangDomain`. For real ferromagnetic `β > 0`, `J > 0`, compact `K ⊆ leeYangDomain`, nonempty stages, and bounded edge density, the Lee-Yang nonvanishing theorem supplies the nonzero hypothesis required by PR #2681, yielding a stage-independent upper bound for `Real.log ‖Z_{Λ_n}(h)‖ / |Λ_n|` on `K`. The ℤ^d wrappers are defined in `PerStageComplex/Bounds/PolynomialLower.lean`. The `PerStageComplex/Bounds.lean` group umbrella re-exports them. This row is the upper half only; the compact Lee-Yang lower half is supplied later by PR #2692. (PR #2682.) |
| §4.6 Thm 4.6.2 | ∞-vol Vitali envelope input: Lee-Yang lower-log to locally bounded handoff | **Done conditionally (lower-log handoff)** | Abstract (`IsingModel/AmbientComplexAnalyticity.lean`): `exists_abs_log_norm_partitionFunctionComplexAlongExhaustion_div_card_le_lower_leeYang` and `exists_norm_freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_le_lower_log_leeYang`. A compact Lee-Yang upper bound from PR #2682 plus a stage-uniform lower normalised-log hypothesis yields the absolute normalised-log bound and then the locally bounded free-energy estimate `‖f_n(h)‖ ≤ C + π`. The ℤ^d wrappers are defined in `PerStageComplex/Bounds/PolynomialLower.lean`. The `PerStageComplex/Bounds.lean` group umbrella re-exports them. In this handoff theorem the lower control is an explicit input; PR #2692 supplies that input on compact Lee-Yang sets. (PR #2683.) |
| §4.6 Thm 4.6.2 | ∞-vol Vitali envelope input: normalised log bound handoff | **Done conditionally (normalised-log handoff)** | Abstract (`IsingModel/AmbientComplexAnalyticity.lean`): `norm_freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_le_of_abs_log_norm_bound_stage` and `_on_set`. A bound on the normalised absolute logarithm `|log ‖Z_{Λ_n}(h)‖| / |Λ_n|` gives the corresponding principal free-energy bound `‖f_n(h)‖ ≤ C + π/|Λ_n|`; the setwise version packages a common `C` for all stages and fields in a set. The ℤ^d wrappers are defined in `PerStageComplex/Bounds/UpperLog.lean`. The `PerStageComplex/Bounds.lean` group umbrella re-exports them. The required normalised-log control remains the missing normal-family input. (PR #2679.) |
| §4.6 Thm 4.6.2 | ∞-vol Vitali envelope input: locally bounded family from normalised log control | **Done conditionally (locally bounded handoff)** | Abstract (`IsingModel/AmbientComplexAnalyticity.lean`): `norm_freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_le_of_abs_log_norm_bound_on_set_uniform`. Since nonempty finite stages have `1 ≤ |Λ_n|`, the PR #2679 bound improves to the stage-independent estimate `‖f_n(h)‖ ≤ C + π` on the same set. The ℤ^d wrappers are defined in `PerStageComplex/Bounds/UpperLog.lean`. The `PerStageComplex/Bounds.lean` group umbrella re-exports them. This is the locally bounded family shape needed by Montel, still conditional on the normalised-log hypothesis. (PR #2680.) |

| Section | Result | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| §5.1 | Pure/mixed phase criteria | **Done (algebraic)** | `mixed_phase_truncated2`, `mixed_phase_pure_iff`, `truncated2_le_one` |
| §5.1 | Spontaneous magnetization `m*` (p. 77) | **Done (complete)** | `spontaneousMagnetization` (infimum form) + `tendsto_…_nhdsGT` (right-limit `m* = lim_{h→0+} M(h)`) + nonneg/≤1/≤M(h)/indep_exhaustion |
| §5.1 | Cluster property — `J = 0` (non-interacting) slice (pp. 72–74) | **Done (trivial slice)** | `truncated2_J_zero_of_ne` (`Inequalities/GHS.lean`): for `i ≠ j` and any `h, β`, `truncated2 G ⟨0, h, β⟩ i j = 0`. Via `correlation_J_zero` (`⟨σ^A⟩ = tanh(β·h)^{\|A\|}`) + `Finset.card_pair`/`card_singleton`. The non-interacting slice: the Hamiltonian has no `J`-coupling, so any two distinct sites factorise identically — no distance / separation / high-temperature hypothesis needed (`β` is arbitrary). General-`J` decay-at-large-separation in pure phases remains unformalized. |
| §5.1 | Cluster property — `β = 0` (infinite-temperature) slice (pp. 72–74) | **Done (trivial slice)** | `truncated2_beta_zero` (`Inequalities/GHS.lean`): for any `J, h` and any sites `i, j` (not necessarily distinct), `truncated2 G ⟨J, h, 0⟩ i j = 0`. Via `correlation_beta_zero_vanish_of_nonempty_A`. Companion to the `J = 0` slice. At `β = 0` the diagonal truncated value also vanishes since `⟨σ_i⟩ = 0`. |
| §5.1 | Cluster property — truncated 3-point trivial slices (pp. 72–74) | **Done (trivial slices)** | `truncated3_J_zero_of_pairwise_distinct` and `truncated3_beta_zero` (`Inequalities/GHS.lean`): the Ursell 3-point function vanishes at `J = 0` (pairwise distinct sites, via `correlation_J_zero` giving the `t³ - 3t³ + 2t³ = 0` combination with `t = tanh(β·h)`) and at `β = 0` (any sites; coincident indices are collapsed at the Finset level in the definition of `truncated3`, so the statement is unconditional on distinctness — cf. `truncated2_beta_zero`). Extension of `truncated2_*` lemmas to the 3-point case. `correlation_beta_zero_vanish_of_nonempty_A` is the common ingredient at `β = 0`. |
| §5.1 | Cluster property — ∞-vol J=0 slice (pp. 72–74) | **Done (trivial slice ∞-vol lift)** | `correlationInfinite_J_zero` (`AmbientLattice.lean`): for ferromagnetic `⟨0, h, β⟩`, `correlationInfinite G Λ ⟨0, h, β⟩ A = tanh(β·h)^{\|A\|}`. Proof via `correlationAlongExhaustion_J_zero_of_subset` (stagewise closed form using `liftFinset_card` helper) + `correlationAlongExhaustion_J_zero_eventually_eq` (eventually-constant sequence) + uniqueness of limits against `correlationAlongExhaustion_tendsto_ciSup`. Corollary: `truncated2Infinite_J_zero_of_ne` — ∞-vol counterpart of `truncated2_J_zero_of_ne` (PR #207). |
| §5.1 | Cluster property — ∞-vol β=0 slice (pp. 72–74) | **Done (trivial slice ∞-vol lift)** | `correlationInfinite_beta_zero_vanish` (`AmbientLattice.lean`): for any `J, h` and nonempty `A`, `correlationInfinite G Λ ⟨J, h, 0⟩ A = 0`. Proof: the sequence `correlationAlongExhaustion G Λ ⟨J, h, 0⟩ A` is pointwise zero (`correlation_beta_zero_vanish_of_nonempty_A` on the lifted subset when `A ⊆ Λ.volume n`; default-0 otherwise), hence `⨆ = 0`. No ferromagnetic hypothesis needed. Corollary: `truncated2Infinite_beta_zero` — ∞-vol counterpart of `truncated2_beta_zero` (PR #208). |
| §5.1 | Cluster property — ∞-vol truncated 3-point trivial slices (pp. 72–74) | **Done (trivial slices ∞-vol lift)** | `truncated3Infinite_J_zero_of_pairwise_distinct` and `truncated3Infinite_beta_zero` (`AmbientLattice.lean`): ∞-vol counterparts of the finite-volume PR #209 slices. J=0 ferromagnetic case via `correlationInfinite_J_zero` + Finset card identities (`t³ - 3·t³ + 2·t³ = 0`). β=0 case via `correlationInfinite_beta_zero_vanish` (all seven Ursell terms zero). |
| §5.1 | Cluster property — Lebowitz 4-point β=0 slice (finite + ∞-vol) (pp. 72–74) | **Done (β=0 trivial slice)** | `truncated4_beta_zero` (`Inequalities/GHS.lean`) and `truncated4Infinite_beta_zero` (`AmbientLattice.lean`): at β=0 the Lebowitz 4-point truncated function vanishes identically, via `correlation_beta_zero_vanish_of_nonempty_A` / `correlationInfinite_beta_zero_vanish`. Note: J=0 does *not* give vanishing (for pairwise distinct sites the Lebowitz 4-point is `-2·t⁴` with `t = tanh(β·h)`), so only the β=0 slice is added.
| §5.1 | Cluster property — Lebowitz 4-point J=0 closed form (finite + ∞-vol) (pp. 72–74) | **Done (J=0 closed form)** | `truncated4_J_zero_of_pairwise_distinct` (`Inequalities/GHS.lean`) and `truncated4Infinite_J_zero_of_pairwise_distinct` (`AmbientLattice.lean`, ferromagnetic): at J=0 with pairwise distinct sites, the Lebowitz 4-point equals `-2·t⁴` with `t = tanh(β·h)`. Via `correlation_J_zero` / `correlationInfinite_J_zero` + Finset card identities (4, 2, 2, 2, 2, 2, 2). This is non-vanishing in general but always `≤ 0`, consistent with Cor 4.3.3's `U₄ ≤ 0` bound at h=0 (the J=0 slice here is a separate special case, not a direct witness). |
| §5.1 | Cluster property — conditional decay: summable ⇒ cofinite → 0 (pp. 72–74) | **Done (conditional real-analysis step)** | `truncated2Infinite_tendsto_cofinite_zero_of_summable` (`AmbientLattice.lean`): `Summable (fun j : V => truncated2Infinite G Λ p i j) → Tendsto ... Filter.cofinite (nhds 0)`. The `Summable` hypothesis is a finiteness condition on the two-point function summed over `j`; in translation-invariant / connected-correlation settings it matches the physical "finite susceptibility" (away from the critical line). `Filter.cofinite` is the filter of cofinite subsets; on `Fin d → ℤ` (with `d ≥ 1`) bounded subsets are finite, so cofinite aligns with the informal "|r| → ∞" filter. Direct specialization of mathlib's `Summable.tendsto_cofinite_zero`. ℤ^d wrapper `truncated2Infinite_latticeGraph_tendsto_cofinite_zero_of_summable` (`Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/CorrelationDecay.lean`). This PR supplies the elementary real-analysis building block; later Simon–Lieb/high-temperature results discharge the summability input in high-temperature regimes. (PR #779.) |
| §5.1 | ℓ¹ lattice distance on ℤ^d (infrastructure for cluster decay) | **Done (definition + 5 basic lemmas)** | `latticeDistance (d : ℕ) (x y : Fin d → ℤ) : ℕ := ∑ i, (x i - y i).natAbs` (`Lattice.lean`). Basic lemmas: `latticeDistance_self` (`= 0`), `latticeDistance_comm` (symmetry), `latticeDistance_eq_zero_iff` (`= 0 ↔ x = y`), `latticeDistance_triangle` (`d(x,z) ≤ d(x,y) + d(y,z)`), and `latticeGraph_adj_iff_latticeDistance_eq_one` (`(latticeGraph d).Adj x y ↔ latticeDistance d x y = 1`). Range kept in `ℕ` to sidestep `ℕ`/`ℤ`/`ℝ` cast bookkeeping in subsequent summability arguments. Part of the §5.1 cluster-decay infrastructure series (Epic #780); the companion statement that `Filter.cofinite` on `Fin d → ℤ` is equivalent to `latticeDistance (fixed basepoint, ·) → ∞` for `d ≥ 1` (which upgrades PR #779 into a concrete distance-based statement) is deferred to a follow-up PR. (PR #781.) **Graph distance = ℓ¹ distance**: `latticeGraph_dist_eq_latticeDistance` (`(latticeGraph d).dist x y = latticeDistance d x y`) — the shortest-path metric of the lattice graph coincides with the ℓ¹ distance, with `latticeGraph_connected` (ℤ^d is connected), `latticeDistance_exists_adj_step` (the inductive geodesic step: one coordinate moved toward `y` reduces the distance by one), `latticeGraph_exists_walk_length` (a geodesic walk of length `latticeDistance` exists, giving `dist ≤`), and `latticeDistance_le_walk_length` (every walk has length `≥ latticeDistance`, giving `dist ≥`). This grounds the lattice-distance decay statements in the actual graph metric. |
| §5.1 | Finite ℓ¹ balls + proper map to ℕ (infrastructure for cluster decay) | **Done (finite balls + `Tendsto` to `atTop` along `cofinite`)** | `latticeDistance_le_finite` + `tendsto_latticeDistance_atTop_cofinite` (`Lattice.lean`). The ball `{j : Fin d → ℤ | latticeDistance d i j ≤ N}` is finite for every dimension `d`, basepoint `i`, and radius `N`, via the coordinatewise bound `(i k - j k).natAbs ≤ latticeDistance d i j` (one summand is at most a ℕ-sum) placing each `j k` in a finite integer interval `[i k - N, i k + N]`, then `Set.Finite.pi` + `Set.finite_Icc`. The proper-map property `Tendsto (fun j => latticeDistance d i j) Filter.cofinite Filter.atTop` follows by reducing `Filter.eventually_cofinite` to finiteness of `{j | latticeDistance d i j < N}` for every `N`, a subset of the ball at radius `N`. No `d ≥ 1` hypothesis (for `d = 0` the domain `Fin 0 → ℤ` is a singleton, `Filter.cofinite` collapses to `⊥`, and the Tendsto holds vacuously). Combined with PR #779's conditional cofinite cluster decay this is the upgrade pathway to a distance-based "`U_2 → 0` as `d_latt(i,j) → ∞`" statement. (PR #782.) |
| §17.1 | Theorem 17.1.1 / Corollary 17.1.2 (pp. 304–306) — scope determination | **Out of scope (literal); lattice analog in-scope and DONE** | The literal theorems concern Schwinger-function monotonicity in continuum `μ, −σ` parameters, requiring Hamiltonian / Osterwalder–Schrader machinery not built here. The **underlying mechanism** (GKS-II-derivative monotonicity) has an in-scope lattice-Ising analog, formalized and axiom-free: magnetization monotone in `β, J` via `spontaneousMagnetization_monotone_beta` / `spontaneousMagnetization_monotone_J` (`AmbientLattice/SpontaneousMono.lean`), and mass antitone in `β, J` via `latticeMass_antitone_beta` / `latticeMass_antitone_J` (`Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/LatticeMassHighTemperature/PosAndAntitone.lean`). These correctly cite §17.1 pp. 304–306 as the physical motivation (GKS-II mechanism) and are captioned as lattice analogs. |
| §17.1 | 1D Ising transfer matrix — eigenpairs, trace, determinant, tanh ratio (Issue #3511 PR 1) | **Done (definition + 11 lemmas)** | `isingTransferMatrix1D (a : ℝ) : Matrix (Fin 2) (Fin 2) ℝ := Matrix.of (fun i j => Real.exp (a · spin1D i · spin1D j))` (`TransferMatrix/OneDim.lean`), the zero-field 1D Ising transfer matrix `T(a)ᵢⱼ = e^{a·sᵢ·sⱼ}` with `a = βJ` and spin values `spin1D = ![1, -1]`, explicitly `T(a) = !![eᵃ, e⁻ᵃ; e⁻ᵃ, eᵃ]` — the concrete instance of the Glimm–Jaffe §17.1 transfer-matrix method. Entry lemmas (`isingTransferMatrix1D_{zero_zero,zero_one,one_zero,one_one}`), symmetry `Tᵀ = T` (`isingTransferMatrix1D_transpose`), entrywise positivity (`isingTransferMatrix1D_pos`). Eigenvalues `transferEigenvalueTop a = eᵃ + e⁻ᵃ = 2 cosh a` (`transferEigenvalueTop_eq`, `transferEigenvalueTop_pos`) and `transferEigenvalueBot a = eᵃ − e⁻ᵃ = 2 sinh a` (`transferEigenvalueBot_eq`); trace `= 2eᵃ = λ₊ + λ₋` (`trace_isingTransferMatrix1D`) and determinant `= e²ᵃ − e⁻²ᵃ = λ₊·λ₋` (`det_isingTransferMatrix1D`). Hadamard-basis eigenpairs `T·(1,1) = λ₊·(1,1)` (`isingTransferMatrix1D_mulVec_hadamardTop`) and `T·(1,−1) = λ₋·(1,−1)` (`isingTransferMatrix1D_mulVec_hadamardBot`). Spectral ratio `λ₋/λ₊ = tanh a` (`transferEigenvalue_ratio`) — the source of the ferromagnetic 1D correlation decay rate `−log tanh a` (`a = βJ > 0`; GJ §17.5). First PR of the transfer-matrix formalization programme (Issue #3511); next: cyclic-chain partition function as a trace `Z_N = Tr(Tᴺ)`. |
| §17.1 | 1D transfer matrix diagonalization — `Tr(Tᴺ) = λ₊ᴺ + λ₋ᴺ` (Issue #3511 PR 2) | **Done (definition + 13 lemmas)** | `TransferMatrix/OneDimPower.lean`. Hadamard matrix `hadamardMatrix = !![1, 1; 1, −1]` with `H·H = 2·1` (`hadamardMatrix_mul_self`), `det H = −2` (`hadamardMatrix_det`, unit `hadamardMatrix_isUnit_det`), inverse `H⁻¹ = (1/2)·H` (`hadamardMatrix_inv`). Eigenvalue diagonal `transferDiagonal a = diagonal ![λ₊, λ₋]` (entry simp lemmas `transferDiagonal_{zero_zero,one_one,zero_one,one_zero}`). The inverse-free **Hadamard intertwining** `T(a)·H = H·D(a)` (`isingTransferMatrix1D_mul_hadamard`) powers by induction to `T(a)ᴺ·H = H·D(a)ᴺ` (`isingTransferMatrix1D_pow_mul_hadamard`), giving the diagonalization `T(a)ᴺ = H·D(a)ᴺ·H⁻¹` (`isingTransferMatrix1D_pow_eq_conj`) with `D(a)ᴺ = diagonal ![λ₊ᴺ, λ₋ᴺ]` (`transferDiagonal_pow`). Hence the **cyclic-chain partition function** `Tr(T(a)ᴺ) = λ₊ᴺ + λ₋ᴺ` (`trace_isingTransferMatrix1D_pow`, via `trace_mul_comm` + `H⁻¹·H = 1`) and `det(T(a)ᴺ) = (λ₊·λ₋)ᴺ` (`det_isingTransferMatrix1D_pow`). The trace `Z_N = λ₊ᴺ + λ₋ᴺ` is (for physical `N ≥ 1`) the partition function of the `N`-site cyclic Ising chain at zero field; the identity is a pure algebraic fact valid for all `N : ℕ` (GJ §17.1). Issue #3511 PR 2; next: the configuration-sum = trace identity `∑_σ ∏ exp(βJ σᵢσᵢ₊₁) = Tr(Tᴺ)` and the exact two-point function `⟨σ₀σₙ⟩ = (tanh βJ)ⁿ`. |
| §17.1 | 1D Ising log-partition (free-energy) density `log(2 cosh βJ)` from transfer matrix (Issue #3511 PR 3) | **Done (8 lemmas)** | `TransferMatrix/OneDimFreeEnergy.lean`. Builds on the trace formula `Tr(Tᴺ) = λ₊ᴺ + λ₋ᴺ` (#3513) to derive the per-site log-partition (dimensionless free-energy) density `(1/N)·log Z_N` of the 1D Ising chain. Spectral-gap inputs for `a = βJ > 0`: `transferEigenvalueBot_pos` (`λ₋ = 2 sinh a > 0`), `transferEigenvalueBot_lt_top` (`λ₋ < λ₊`, gap `2e⁻ᵃ`, all `a`), `transferEigenvalue_ratio_pos`/`transferEigenvalue_ratio_lt_one` (`λ₋/λ₊ = tanh a ∈ (0,1)`), and `tendsto_transferEigenvalue_ratio_pow` (`(λ₋/λ₊)ᴺ → 0`). Main limit `tendsto_log_eigenvalueSum_div_nat`: `(1/N)·log(λ₊ᴺ + λ₋ᴺ) → log λ₊` (writing `λ₊ᴺ + λ₋ᴺ = λ₊ᴺ(1 + tanhᴺ a)`, the subdominant eigenvalue gives the vanishing correction `(1/N)·log(1 + tanhᴺ a) → 0`). Trace corollary `tendsto_log_trace_pow_div_nat`: `(1/N)·log Tr(Tᴺ) → log λ₊`; since `Z_N = Tr(Tᴺ)` (cyclic chain), this is the 1D Ising log-partition density (free energy `f = −β⁻¹·log(2 cosh βJ)`). Closed form `log_transferEigenvalueTop_eq`: `log λ₊ = log(2 cosh a)`. The transfer-matrix solution of the 1D Ising free energy `f = −β⁻¹ log(2 cosh βJ)` (GJ §17.1). Issue #3511 PR 3. |
| §17.1 | Closed-walk trace identity `Tr(Mᴺ)=∑ paths` for matrix powers (Issue #3511 PR 4) | **Done (6 declarations)** | `TransferMatrix/TraceSum.lean`. The combinatorial core of the transfer-matrix partition function `Z_N = Tr(Tᴺ)`, for any finite matrix `M : Matrix ι ι R` over a commutative semiring (Mathlib has no such lemma; only `adjMatrix_pow_apply_eq_card_walk` for 0/1 matrices). `pathWeight M σ := ∏_{i:Fin n} M (σ i.castSucc) (σ i.succ)` (open-path edge product) with `pathWeight_snoc` (`pathWeight (snoc σ x) = pathWeight σ · M (σ last) x`). **Open-path entry formula** `pow_apply_eq_sum`: `(Mⁿ) a b = ∑_{σ:Fin(n+1)→ι} [σ 0 = a ∧ σ (last n) = b] · pathWeight M σ`, by induction peeling the last edge via `Fin.snocEquiv`. **Closed-walk trace identity** `trace_pow_eq_sum`: `Tr(Mⁿ) = ∑_{σ:Fin(n+1)→ι} [σ 0 = σ (last n)] · pathWeight M σ` (sum over vertex sequences returning to their start), from `pow_apply_eq_sum` on the diagonal. Cyclic vocabulary `closedWalkWeight M σ := ∏_{i:Fin n} M (σ i) (σ (i+1))` (cyclic `Fin n`, `NeZero n`) with the factorization `closedWalkWeight_succ`: `closedWalkWeight τ = pathWeight τ · M (τ last) (τ 0)`. Underlies the cyclic 1D Ising `Z_N = Tr(Tᴺ)` (GJ §17.1). Issue #3511 PR 4; next: `partitionFunction (cycleGraph N) = Tr(Tᴺ) = λ₊ᴺ + λ₋ᴺ` and `⟨σ₀σₙ⟩ = (tanh βJ)ⁿ`. |
| §17.1 | Cyclic trace identity `Tr(Tᴺ)=∑ closed walks` and 1D Ising `Z_N=λ₊ᴺ+λ₋ᴺ` (Issue #3511 PR 5) | **Done (5 declarations)** | `TransferMatrix/{TraceSum,TraceSum1D}.lean`. Completes the closed-walk identity (#3515) into its purely cyclic form. `pathWeight_eq_closedWalkWeight_init`: for a vertex sequence returning to its start (`σ 0 = σ (last (m+1))`), `pathWeight M σ = closedWalkWeight M (init σ)`. **Cyclic trace identity** `trace_pow_eq_sum_cycle`: `Tr(M^(m+1)) = ∑_{τ:Fin(m+1)→ι} closedWalkWeight M τ`, reindexing the open-return form `trace_pow_eq_sum` by the `init`/`snoc` bijection (`Finset.sum_bij'`) between vertex sequences returning to their start and closed walks. 1D Ising specialization (`TraceSum1D.lean`): `trace_isingTransferMatrix1D_pow_eq_sum_cycle` (`Tr(T(a)^(m+1)) = ∑_{σ:Fin(m+1)→Fin 2} closedWalkWeight T(a) σ`) and the headline `sum_closedWalkWeight_isingTransferMatrix1D`: `∑_{σ:Fin(m+1)→Fin 2} closedWalkWeight T(a) σ = λ₊^(m+1) + λ₋^(m+1)` — the transfer-matrix evaluation `Z_N = Tr(Tᴺ) = λ₊ᴺ + λ₋ᴺ` of the cyclic N-site zero-field Ising chain (GJ §17.1). Issue #3511 PR 5; next: connecting `closedWalkWeight` to the project's `partitionFunction (cycleGraph N)` Hamiltonian form, and `⟨σ₀σₙ⟩ = (tanh βJ)ⁿ`. |
| §17.1 | 1D Ising two-point function (transfer-matrix ratio) `→ (tanh βJ)ⁿ` (Issue #3511 PR 6) | **Done (14 declarations)** | `TransferMatrix/OneDimTwoPoint.lean`. The transfer-matrix expression for the two-point function, `Tr(S·Tⁿ·S·T^{N-n})/Tr(Tᴺ)` (its identification with the Gibbs `⟨σ₀σₙ⟩` via the cyclic config sum is a later PR), with spin operator `spinOperator = diag(1,−1)` and `swapMatrix P = !![0,1;1,0]`. **Key**: the spin operator swaps the Hadamard eigenvectors, `spinOperator_mul_hadamard` (`S·H = H·P`), so `hadamardInv_mul_spinOperator_mul_hadamard` (`H⁻¹·S·H = P`) and `swapMatrix_mul_transferDiagonal_pow_mul_swapMatrix` (`P·D(a)ᵏ·P = diagonal ![λ₋ᵏ, λ₊ᵏ]`). **Two-point trace** `twoPointTrace`: `Tr(S·Tⁿ·S·Tᵐ) = λ₋ⁿ·λ₊ᵐ + λ₊ⁿ·λ₋ᵐ` (via `spinOperator_mul_pow`, `S·Tⁿ = H·P·D(a)ⁿ·H⁻¹`). Closed forms `twoPointCorrelation_eq` (`= (λ₋ⁿλ₊^{N-n}+λ₊ⁿλ₋^{N-n})/(λ₊ᴺ+λ₋ᴺ)`) and `twoPointCorrelation_eq_ratio` (`= (rⁿ+r^{N-n})/(1+rᴺ)`, `r = λ₋/λ₊`). **Headline** `tendsto_twoPointCorrelation`: for `a = βJ > 0`, `⟨σ₀σₙ⟩_N → (tanh βJ)ⁿ` as `N → ∞` — the exact exponential decay of the 1D Ising correlation with rate `−log tanh βJ` (GJ §17.1). Issue #3511 PR 6; next: identifying `Tr(S·Tⁿ·S·T^{N-n})` with the project's `gibbsExpectation`/`cycleGraph` two-point function. |
| §17.1 | Cycle-graph edge enumeration and partition function as a site product (Issue #3511 PR 7) | **Done (6 declarations)** | `TransferMatrix/CycleGraphLink.lean`. The combinatorial bridge from the project's graph-based Ising partition function on the cyclic chain to the transfer-matrix's linear product over sites. **Edge enumeration** (Mathlib gap — only adjacency `cycleGraph_adj` is provided): `cycleGraph_edgeFinset_eq_image` — `(cycleGraph (n+2)).edgeFinset = image (fun i => s(i, i+1)) univ`, the `N` cyclic nearest-neighbour pairs (proved via `Sym2.ind` + `cycleGraph_adj` + `Sym2.eq_iff`). `cyclePair_injective` (`i ↦ s(i, i+1)` injective on `Fin (n+3)`, a cycle on `≥3` vertices has no repeated edges) gives `prod_cycleGraph_edgeFinset` (`∏_{e ∈ edgeFinset} f e = ∏_{i:Fin(n+3)} f s(i,i+1)`) and `card_cycleGraph_edgeFinset` (`= n+3`). With the `h=0` Boltzmann edge product `boltzmannWeight_eq_prod_exp_of_h_zero` (`boltzmannWeight G ⟨J,0,β⟩ σ = ∏_{e} exp(βJ·edgeSpin σ e)`, general graph), this yields `partitionFunction_cycleGraph_eq_sum_prod`: `Z_N = ∑_σ ∏_{i:Fin N} exp(βJ·σᵢσᵢ₊₁)` for the cyclic chain `N = n+3` — the partition function as a linear site product, the transfer-matrix shape (GJ §17.1). Issue #3511 PR 7; next: the spin↔`Fin 2` encoding identifying this with `∑ closedWalkWeight T(βJ) = λ₊ᴺ + λ₋ᴺ`. |
| §17.1 | **Cyclic Ising partition function `Z_N = λ₊ᴺ + λ₋ᴺ`** via spin↔`Fin 2` encoding (Issue #3511 PR 8, capstone) | **Done (5 declarations)** | `TransferMatrix/CycleGraphZ.lean`. Capstone identifying the **Gibbs** partition function with the transfer-matrix eigenvalues. `spinEquivFin2 : Spin ≃ Fin 2` (`up↦0`, `down↦1`) with `spin1D_spinEquivFin2` (`spin1D (encode s) = Spin.sign ℝ s`) and `isingTransferMatrix1D_spinEquivFin2` (`T(βJ)(encode s)(encode t) = exp(βJ·sign s·sign t)`, the Boltzmann edge factor). `prod_exp_edgeSpin_eq_closedWalkWeight`: the site product equals a closed-walk weight of the encoded config, `∏_i exp(βJ·edgeSpin σ s(i,i+1)) = closedWalkWeight T(βJ) (encode∘σ)`. **Headline** `partitionFunction_cycleGraph_eq_eigenvaluePow`: `partitionFunction (cycleGraph N) ⟨J,0,β⟩ = λ₊(βJ)ᴺ + λ₋(βJ)ᴺ = (2cosh βJ)ᴺ + (2sinh βJ)ᴺ` (`N = n+3`) — the classic transfer-matrix evaluation of the cyclic N-site zero-field Ising partition function, tying the site-product form (`partitionFunction_cycleGraph_eq_sum_prod`, #3518) to the closed-walk sum (`sum_closedWalkWeight_isingTransferMatrix1D`, #3516) via the config reindex `(Fin N → Spin) ≃ (Fin N → Fin 2)`. Completes the transfer-matrix evaluation of Z_N (GJ §17.1). Issue #3511 PR 8. |
| §17.1 / §17.5 | 1D Ising correlation length and mass `m = −log tanh βJ` (Issue #3511) | **Done (7 declarations)** | `TransferMatrix/OneDimCorrelationLength.lean`. Building on the two-point decay `⟨σ₀σₙ⟩ → (tanh βJ)ⁿ` (#3517): the **mass** `correlationMass a = −log tanh a` (inverse correlation length, the §17.5 mass) and **correlation length** `correlationLength a = 1/m`. `correlationMass_pos`/`correlationLength_pos` (`> 0` for `a = βJ > 0`, since `tanh a ∈ (0,1)` ⟹ `log tanh a < 0`), `correlationMass_eq_inv_length` (`m = 1/ξ`). `tanh_pow_eq_exp_neg_mass`: `(tanh a)ⁿ = exp(−m·n)`. Headline `tendsto_twoPointCorrelation_exp_neg_mass`: `⟨σ₀σₙ⟩_N → exp(−m·n)` as `N → ∞` — exponential decay at the inverse correlation length (GJ §17.1/§17.5). |
| §17.1 | 1D Ising susceptibility `χ = (1+tanh βJ)/(1-tanh βJ)` (Issue #3511) | **Done (5 declarations)** | `TransferMatrix/OneDimSusceptibility.lean`. Summing the two-point decay rate `(tanh βJ)ⁿ`: `summable_tanh_pow` and `tsum_tanh_pow` (`∑ₙ (tanh a)ⁿ = (1-tanh a)⁻¹`, `tanh a ∈ [0,1)`). The two-sided lattice **susceptibility** `isingSusceptibility1D a = (1+tanh a)/(1-tanh a)` with `isingSusceptibility1D_eq_two_tsum_sub_one` (`χ = 2·∑ₙ(tanh a)ⁿ − 1`, the diagonal counted once) and `isingSusceptibility1D_pos` (`> 0` for `βJ > 0`, finite, diverging only as `βJ → ∞` — no finite-temperature phase transition in 1D) (GJ §17.1). |
| §17.1 | Cyclic Ising **Gibbs** free-energy density `log(2cosh βJ)` (Issue #3511) | **Done (2 declarations)** | `TransferMatrix/OneDimGibbsFreeEnergy.lean`. Combines the eigenvalue free-energy limit (#3514) with the Gibbs `Z_N=λ₊ᴺ+λ₋ᴺ` identity (#3519) to give the free-energy density of the project's **Gibbs** partition function on the cyclic chain. `tendsto_log_partitionFunction_cycleGraph_div_nat`: `(1/N)·log partitionFunction(cycleGraph N)⟨J,0,β⟩ → log(2cosh βJ)` as `N→∞` (over `N=n+3`, composing `tendsto_log_eigenvalueSum_div_nat` with `n↦n+3`). `tendsto_gibbs_freeEnergy_density`: the physical Helmholtz density `-(βN)⁻¹·log Z_N → -β⁻¹·log(2cosh βJ)`. The Gibbs (not merely transfer-trace) 1D Ising free-energy density (GJ §17.1). |
| §17.1 | Trace with diagonal insertions `Tr(D·Mᵃ·D·Mᵇ)` — two-point trace foundation (Issue #3511) | **Done (3 declarations)** | `TransferMatrix/MarkedTrace.lean`. Foundation for the Gibbs two-point function `⟨σ₀σₙ⟩ = Tr(S·Tⁿ·S·T^(N-n))/Tr(Tᴺ)`. `trace_diagonal_pow_diagonal_pow_expand`: `Tr(D·Mᵃ·D·Mᵇ) = ∑_{i,j} dᵢ·dⱼ·(Mᵃ)ᵢⱼ·(Mᵇ)ⱼᵢ` (D = diagonal d). `trace_diagonal_mul_pow`: single insertion `Tr(D·Mᵃ) = ∑_i dᵢ·(Mᵃ)ᵢᵢ`. `trace_diagonal_pow_diagonal_pow_comm`: cyclic symmetry `Tr(D·Mᵃ·D·Mᵇ) = Tr(D·Mᵇ·D·Mᵃ)`. The next step is the marked closed-walk form `= ∑_τ d(τ₀)·d(τₐ)·closedWalkWeight M τ` (gluing two open paths via `pow_apply_eq_sum`) toward the Gibbs two-point identification (GJ §17.1). |
| §17.1 | Cyclic→open closed-walk conversion + marked closed-walk weight (Issue #3511) | **Done (3 declarations)** | `TransferMatrix/MarkedTraceClosedWalk.lean`. The technical key (codex-designed) for the marked closed-walk trace identity, avoiding cyclic `Fin`-arithmetic. `closedWalkWeight_eq_pathWeight_snoc_zero`: `closedWalkWeight M τ = pathWeight M (Fin.snoc τ (τ 0))` — appending the first vertex turns the cyclic closed walk into an open path returning to its start, converting the cyclic `+1` product into the boundary-free open-path product (proved from `pathWeight_eq_closedWalkWeight_init`). `markedClosedWalkWeight M d hb τ = d(τ 0)·d(τ a)·closedWalkWeight M τ` (the summand of the marked trace) with `markedClosedWalkWeight_eq` giving its open-path form. The remaining steps (path append `pathWeight (append (init σ) ρ) = pathWeight σ·pathWeight ρ` and the path-pair bijection) are the next PRs (GJ §17.1). |
| §17.1 | Path gluing `pathWeight (append (init σ) ρ) = pathWeight σ·pathWeight ρ` + marked glue (Issue #3511) | **Done (4 declarations)** | `TransferMatrix/MarkedTraceClosedWalk.lean`. `pathWeight_append`: gluing open paths `σ : Fin(a+1)→ι`, `ρ : Fin(b+1)→ι` at a shared vertex (`ρ 0 = σ (last a)`) multiplies path weights, `pathWeight M (Fin.append (init σ) ρ) = pathWeight M σ · pathWeight M ρ` (by induction on `b`, peeling `ρ` via `Fin.append_snoc` + `pathWeight_snoc`). `markedGlue σ ρ = Fin.append (init σ) (init ρ) : Fin(a+b)→ι` (the closed walk gluing two paths) with mark-position lemmas `markedGlue_apply_zero` (`= σ 0`) and `markedGlue_apply_a` (`= ρ 0` at site `a`). The remaining `closedWalkWeight_markedGlue` (`= pathWeight σ·pathWeight ρ`, via the snoc helper) and the path-pair bijection to `Tr(D·Mᵃ·D·Mᵇ) = ∑_τ markedClosedWalkWeight` are the next PRs (GJ §17.1). |
| §17.1 | Closed-walk weight of the marked glue `= pathWeight σ·pathWeight ρ` (Issue #3511) | **Done (2 declarations)** | `TransferMatrix/MarkedTraceClosedWalk.lean`. `closedWalkWeight_eq_pathWeight_snoc_zero'` (NeZero-indexed form of the cyclic→open conversion). `closedWalkWeight_markedGlue`: under the gluing conditions `ρ 0 = σ (last a)` and `ρ (last b) = σ 0`, the cyclic closed-walk weight of the glued walk factors as `closedWalkWeight M (markedGlue σ ρ) = pathWeight M σ · pathWeight M ρ` — converting to open-path form, recognising `Fin.snoc (markedGlue σ ρ) (σ 0) = Fin.append (init σ) ρ` (via `append_snoc` + `ρ (last b) = σ 0`), and applying `pathWeight_append`. The remaining path-pair bijection to `Tr(D·Mᵃ·D·Mᵇ) = ∑_τ markedClosedWalkWeight` is the next PR (GJ §17.1). |
| §17.1 | Marked trace as a sum over glued path pairs (Issue #3511) | **Done (2 declarations)** | `TransferMatrix/MarkedTraceClosedWalk.lean`. `sum_reorder_4`: reorders four nested `Finset.univ` sums `∑_i ∑_j ∑_σ ∑_ρ F = ∑_σ ∑_ρ ∑_i ∑_j F` (a `calc` chain of `Finset.sum_comm` — the robust idiom avoiding HO-unification/metavar issues). `trace_diagonal_pow_diagonal_pow_eq_sum_glued`: the open glued-pair form `Tr(D·Mᵃ·D·Mᵇ) = ∑_σ ∑_ρ [σ(last a)=ρ0 ∧ ρ(last b)=σ0] d(σ0)·d(ρ0)·pathWeight σ·pathWeight ρ` (from the expand + two `pow_apply_eq_sum`, `sum_reorder_4`, and collapsing the endpoint sums via `Finset.sum_eq_single`). The remaining path-pair bijection to `∑_τ markedClosedWalkWeight` (via `markedGlue` + `closedWalkWeight_markedGlue`) is the next PR (GJ §17.1). |
| §17.1 | **Marked closed-walk trace identity** `Tr(D·Mᵃ·D·Mᵇ) = ∑_τ markedClosedWalkWeight` (Issue #3511) | **Done (4 declarations)** | `TransferMatrix/MarkedTraceClosedWalk.lean`. Completes the marked closed-walk trace identity for two-point functions. Reconstruction maps `σOf τ = snoc (τ∘castAdd b) (τ a)` and `ρOf τ = snoc (τ∘natAdd a) (τ 0)` with `markedGlue_σOf_ρOf` (`markedGlue (σOf τ) (ρOf τ) = τ`, via `Fin.append_castAdd_natAdd`). **Headline** `trace_diagonal_pow_diagonal_pow_eq_sum_markedClosedWalk`: `Tr(D·Mᵃ·D·Mᵇ) = ∑_{τ:Fin(a+b)→ι} d(τ 0)·d(τ a)·closedWalkWeight M τ` (a sum over closed walks of length `a+b` with the diagonal marks at sites `0` and `a`), proved from the glued-pair form by the `Finset.sum_bij'` bijection between closed walks and glued path pairs (weight match via `closedWalkWeight_markedGlue`). The combinatorial core of the Gibbs two-point function `⟨σ₀σₙ⟩ = Tr(S·Tⁿ·S·T^(N-n))/Tr(Tᴺ)`; next: the spin↔`Fin 2` encoding connecting it to the project's `gibbsExpectation`/`twoPointCorrelation` (GJ §17.1). |
| §17.1 | 1D marked closed-walk sum = λ₋ⁿλ₊ᵐ+λ₊ⁿλ₋ᵐ (Issue #3511) | **Done (2 declarations)** | `TransferMatrix/MarkedTrace1D.lean`. `spinOperator_eq_diagonal_spin1D` (`spinOperator = Matrix.diagonal spin1D`). `sum_markedClosedWalkWeight_isingTransferMatrix1D`: the closed-walk form of the two-point numerator equals the eigenvalue powers, `∑_{τ:Fin(n+m)→Fin 2} markedClosedWalkWeight T(a) spin1D τ = λ₋ⁿ·λ₊ᵐ + λ₊ⁿ·λ₋ᵐ` (= `Tr(S·Tⁿ·S·Tᵐ)`, combining the marked closed-walk trace identity #3528 with `twoPointTrace` #3517). The closed-walk form of the transfer-matrix two-point numerator, bridging toward the Gibbs two-point function (GJ §17.1). |
| §17.1 | **Gibbs two-point function = transfer-matrix ratio** `correlation (cycleGraph N) {0,n} = twoPointCorrelation βJ n N` (Issue #3511) | **Done (3 declarations)** | `TransferMatrix/GibbsTwoPoint.lean`. Identifies the project's **Gibbs** `correlation` of the two endpoint spins on the cyclic 1D Ising chain with the transfer-matrix two-point ratio, completing the chain `correlation → site-marked closed-walk sum → Tr(S·Tⁿ·S·Tᵐ) → eigenvalues`. `twoPointNumerator_cycleGraph_eq_sum_siteMarked`: the Gibbs two-point numerator `∑_σ spinProduct {0,n} σ · boltzmannWeight (cycleGraph N) ⟨J,0,β⟩ σ = ∑_{τ:Fin N→Fin 2} spin1D(τ 0)·spin1D(τ n)·closedWalkWeight T(βJ) τ`, via the same spin↔`Fin 2` encoding as `partitionFunction_cycleGraph_eq_eigenvaluePow` (#3519) with the two spin insertions `σ₀·σ_n` becoming the marks `spin1D(τ 0)·spin1D(τ n)`. `sum_siteMarkedClosedWalkWeight_isingTransferMatrix1D`: evaluates that marked sum to `λ₋ⁿ·λ₊ᵐ + λ₊ⁿ·λ₋ᵐ` (`N = n+m`, `0 < n`, `0 < m`), unfolding the marked closed-walk weight in `sum_markedClosedWalkWeight_isingTransferMatrix1D` (#3529) to its insertion-site product form. **Headline** `correlation_cycleGraph_eq_twoPointCorrelation`: `correlation (cycleGraph N) ⟨J,0,β⟩ {0,n} = twoPointCorrelation (βJ) n N` (`0 < n < N`, `N = k+3`), dividing the marked-sum eigenvalue numerator by the partition function `λ₊ᴺ + λ₋ᴺ` (`partitionFunction_cycleGraph_eq_eigenvaluePow`) and matching `twoPointCorrelation_eq`. This turns the abstract transfer-matrix two-point analysis (`twoPointCorrelation_eq`, geometric decay `⟨σ₀σₙ⟩ → (tanh βJ)ⁿ`, #3517) into a statement about the project's Gibbs correlation `⟨σ₀σₙ⟩`. The standard cyclic transfer-matrix computation of the 1D Ising two-point function, giving the Gibbs reading of the GJ §17.1 mass/exponential-decay discussion. Issue #3511. |
| §17.1 | **Gibbs-form exponential decay** of the 1D Ising two-point function `⟨σ₀σₙ⟩ → (tanh βJ)ⁿ` (Issue #3511) | **Done (5 declarations)** | `TransferMatrix/GibbsTwoPointDecay.lean`. Lifts the transfer-matrix two-point analysis to the project's **Gibbs** `correlation` on the cyclic chain, via the bridge `correlation_cycleGraph_eq_twoPointCorrelation` (#3530). `correlation_cycleGraph_eq_eigenvaluePow`: closed eigenvalue form `correlation (cycleGraph N) ⟨J,0,β⟩ {0,n} = (λ₋ⁿλ₊^{N-n}+λ₊ⁿλ₋^{N-n})/(λ₊ᴺ+λ₋ᴺ)` (`0 < n < N`). `correlation_cycleGraph_eq_ratio`: `tanh` ratio form `(rⁿ+r^{N-n})/(1+rᴺ)` (`r = λ₋/λ₊ = tanh βJ`). `tendsto_nat_add_offset_atTop`: the volume `N = n+k+3 → ∞` as `k → ∞`. **Headline** `tendsto_correlation_cycleGraph`: for `βJ > 0` and fixed endpoint distance `0 < n`, `correlation (cycleGraph (n+k+3)) ⟨J,0,β⟩ {0,n} → (tanh βJ)ⁿ` as `k → ∞` (parametrising `N = n+k+3` keeps the sites `0`, `n` valid for all `k` while the endpoint distance `n` stays fixed and the volume diverges). `tendsto_correlation_cycleGraph_exp_neg_mass`: the same Gibbs limit as the pure exponential `exp(-m·n)` at the inverse correlation length `m = correlationMass βJ = -log tanh βJ`. The exact exponential decay of the 1D Ising correlation with rate `-log tanh βJ`, stated for the project's Gibbs correlation `⟨σ₀σₙ⟩` (the Gibbs reading of GJ §17.1). Issue #3511. |
| §17.1 | **Exact open 1D chain two-point function** `⟨σ₀σₙ⟩ = (tanh βJ)ⁿ` (Issue #3532) | **Done (12 declarations)** | `TransferMatrix/PathGraphEdges.lean` + `TransferMatrix/PathGraphTwoPoint.lean`. First PR toward the infinite-volume 1D two-point function `twoPointFunction 1 = (tanh βJ)^dist` (Issue #3532, Part of #3511). **Edge enumeration** (`PathGraphEdges.lean`): Mathlib provides only `pathGraph_adj`; `pathGraph_edgeFinset_eq_image` enumerates the `n` edges of `pathGraph (n+1)` as the consecutive pairs `s(i.castSucc, i.succ)` (`i : Fin n`), with `pathPair_injective`, `card_pathGraph_edgeFinset = n`, `prod_pathGraph_edgeFinset`, and decidable-adjacency / finite-edge-set instances. **Exact two-point** (`PathGraphTwoPoint.lean`): via the FV (3.46) high-temperature closed form `correlation_high_temp_expansion_h_zero_closed`. The open path is a tree, so the even-degree subgraph denominator collapses to `{∅}` (`even_subgraph_eq_empty`, sum `1`) and the `{0,n}`-odd-boundary numerator to the single full edge set (`endpoint_subgraph_eq_edgeFinset`, sum `tanhⁿ`); both proved by an index-set degree formula (`deg_eq_of_subset`, `incident_filter_card`) and a minimal-index/`sdiff`-complement counterexample argument. **Headline** `correlation_pathGraph_eq_tanh_pow` (for `0 < n`, so the endpoints `0`, `Fin.last n` are distinct): `correlation (pathGraph (n+1)) ⟨J,0,β⟩ {0, Fin.last n} = (tanh βJ)ⁿ`. Unlike the cyclic chain there is **no boundary correction**, making this the clean finite-volume input to the infinite-volume limit (GJ §17.1). Issue #3532. |
| §17.1 | **General-pair open 1D chain two-point function** `⟨σₐσᵦ⟩ = (tanh βJ)^\|a-b\|` (Issue #3532) | **Done (8 declarations)** | `TransferMatrix/PathGraphPairTwoPoint.lean`. Generalises the endpoint open-chain two-point (#3533) to an **arbitrary distinct pair** `{a, b}` of `pathGraph (m+1)` vertices — needed for the infinite-volume lift (#3532 PR 3) since the box endpoints `{0, r} ⊆ ℤ` map to *interior* path vertices under the box≅path relabelling. **Segment index set** `segmentIdx m a b = {i : Fin m \| a ≤ i.val < b}` (the edges of the path connecting `a` to `b`) with `card_segmentIdx = b − a`. **Segment characterisation** `idx_eq_segmentIdx`: an edge subset `X` with odd degree exactly at `{a,b}` (`a.val < b.val`) has index set the segment, proved by the discrete-derivative parity recurrence `deg(vertex k) = [edge (k−1) ∈ S] + [edge k ∈ S]` via strong induction on `k`. **Segment parity** `segment_parity`: the segment subgraph has degree `1` at the endpoints, `2` in the interior, `0` outside. Applying the FV (3.46) closed form `correlation_high_temp_expansion_h_zero_closed` (even denominator `{∅}`, `{a,b}`-odd numerator the single segment of cardinality `b−a`): `correlation_pathGraph_pair_eq_tanh_pow_of_lt` (`a.val < b.val`: `= (tanh βJ)^(b.val − a.val)`) and the **headline** `correlation_pathGraph_pair_eq_tanh_pow` (`a ≠ b`: `= (tanh βJ)^\|a.val − b.val\|`, both orders) — the exact geometric decay in the path distance between the two sites (GJ §17.1). Issue #3532. |
| §17.1 | **Infinite-volume 1D Ising two-point function** `twoPointFunction 1 = (tanh βJ)^dist` (Issue #3532, CAPSTONE) | **Done (13 declarations)** | `TransferMatrix/InfiniteVolumeOneD.lean`. **Capstone of the infinite-volume 1D programme** (Issue #3532) — the project's most-important long-term goal realised in one dimension: the project's infinite-volume Gibbs two-point function equals the exact geometric decay `twoPointFunction 1 ⟨J,0,β⟩ r = (tanh βJ)^(latticeDistance 1 0 r)` for `r ≠ 0` (ferromagnetic `0 ≤ J`, `0 < β`). **Box ≅ path isomorphism**: `boxEquiv : Fin (2N+1) ≃ ↑(cubicBox 1 N)` (the relabelling `k ↦ k − N`) and `pathGraph_map_boxEquiv`: `(pathGraph (2N+1)).map boxEquiv = inducedGraph (latticeGraph 1) (cubicBox 1 N)` (adjacency on both sides reduces to `\|u₀ − v₀\| = 1`). **Finite-volume reduction** `correlationAlongExhaustion_pair_eq`: at stage `N ≥ \|r₀\|` the correlation along the cubic exhaustion equals `(tanh βJ)^dist`, by transporting the open-chain pair two-point (#3534) along the isomorphism via `correlation_map_equiv` (the lattice pair `{0,r}` lifts to the `boxEquiv`-image of the path pair `{N, r₀+N}`). **Headline** `twoPointFunction_one_eq_tanh_pow`: the sequence is eventually constant in `N`, so the thermodynamic-limit supremum `correlationInfinite` collapses to `(tanh βJ)^dist` (via `correlationAlongExhaustion_tendsto_ciSup` + `tendsto_nhds_unique`). The first genuinely infinite-volume Gibbs correlation result in the project (GJ §17.1). Issue #3532. |
| §17.1 | **Infinite-volume 1D Ising susceptibility** `∑_r ⟨σ₀σ_r⟩ = (1+tanh βJ)/(1−tanh βJ)` + mass form (Issue #3511) | **Done (5 declarations)** | `TransferMatrix/InfiniteVolumeOneDSusceptibility.lean`. Two infinite-volume 1D observables consuming the two-point capstone `twoPointFunction 1 r = (tanh βJ)^\|r\|` (#3535, for `r ≠ 0`; at `r = 0` the `Finset {0,0}={0}` collapse gives the magnetization, not `⟨σ₀²⟩=1`). **Bilateral geometric sum** `hasSum_tanh_pow_natAbs_int` / `tsum_tanh_pow_natAbs_int`: the two-sided `ℤ`-sum `∑_{n:ℤ} (tanh a)^\|n\| = isingSusceptibility1D a`, via the `ℤ = ℕ ⊔ ℕ` split (`HasSum.int_rec`: non-negative branch `∑ₖ (tanh)ᵏ`, negative branch `∑ₖ (tanh)^{k+1}`). **Infinite-volume susceptibility** `tsum_tanh_pow_latticeDistance_eq_susceptibility`: `∑_{r : Fin 1 → ℤ} (tanh βJ)^(latticeDistance 1 0 r) = isingSusceptibility1D (βJ) = (1 + tanh βJ)/(1 − tanh βJ)` (`βJ > 0`), via the `(Fin 1 → ℤ) ≃ ℤ` reduction (`Equiv.funUnique`); the summand equals `twoPointFunction 1 r` for `r ≠ 0` and `1 = ⟨σ₀²⟩` at the diagonal. **Mass form** `twoPointFunction_one_eq_exp_neg_mass`: `twoPointFunction 1 ⟨J,0,β⟩ r = exp(−m·\|r\|)` with mass `m = correlationMass (βJ) = −log tanh βJ` (the inverse correlation length), for `r ≠ 0`. The infinite-volume susceptibility and exponential clustering of the 1D Ising chain (GJ §17.1). |
| §17.1 / §17.5 | **Sharp 1D lattice mass `latticeMass = ofReal(−log tanh βJ)` + cluster property** (Issue #3511) | **Done (12 declarations)** | `TransferMatrix/InfiniteVolumeOneDMass.lean`. Identifies the abstract §17.5 **lattice mass** `latticeMass 1 (cubicExhaustion 1) ⟨J,0,β⟩` — the `ENNReal` supremum of admissible exponential-decay rates of the truncated (Ursell) two-point function — *exactly* with the exact transfer-matrix mass `correlationMass (βJ) = −log tanh βJ`, and derives the infinite-volume cluster property, all from the exact two-point identity `twoPointFunction 1 r = (tanh βJ)^\|r\| = exp(−m·\|r\|)` (#3535/#3536). At zero field the spontaneous magnetization vanishes (`magnetizationInfinite_latticeGraph_zero_at_h_zero`), so the truncated two-point function equals the full two-point function (`truncated2Infinite_one_zero_eq_twoPointFunction`); by translation invariance the truncated function at any distinct pair equals the exact geometric value `truncated2Infinite_one_eq_tanh_pow` / `truncated2Infinite_one_eq_exp_neg_mass` (`= (tanh βJ)^{dist(i,j)} = exp(−m·dist(i,j))`, helper `latticeDistance_zero_sub`). **Exponential decay** `HasExponentialDecay_one_correlationMass`: decay holds at the mass rate `m` with constant `C = 1`, giving the lower bound `latticeMass_one_ge_correlationMass` (`ofReal m ≤ latticeMass`). **No faster decay** `le_correlationMass_of_HasExponentialDecay`: any admissible rate `α` satisfies `α ≤ m`, since the exact value `exp(−m·n)` at distance `n` forces `exp((α−m)·n) ≤ C` for all `n` (impossible for `α > m` as `exp((α−m)·n) → ∞`), giving the upper bound `latticeMass_one_le_correlationMass`. **Sharp equality** `latticeMass_one_eq_correlationMass`: `latticeMass 1 (cubicExhaustion 1) ⟨J,0,β⟩ = ENNReal.ofReal (correlationMass βJ)` (`J ≥ 0`, `β > 0`, `βJ > 0`) — the §17.5 mass realised and sharp in one dimension — with the correlation-length reading `latticeMass_one_eq_ofReal_inv_correlationLength` (`= ofReal(1/ξ)`). **Cluster property** `clusterProperty_one` (positive-mass exponential decay ⟹ truncated two-point `→ 0` cofinite) and `twoPointFunction_one_tendsto_cofinite_zero` (the full two-point function `→ 0` cofinite). The 1D §17.5 mass picture closed end-to-end (GJ §17.1/§17.5). Issue #3511. |
| §17.1 | **1D Ising transfer matrix with external field `h ≠ 0` — foundations** (Issue #3538 PR 1) | **Done (definition + ~21 declarations)** | `TransferMatrix/OneDimField.lean`. Extends the zero-field 1D transfer matrix (#3512) to a **general external field**: `isingTransferMatrix1DField (a b : ℝ) : Matrix (Fin 2) (Fin 2) ℝ := Matrix.of (fun i j => exp (a·sᵢ·sⱼ + (b/2)(sᵢ+sⱼ)))` with `a = βJ`, `b = βh`, explicitly `T(a,b) = !![e^{a+b}, e^{-a}; e^{-a}, e^{a-b}]`. Entry lemmas (`isingTransferMatrix1DField_{zero_zero,zero_one,one_zero,one_one}`), symmetry `Tᵀ = T` (`isingTransferMatrix1DField_transpose`), entrywise positivity (`isingTransferMatrix1DField_pos`). **Discriminant** `fieldTransferDiscriminant a b = e^{2a} sinh²b + e^{-2a}` with `fieldTransferDiscriminant_pos` (`> 0`, real distinct eigenvalues). **Eigenvalues** `fieldTransferEigenvalue{Top,Bot} a b = eᵃ cosh b ± √D`; trace `2 eᵃ cosh b` (`trace_isingTransferMatrix1DField`) and determinant `e^{2a} − e^{-2a}` (`det_isingTransferMatrix1DField`). Unlike `h = 0`, the eigenvectors are not the fixed Hadamard pair, so the spectral data is organised via the **characteristic polynomial**: Vieta sum `λ+ + λ- = trace` (`fieldTransferEigenvalueTop_add_bot{,_eq_trace}`), product `λ+ · λ- = det` (`fieldTransferEigenvalueTop_mul_bot{,_eq_det}`, using `√D² = D` and `cosh²b = sinh²b + 1`), and the characteristic equation `λ± ² = trace·λ± − det` (`fieldTransferEigenvalue{Top,Bot}_sq`). Ordering `λ- < λ+` (`fieldTransferEigenvalueBot_lt_top`), positivity `0 < λ+` (`fieldTransferEigenvalueTop_pos`) and `0 < λ-` for `a > 0` (`fieldTransferEigenvalueBot_pos`, via `λ+ · λ- = e^{2a} − e^{-2a} > 0`). **Zero-field bridge** at `b = 0`: `isingTransferMatrix1DField_zero` (`= isingTransferMatrix1D a`), `fieldTransferDiscriminant_zero` (`= e^{-2a}`), `fieldTransferEigenvalue{Top,Bot}_zero` (`= transferEigenvalue{Top,Bot} a`). The foundational PR of the general external-field 1D transfer-matrix programme (Glimm–Jaffe §17.1); next: partition function `Z_N = λ+ᴺ + λ-ᴺ`, free energy, magnetization `m(h)`, susceptibility `∂_h m`. Issue #3538 PR 1. |
| §17.1 | **1D Ising field partition function `Z_N = λ₊ᴺ + λ₋ᴺ` + free energy** (Issue #3538 PR 2) | **Done (8 declarations)** | `TransferMatrix/OneDimFieldPower.lean`. Evaluates the cyclic-chain partition function `Z_N = Tr(T(a,b)ᴺ)` and free-energy density of the 1D Ising chain in a field (#3539). Without a Hadamard eigenbasis, the trace power is computed via the **Cayley–Hamilton recurrence**: `isingTransferMatrix1DField_sq` (`T² = (tr T)·T − (det T)·1`, via `Matrix.aeval_self_charpoly` + `Matrix.charpoly_fin_two`), `isingTransferMatrix1DField_pow_succ_succ` (`T^{N+2} = tr·T^{N+1} − det·T^N`), and the trace recurrence `trace_isingTransferMatrix1DField_pow_succ_succ` (`Tr(T^{N+2}) = tr·Tr(T^{N+1}) − det·Tr(T^N)`). **Headline** `trace_isingTransferMatrix1DField_pow`: `Tr(T(a,b)ᴺ) = λ₊ᴺ + λ₋ᴺ` for all `N` (two-step induction `Nat.twoStepInduction`; both sides obey `x_{N+2} = tr·x_{N+1} − det·x_N` with `x₀ = 2`, `x₁ = tr`, the eigenvalue side from the characteristic equation `λ±² = tr·λ± − det`). **Free energy**: `fieldTransferEigenvalue_ratio_{nonneg,lt_one}` (`λ₋/λ₊ ∈ [0,1)` for `a > 0`), `tendsto_fieldTransferEigenvalue_ratio_pow` (`(λ₋/λ₊)ᴺ → 0`), `tendsto_log_fieldEigenvalueSum_div_nat` (`(1/N) log(λ₊ᴺ+λ₋ᴺ) → log λ₊`), and the headline `tendsto_log_trace_isingTransferMatrix1DField_pow_div_nat`: `(1/N) log Tr(T(a,b)ᴺ) → log λ₊` — the free-energy density of the 1D Ising chain in a field (GJ §17.1). Issue #3538 PR 2; next: magnetization `m(h)`, susceptibility `∂_h m`. |
| §17.1 | **1D Ising field magnetization `m(h) = sinh βh / √(sinh²βh + e^{-4βJ})`** (Issue #3538 PR 3) | **Done (6 declarations)** | `TransferMatrix/OneDimFieldMagnetization.lean`. The magnetization of the 1D Ising chain in a field as the field-derivative of the free-energy density `log λ₊` (#3540). `fieldMagnetization a b = sinh b / √(sinh²b + e^{-4a})` (`a = βJ`, `b = βh`). Helper `sqrt_fieldTransferDiscriminant`: `√D = eᵃ √(sinh²b + e^{-4a})` (from `D = e^{2a}(sinh²b + e^{-4a})`, `√(e^{2a}) = eᵃ`). `hasDerivAt_fieldTransferDiscriminant` (`∂_b D = e^{2a}·2 sinh b cosh b`). **Headline** `hasDerivAt_log_fieldTransferEigenvalueTop`: `d/db [log λ₊(a,b)] = fieldMagnetization a b` — the logarithmic derivative `(∂_b λ₊)/λ₊` collapses to `sinh b/√(...)` via the discriminant factorisation (built from `HasDerivAt.sqrt`, `Real.hasDerivAt_cosh`, `HasDerivAt.log`). Physics: `fieldMagnetization_zero` (`m(a,0) = 0` — no spontaneous magnetization in 1D) and `abs_fieldMagnetization_lt_one` (`|m| < 1` strictly sub-saturated, since `√(sinh²b + e^{-4a}) > |sinh b|` as the field gap `e^{-4a} > 0`). The exact 1D Ising magnetization curve (GJ §17.1). Issue #3538 PR 3; next: susceptibility `∂_h m`. |
| §17.1 | **1D Ising field susceptibility `χ = ∂_b m = cosh βh·e^{-4βJ}/(sinh²βh+e^{-4βJ})^{3/2}`** (Issue #3538 PR 4) | **Done (5 declarations)** | `TransferMatrix/OneDimFieldSusceptibility.lean`. The susceptibility of the 1D Ising chain in a field as the field-derivative of the magnetization (#3541). `fieldSusceptibility a b = cosh b·e^{-4a}/((sinh²b+e^{-4a})·√(sinh²b+e^{-4a}))` (`a = βJ`, `b = βh`), strictly positive (`fieldSusceptibility_pos`). **Headline** `hasDerivAt_fieldMagnetization`: `d/db [fieldMagnetization a b] = fieldSusceptibility a b` — by the quotient rule (`HasDerivAt.div`) on `m = sinh b/√X` (`X = sinh²b + e^{-4a}`), the derivative collapses via `(√X)² = X`, `X − sinh²b = e^{-4a}` (substituting `e^{-4a} = q² − sinh²b`, `q = √X`, then `field_simp`). **Physical susceptibility** `hasDerivAt_fieldMagnetization_field`: `∂_h m = β·χ` (chain rule through `b = βh`). **Zero-field bridge** `fieldSusceptibility_zero`: `χ(a,0) = e^{2a}` (so `∂_h m|₀ = β e^{2a}` — the gap-enhanced zero-field susceptibility of the 1D chain). Completes the exact solution of the 1D Ising chain in a field (matrix → eigenvalues → partition function → free energy → magnetization → susceptibility); GJ §17.1. Issue #3538 PR 4 (final). |
| §17.1 | **General finite layer transfer matrix and trace identities** (Issue #3511) | **Done (finite layer abstraction)** | `TransferMatrix/LayerTransfer.lean`. Adds the finite-state layer abstraction needed before the higher-dimensional hyperplane transfer-matrix route. For a finite layer state space `Ω`, one-layer weight `u : Ω → R`, and transition weight `k : Ω → Ω → R`, `layerTransferMatrix u k : Matrix Ω Ω R` is defined by `T a b = u b * k a b`. `layerClosedWalkWeight_eq_prod` identifies the induced cyclic weight with `∏ i, u(c(i+1)) * k(c i)(c(i+1))`. **Headline** `layerTransferPartition_trace` specialises the existing closed-walk identity to layers: `layerTransferPartitionTrace u k (m+1) = ∑ c : Fin(m+1) → Ω, layerClosedWalkWeight u k c`; `layerTransferPartition_trace_eq_sum_prod` gives the product-expanded version. Marked insertion wrappers `layerTransferOnePointTrace_eq_sum`, `layerMarkedClosedWalkWeight`, `layerTransferCorrelation_matrixElement_eq_sum_markedClosedWalk`, `layerMarkedClosedWalkWeight_eq_prod`, and `layerTransferCorrelation_matrixElement_eq_sum_prod` package `Tr(D_f T^n)` and `Tr(D_f T^a D_f T^b)` for later finite-volume hyperplane two-point representations; the layer wrapper hides the redundant `NeZero (a+b)` requirement behind `hb : 0 < b`. This PR deliberately contains no spectral gap, Perron--Frobenius argument, lattice hyperplane identification, thermodynamic limit, or exponential-decay theorem; those remain later #3511 steps. Reference: Glimm--Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304--306. |
| §17.1 | **Finite cyclic layer Gibbs representation and two-point trace ratio** (Issue #3511) | **Done (abstract hyperplane bridge)** | `TransferMatrix/LayerGibbs.lean`. Adds the Gibbs-sum side of the finite hyperplane transfer-matrix abstraction. `layerCyclicGibbsWeight` is the product `∏ i, u(c(i+1)) * k(c i)(c(i+1))` for a positive-length cyclic stack `c : Fin N → Ω`; `layerCyclicPartition` sums this over stacks. **Headline** `layerCyclicPartition_eq_trace`: the cyclic layer partition sum equals `layerTransferPartitionTrace u k N`, using the positive-length reduction `N = m+1` and the trace identity from `LayerTransfer`. Positivity lemmas `layerCyclicGibbsWeight_pos` and `layerCyclicPartition_pos` package the strictly-positive finite-volume case. The two-insertion side defines `layerMarkedCyclicGibbsWeight`, `layerTwoPointNumerator`, and the normalised `layerTwoPoint`; **headline** `layerTwoPoint_eq_trace_ratio` identifies the normalised Gibbs two-point function with `Tr(D_f T^a D_f T^b) / Tr(T^(a+b))`. The hyperplane-facing entry points `LayerState S := Config S`, `layerSpinAt`, `layerSpinTwoPoint`, and `layerSpinTwoPoint_eq_trace_ratio` specialise the observable to a spin at a fixed transverse site, ready for a later finite box/hyperplane edge decomposition. This remains finite and abstract: no concrete `Z^d` box decomposition, Perron--Frobenius theory, thermodynamic limit, or exponential-decay estimate. Reference: Glimm--Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304--306. |
| §17.1 | **Finite cyclic layer cylinder reindexing and trace bridge** (Issue #3511) | **Done (finite stack bridge)** | `TransferMatrix/LayerCylinder.lean`. Adds the concrete finite stack side of the layer formalism. `LayerCylinderSite N S := Fin N × S` and `layerCylinderConfigEquiv` identify ordinary spin configurations on a periodic stack with layer configurations `Fin N → LayerState S`. The Ising-facing weights `layerInternalWeight` and `layerTransitionWeight` package the internal transverse graph contribution, the field contribution, and finite adjacent-layer pair couplings. The concrete stack sum `layerCylinderPartition` is reindexed to the abstract layer sum by **headline** `layerCylinderPartition_eq_layerCyclicPartition`, and therefore to the transfer trace by `layerCylinderPartition_eq_trace`; the Ising-weight wrappers `isingLayerCylinderPartition_eq_layerCyclicPartition` and `isingLayerCylinderPartition_eq_trace` specialise these identities. The same reindexing is provided for same-transverse-site two-point numerators via `layerCylinderSpinTwoPointNumerator_eq_layerTwoPointNumerator`, giving `layerCylinderSpinTwoPoint_eq_layerSpinTwoPoint` and `layerCylinderSpinTwoPoint_eq_trace_ratio`. This is still finite and periodic in the transfer direction: no open-box/free-boundary formula, Perron--Frobenius theory, thermodynamic limit, or exponential-decay estimate is included. Reference: Glimm--Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304--306. |
| §17.1 | **Finite layer-cylinder graph and stack-weight bridge** (Issue #3511) | **Done (project graph ↔ layer stack)** | `TransferMatrix/LayerCylinderGraph.lean`. Adds the explicit finite periodic layer-cylinder graph on `LayerCylinderSite N S = Fin N × S`. A transverse graph `H` supplies copied internal layer edges (`layerCylinderInternalEdgeFinset`), and a finite set of directed transverse pairs `E : Finset (S × S)` supplies transition edges `s((i,x),(i+1,y))` (`layerCylinderTransitionEdgeFinset`). The graph `layerCylinderGraph H E N` is built by `SimpleGraph.fromEdgeSet` from the union of these explicit finite edge sets. The finitary edge sanity lemmas prove that these generated edges are non-diagonal for `2 ≤ N`, giving `layerCylinderGraph_edgeFinset_eq`: the graph's project-level `edgeFinset` is exactly the explicit cylinder edge finset. For `3 ≤ N`, `layerCylinderTransitionEdge_injective_of_three_le` rules out the `N=2` unordered-edge collapse, and the product split lemmas identify the explicit edge/field product with the layer stack weight (`layerCylinderEdgeFieldProd_eq_layerCylinderStackWeight`). Headline bridges: `boltzmannWeight_layerCylinderGraph_eq_layerCylinderStackWeight`, `partitionFunction_layerCylinderGraph_eq_isingLayerCylinderPartition`, `correlation_layerCylinderGraph_same_transverse_eq_layerCylinderSpinTwoPoint`, and `correlation_layerCylinderGraph_same_transverse_eq_trace_ratio`. Open slab/box geometry, Perron--Frobenius theory, thermodynamic limits, and exponential decay remain later Issue #3511 steps. Reference: Glimm--Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304--306. |
| §17.1 | **Cubic transverse boxes as finite layer cylinders** (Issue #3511) | **Done (concrete transverse-box specialisation)** | `TransferMatrix/CubicLayerCylinder.lean`. Specialises the project graph ↔ layer stack bridge to the concrete transverse site type `CubicLayerSite d R := ↑(Ambient.cubicBox d R)` with transverse graph `cubicLayerGraph d R := Ambient.inducedGraph (latticeGraph d) (Ambient.cubicBox d R)`. Consecutive periodic layers are coupled by identity transition pairs `cubicLayerTransitionPairs`, and `cubicLayerCylinderGraph d R N` is the resulting `layerCylinderGraph` over the cubic box. Headline bridges: `boltzmannWeight_cubicLayerCylinderGraph_eq_layerCylinderStackWeight`, `partitionFunction_cubicLayerCylinderGraph_eq_isingLayerCylinderPartition`, and `correlation_cubicLayerCylinderGraph_same_transverse_eq_trace_ratio`, all retaining the `3 ≤ N` / `3 ≤ a+b` hypothesis inherited from the unordered transition-edge injectivity in the periodic cylinder. Open longitudinal slabs/boxes, Perron--Frobenius theory, thermodynamic limits, and exponential decay remain later Issue #3511 steps. |
| §17.1 | **Finite positive layer spectral scaffold** (Issue #3511) | **Done (certificate scaffold, no PF theorem)** | `TransferMatrix/LayerSpectral.lean`. Adds the algebraic scaffold needed before the higher-dimensional Perron--Frobenius/spectral-gap step. Positivity vocabulary `MatrixEntrywiseNonnegative`, `MatrixEntrywisePositive`, `VectorNonnegative`, and `VectorPositive` packages finite positive matrices and vectors; `layerTransferMatrix_entrywisePositive` records that positive layer and transition weights make `layerTransferMatrix u k` entrywise positive. The finite positive-matrix basics `matrixEntrywisePositive_mul`, `matrixEntrywisePositive_pow_of_pos`, `trace_pow_pos_of_entrywisePositive`, `StrictPositiveRightEigenpair`, and `eigenvalue_pos_of_strictPositiveRightEigenpair` give the minimal reusable PF-facing language without asserting existence or uniqueness of eigenvectors. The balanced matrix `layerSymmetricTransferMatrix u k` has entries `sqrt (u a) * k a b * sqrt (u b)`; for positive `u`, `layerTransferMatrix u k = D⁻¹ S D`, and the partition and marked traces are invariant under this diagonal similarity (`layerTransferPartitionTrace_eq_layerSymmetricTransferPartitionTrace`, `layerTransferCorrelation_matrixElement_eq_layerSymmetricTransferCorrelationTrace`). If `k` is symmetric then `S` is symmetric (`layerSymmetricTransferMatrix_transpose`). Finally, `LayerSpectralGapCertificate` packages later spectral input as a positive scale, subdominant ratio `theta < 1`, a partition lower bound, and a marked-trace upper bound; `layerTwoPoint_abs_le_of_spectralGapCertificate` and `layerSpinTwoPoint_abs_le_of_spectralGapCertificate` turn that certificate into an exponential bound for the finite cyclic layer two-point trace ratio. This PR deliberately proves no Perron--Frobenius theorem, spectral-radius theorem, open-slab transfer API, thermodynamic limit, or final hyperplane exponential decay. Reference: Glimm--Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304--306. |
| §17.1 | **Balanced layer spectral certificate transport** (Issue #3511) | **Done (balanced certificate transport)** | `TransferMatrix/LayerSpectral.lean`. Adds the balanced-matrix certificate layer used by the future symmetric spectral route. `LayerBalancedSpectralGapCertificate` states the same finite scale/ratio/prefactor data as `LayerSpectralGapCertificate`, but its partition and marked-trace bounds are phrased directly for the balanced matrix `layerSymmetricTransferMatrix u k` through `layerSymmetricTransferPartitionTrace` and `layerSymmetricTransferCorrelationTrace`. The transport `LayerBalancedSpectralGapCertificate.toLayerSpectralGapCertificate` uses the already-proved diagonal similarity trace identities, requiring only positivity of `u`, to recover the ordinary transfer-matrix certificate. Explicit constructors `layerSpectralGapCertificate_of_traceBounds`, `layerBalancedSpectralGapCertificate_of_traceBounds`, and `layerSpectralGapCertificate_of_balancedTraceBounds` package trace-bound hypotheses without claiming where they come from. Consumer wrappers `layerTwoPoint_abs_le_of_balancedSpectralGapCertificate` and `layerSpinTwoPoint_abs_le_of_balancedSpectralGapCertificate` give the finite cyclic layer two-point bound from a balanced certificate. This remains a finite certificate-transport PR: no Perron--Frobenius existence theorem, spectral-radius theorem, open-slab API, thermodynamic limit, or final hyperplane exponential decay is proved. Reference: Glimm--Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304--306. |
| §17.1 | **Balanced finite spectral input** (Issue #3511) | **Done (orthogonal spectral data, no PF theorem)** | `TransferMatrix/LayerSpectral.lean`. Connects the balanced layer matrix to finite Hermitian spectral theory without proving Perron--Frobenius. For symmetric `k`, `layerSymmetricTransferMatrix_isHermitian` turns the balanced matrix into a mathlib Hermitian matrix, with spectral-theorem accessors `layerSymmetricTransferEigenvalues`, `layerSymmetricTransferEigenvectorBasis`, and `layerSymmetricTransferMatrix_mulVec_eigenvectorBasis`. The generic bridge `trace_pow_eq_sum_hermitian_eigenvalues_pow` gives `Tr(M^N) = ∑ i, λ_i^N` for finite real Hermitian matrices. The explicit-data API `RealOrthogonalSpectralData` packages an orthogonal diagonalization, proves `pow_eq`, `trace_pow_eq_sum`, and `marked_trace_eq_sum`, where the marked trace is expressed through the transported marking matrix `markedMatrix`. Finally, `layerBalancedSpectralGapCertificate_of_orthogonalSpectralData` constructs a balanced certificate from explicit spectral-basis partition and marked-trace bounds. The constructor deliberately requires the one-sided cyclic marked-trace bound as an explicit finite spectral hypothesis; it does not derive a PF eigenvector, spectral-radius maximality, cancellation, open-slab API, thermodynamic limit, or final hyperplane exponential decay. Reference: Glimm--Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304--306. |
| §17.1 | **Perron–Frobenius finite-volume spin two-point decay** (Issue #3511) | **Done (PF wired to finite-volume decay, modulo the finite prefactor)** | _(scaffolding removed in PR-B1: route obstructed #4081, superseded by §18 #4228/#4255; archived at git tag `archive/transfermatrix-spectral-gap-scaffolding`)_. Wired the Perron–Frobenius theorem for the entrywise-positive symmetric balanced layer transfer matrix (`signedPositiveColumn_maxEigenIndex` constructs the dominant signed-positive Perron column unconditionally; `subdominantRatio_maxEigenIndex_lt_one` gives the strict spectral gap `θ < 1`, both in `TransferMatrix/LayerPerronExistence/OrthogonalSpectralData.lean`) all the way through to the **finite-volume spin two-point decay**. The removed file's two declarations — a certificate and the decay bound it feeds — went with it, so neither name is a declaration of the current tree; the certificate was a one-shot composition of lemmas that all survive and the decay bound was that certificate handed to a surviving consumer, so the route is recorded here through those surviving names. The certificate step is the spin-observable constructor `layerBalancedMinSpectralGapCertificate_of_orthogonalDominantBounds_flipEvenSpin` (`TransferMatrix/LayerSpectral/BalancedSpectralGap.lean`) applied to the spectral datum `layerSymmetricTransferOrthogonalSpectralData` (`TransferMatrix/LayerSpectral/FlipParityLayerSymmetric.lean`) at that datum's maximal eigenindex, giving the balanced min-separation spectral-gap certificate for the layer spin observable `layerSpinAt x` with **every spectral hypothesis discharged by Perron–Frobenius**: entrywise positivity (`layerSymmetricTransferMatrix_entrywisePositive`, `TransferMatrix/LayerSpectral/BalancedMatrix.lean`) gives the dominant signed-positive column, hence the strictly positive dominant eigenvalue (`eigenvalue_pos_of_signedPositiveColumn`), the strict gap, and the per-eigenvalue bound (`eigenvalue_abs_le_subdominantRatio_maxEigenIndex`), while global spin-flip invariance of the weights gives the flip-even marked-channel cancellation (`layerSymmetricTransfer_signedPositiveColumn_flip_even`, `TransferMatrix/LayerPerronExistence/SpinObservableCertificates.lean`); the only remaining quantitative input is the finite prefactor smallness `(card(LayerState S) − 1)·θ < 1`. Feeding that certificate to `layerSpinTwoPoint_abs_le_min_of_balancedMinSpectralGapCertificate`, in the same balanced-spectral-gap file, gives the geometric decay `|⟨σ_x σ_x⟩^{(a,b)}| ≤ C·θ^{min a b}` of the layer spin two-point function `layerSpinTwoPoint` (`TransferMatrix/LayerGibbs.lean`), with `θ` the Perron subdominant ratio. This is the unconditional (modulo the finite prefactor) finite-volume realization of the transfer-matrix spectral-gap decay; the transverse-volume-uniform prefactor condition (`(card − 1)·θ < 1` uniformly in `|S|`) is the remaining separately-tracked input (no infinite-volume or volume-uniform decay is claimed). Requires `0 < u`, `0 < k`, symmetric and global-spin-flip-invariant `u, k` (zero external field). Reference: Glimm–Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304–306. |
| §17.1 | **Quantitative (explicit-ratio) Perron–Frobenius finite-volume spin decay** (Issue #3511) | **Done (PF decay made quantitatively dischargeable, modulo the finite prefactor)** | _(scaffolding removed in PR-B1: route obstructed #4081, superseded by §18 #4228/#4255; archived at git tag `archive/transfermatrix-spectral-gap-scaffolding`)_. Bridged the Perron–Frobenius finite-volume spin two-point decay from the *opaque* `Classical.choose` ratio `subdominantRatio_maxEigenIndex` (which only satisfies `θ < 1` and cannot consume quantitative high-temperature gap estimates) to the *explicit*, quantitatively-boundable rate `subdominantAbsRatio_maxEigenIndex` (`= max_{i≠top} |λ_i|/λ_top`, `TransferMatrix/LayerOpenExplicitSubdominantRatio.lean`). Both ratios survive the removal, but the four certificate/decay pairs the file built on them did not: all eight of its declarations went with it, so none of the names it introduced is a declaration of the current tree. Two of the four routes are recorded here through the surviving lemmas they composed, and two are not. **Generic quantitative form** (supply **any** rate `θ ∈ [0,1)` bounding every non-dominant eigenvalue `|λ_i| ≤ θ·λ_top` with `(card(LayerState S) − 1)·θ < 1`, and obtain geometric decay `|⟨σ_x σ_x⟩^{(a,b)}| ≤ C·θ^{min a b}` at rate `θ`): this is the composition of the row above with the Perron ratio replaced by the supplied `θ`, i.e. `layerBalancedMinSpectralGapCertificate_of_orthogonalDominantBounds_flipEvenSpin` followed by `layerSpinTwoPoint_abs_le_min_of_balancedMinSpectralGapCertificate`, both in `TransferMatrix/LayerSpectral/BalancedSpectralGap.lean`, with all spectral inputs still discharged by Perron–Frobenius. **Explicit-ratio form**: discharging `θ` by the explicit ratio needs nothing beyond `subdominantAbsRatio_maxEigenIndex_nonneg`, `subdominantAbsRatio_maxEigenIndex_lt_one` and `eigenvalue_abs_le_subdominantAbsRatio_maxEigenIndex` of the explicit-ratio file, which are exactly the three rate hypotheses of that generic form and all survive, the prefactor smallness being untouched by them. **Quadratic-form-gap and top-deflated-Gershgorin forms** (discharging `θ` from a top-orthogonal quadratic-form gap `|⟨v, Mv⟩| ≤ θ·λ_top·‖v‖²`, respectively from the circularity-free top-deflated Gershgorin envelope `maxAbsDiag(M_def) + offMass(M_def) ≤ θ·λ_top`, the high-temperature mechanism of GJ §17.1): recorded as history only. The quadratic form itself (`matrixQuadraticForm`, `vectorSqNorm`) survives, but no lemma of the current tree bounds the explicit subdominant ratio by a top-orthogonal quadratic-form gap, and the whole top-deflation layer — deflated matrix, its diagonal and off-diagonal row-sum envelopes, and the ratio bound they gave — was removed with this cluster and has no successor under any name; those proofs survive only in the archived tag. Required `0 < u`, `0 < k`, symmetric and global-spin-flip-invariant `u, k` (zero external field); the prefactor `(card − 1)·θ < 1` and the route hypotheses remained the explicit quantitative inputs (no infinite-volume or transverse-volume-uniform decay was claimed). Reference: Glimm–Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304–306. |
| §17.1 | **Flatness reduction of the top-deflated Gershgorin envelope** (Issue #3511) | **Done (diagonal half of the envelope eliminated)** | _(scaffolding removed in PR-B1: route obstructed #4081, superseded by §18 #4228/#4255; archived at git tag `archive/transfermatrix-spectral-gap-scaffolding`)_. History only: every declaration of this file went with it and, unlike the two rows above, none has a successor in the current tree — the deflated matrix, the flatness ratio and the ratio bound they combined into are absent from `IsingModel/` under any name — so none of its names is cited here and the proofs survive only in the archived tag. What the file did is still worth recording, because it is where the §17.1 obstruction becomes visible. It removed the diagonal half of the top-deflated Gershgorin hypothesis: by the row-cancellation identity `M_def · w = 0` for the Perron column `w` (the deflated diagonal is a Perron-weighted sum of its own off-diagonal row), the deflated diagonal is bounded by the off-diagonal mass scaled by the **Perron flatness ratio** `ρ = sup_{i,j} |w_j/w_i|`, giving `|M_def_ii| ≤ ρ·offMass_i`, then `maxAbsDiag(M_def) ≤ ρ·maxOffMass(M_def)`, then the full envelope `≤ (1+ρ)·maxOffMass(M_def)`, and finally the capstone `maxOffMass(M_def) ≤ η·λ_top ⇒ subdominantAbsRatio_maxEigenIndex ≤ (1+ρ)·η`. This isolated the finite algebraic part of the gap; the inputs left standing were the deflated off-diagonal mass plus the flatness ratio `ρ` — the genuinely analytic, non-transverse-volume-uniform obstruction for the interacting layer, and the reason this route was abandoned rather than completed. Its four top-deflation lemmas lived in the generic `RealOrthogonalSpectralData` namespace, which itself survives (the structure is declared in `TransferMatrix/LayerSpectral/HermitianBridge.lean`); the flatness ratio `ρ` and its two supporting lemmas were plain `IsingModel.TransferMatrix` declarations outside that namespace. Reference: Glimm–Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304–306. |
| §17.1 | **Balanced dominant spectral-basis bounds** (Issue #3511) | **Superseded — route obstructed (#4081) and replaced by the §18 cluster-expansion route (#4228, option ii); conditional finite scaffolding only, not an open book gap (the infinite-volume mass gap / no-phase-transition conclusion is Done via #4228/#4255). Partial (conditional finite spectral bounds)** | `TransferMatrix/LayerSpectral.lean`. Adds the next conditional finite spectral-basis estimate behind the balanced certificate route. Positive finite matrices are now connected to mathlib's graph-theoretic vocabulary by `matrixEntrywisePositive_isPrimitive`, `matrixEntrywisePositive_isIrreducible`, `layerSymmetricTransferMatrix_isPrimitive`, and `layerSymmetricTransferMatrix_isIrreducible`; these are positivity/primitive bridges only, not PF eigenvalue theorems. The finite spectral side adds `finiteSpectralPartitionPrefactor`, `RealOrthogonalSpectralData.markedSpectralPrefactor`, `eigenvalue_abs_le_scale_of_dominant_bounds`, `partition_sum_lower_of_dominant_bounds`, `partition_lower_of_dominant_bounds`, and `marked_sum_abs_le_spectralPrefactor`. The capstone `layerBalancedSpectralGapCertificate_of_orthogonalDominantBounds` constructs a balanced certificate from a chosen dominant spectral index, a subdominant absolute bound, the finite-cardinality condition `(Fintype.card Ω - 1) * theta < 1`, and one-sided marked-column cancellation. Reference: Glimm--Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304--306. |
| §17.1 | **Hermitian spectral data and min-separation marked bounds** (Issue #3511) | **Superseded — route obstructed (#4081) and replaced by the §18 cluster-expansion route (#4228, option ii); conditional finite scaffolding only, not an open book gap (the infinite-volume mass gap / no-phase-transition conclusion is Done via #4228/#4255). Adds the finite cyclic estimate needed before the thermodynamic-limit reading of the balanced transfer-matrix route. `RealOrthogonalSpectralData.ofHermitian` now constructs explicit real orthogonal spectral data from mathlib's Hermitian spectral theorem, and `layerSymmetricTransferOrthogonalSpectralData` applies it to the balanced matrix when `k` is symmetric. The marked matrix is symmetric (`markedMatrix_transpose`, `markedMatrix_comm`). The new theorem `marked_sum_abs_le_spectralPrefactor_min` replaces the too-strong full dominant-column cancellation with the natural finite-cycle hypothesis `G top top = 0`: once the dominant-dominant channel is removed, every remaining spectral term has a subdominant eigenvalue on at least one arc, giving a `theta ^ min a b` bound. `LayerBalancedMinSpectralGapCertificate`, `layerBalancedMinSpectralGapCertificate_of_orthogonalDominantBounds`, and `layerBalancedMinSpectralGapCertificate_of_layerHermitianDominantBounds` package this estimate, while `layerTwoPoint_abs_le_min_of_balancedMinSpectralGapCertificate`, `layerTwoPoint_abs_le_left_of_balancedMinSpectralGapCertificate`, and the spin wrapper consume it. This remains conditional: it does not prove PF existence, spectral-radius maximality, `G top top = 0` for spin observables, open-slab geometry, thermodynamic limits, or final hyperplane exponential decay. Reference: Glimm--Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304--306; §17.5, pp. 311--312. |
| §17.1 | **Spin-flip symmetry and dominant-channel cancellation bridge** (Issue #3511) | **Superseded — route obstructed (#4081) and replaced by the §18 cluster-expansion route (#4228, option ii); conditional finite scaffolding only, not an open book gap (the infinite-volume mass gap / no-phase-transition conclusion is Done via #4228/#4255). | §17.1 | **Positive/simple dominant-column flip-even bridge** (Issue #3511) | **Superseded — route obstructed (#4081) and replaced by the §18 cluster-expansion route (#4228, option ii); conditional finite scaffolding only, not an open book gap (the infinite-volume mass gap / no-phase-transition conclusion is Done via #4228/#4255). The layer wrapper `layerSymmetricTransfer_positive_eigenvector_flip_even_of_simple_eigenspace` turns zero-field global-flip commutation into flip-evenness of a positive simple eigenvector. | §17.1 | **Positive-column eigenvalue-radius bound** (Issue #3511) | **Superseded — route obstructed (#4081) and replaced by the §18 cluster-expansion route (#4228, option ii); conditional finite scaffolding only, not an open book gap (the infinite-volume mass gap / no-phase-transition conclusion is Done via #4228/#4255).
