Adds a finite Collatz--Wielandt-style bridge for the future Perron--Frobenius step. `exists_positive_vector_abs_bound_attained` chooses the optimal coordinatewise scale `C` with `|w i| ≤ C * v i` against a strictly positive vector and records an attaining coordinate. `abs_eigenvalue_le_of_entrywisePositive_positive_eigenpair` uses entrywise positivity and the triangle inequality to show that if `M` has a strictly positive right eigenpair `(lam, v)`, then every real right eigenvalue `mu` satisfies `|mu| ≤ lam`. The spectral-data wrappers `RealOrthogonalSpectralData.changeOfBasis_column_ne_zero`, `RealOrthogonalSpectralData.eigenvalue_pos_of_positive_column`, and `RealOrthogonalSpectralData.eigenvalue_abs_le_of_positive_column` apply this to explicit orthogonal spectral columns; `layerSymmetricTransfer_eigenvalue_abs_le_of_positive_column` gives the balanced layer transfer version. The scale-fixed constructors `layerBalancedMinSpectralGapCertificate_of_orthogonalSubdominantBounds`, `layerBalancedMinSpectralGapCertificate_of_layerHermitianSubdominantBounds`, and their positive/simple spin variants set the certificate scale to the positive top column eigenvalue, leaving only the quantitative subdominant `theta` bound and marked-channel cancellation inputs. This remains conditional: it proves no PF existence theorem, no uniqueness of the positive column, no strict subdominant gap, no open-slab geometry, no thermodynamic limit, and no final hyperplane exponential decay. Reference: Glimm--Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304--306; §17.5, pp. 311--312. |
| §17.1 | **Positive-column eigenspace simplicity and strict subdominant ratio** (Issue #3511) | **Superseded — route obstructed (#4081) and replaced by the §18 cluster-expansion route (#4228, option ii); conditional finite scaffolding only, not an open book gap (the infinite-volume mass gap / no-phase-transition conclusion is Done via #4228/#4255). Partial (conditional positive-column spectral gap)** | `TransferMatrix/LayerPerron.lean`. Strengthens the finite Perron-facing bridge once a positive top spectral column is available. `exists_positive_vector_upper_bound_attained` chooses the optimal signed coordinatewise scale `C` with `w i ≤ C * v i`; `eigenvector_smul_of_entrywisePositive_positive_eigenpair` applies this to `z = C v - w`, using entrywise positivity to show that any vector with the same positive eigenvalue is a scalar multiple of `v`. The strict comparison `abs_eigenvalue_lt_of_entrywisePositive_positive_eigenpair` rules out equality in the weak bound by applying this one-dimensionality argument to `M * M`. For explicit orthogonal spectral data, `changeOfBasis_columns_not_smul`, `eigenspace_simple_of_positive_column`, `eigenvalue_abs_lt_of_positive_column`, and `exists_subdominant_abs_ratio_of_positive_column` show that non-top columns have strictly smaller absolute eigenvalues and yield some finite `theta < 1`. Layer wrappers expose the same conclusions for `layerSymmetricTransferOrthogonalSpectralData`. The new `_positiveColumnFlipSpin` certificate constructors derive the simple-eigenspace input from positive layer weights and the positive top column, while preserving the older `_positiveSimpleFlipSpin` constructors. This still does not prove PF existence of the positive column, choose the dominant column automatically, prove the quantitative certificate prefactor condition, open-slab geometry, thermodynamic limits, or final hyperplane exponential decay. Reference: Glimm--Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304--306; §17.5, pp. 311--312. |
| §17.1 | **Signed-positive spectral-column interface** (Issue #3511) | **Superseded — route obstructed (#4081) and replaced by the §18 cluster-expansion route (#4228, option ii); conditional finite scaffolding only, not an open book gap (the infinite-volume mass gap / no-phase-transition conclusion is Done via #4228/#4255). Partial (sign-invariant PF-output bridge)** | `TransferMatrix/LayerPerronExistence.lean`. Adds the sign-invariant interface needed before the finite Perron--Frobenius existence step can be connected to the spectral certificate API. Orthogonal spectral columns are only determined up to sign, so `RealOrthogonalSpectralData.SignedPositiveColumn` packages a scalar sign with square one and positivity of the oriented column. The wrappers `eigenvalue_pos_of_signedPositiveColumn`, `eigenvalue_abs_le_of_signedPositiveColumn`, `eigenspace_simple_of_signedPositiveColumn`, `eigenvalue_abs_lt_of_signedPositiveColumn`, and `exists_subdominant_abs_ratio_of_signedPositiveColumn` reuse the positive-column Perron bridge after orientation. Layer wrappers expose the same radius, simplicity, strict-ratio, and finite-`theta` conclusions for balanced transfer matrices, and the signed-positive spin constructors derive flip-even cancellation before entering the existing min-separation certificate route. The helper `RealOrthogonalSpectralData.maxEigenIndex` records the finite maximum-eigenvalue index for the maximal-column construction. This interface row remains separate from the actual maximal-column construction below; the quantitative certificate prefactor condition, open-slab geometry, thermodynamic limits, and final hyperplane exponential decay remain later Issue #3511 work. Reference: Glimm--Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304--306; §17.5, pp. 311--312. |
| §17.1 | **Signed-positive maximal spectral column** (Issue #3511) | **Superseded — route obstructed (#4081) and replaced by the §18 cluster-expansion route (#4228, option ii); conditional finite scaffolding only, not an open book gap (the infinite-volume mass gap / no-phase-transition conclusion is Done via #4228/#4255). Partial (finite PF maximal-column output)** | `TransferMatrix/LayerPerronExistence.lean`. Proves the actual finite signed-positive maximal-column output for the existing real orthogonal spectral-data route. New quadratic-form helpers `vectorSqNorm`, `matrixQuadraticForm`, `RealOrthogonalSpectralData.spectralCoord`, `matrixQuadraticForm_eq_sum_spectralCoord_sq`, `sum_spectralCoord_sq`, and `matrixQuadraticForm_le_maxEigenIndex` give the Rayleigh upper bound for the finite maximal eigenvalue index. For an entrywise positive matrix, `matrixQuadraticForm_le_abs`, `matrixQuadraticForm_abs_sub`, and `abs_defect_eq_zero_of_matrixQuadraticForm_abs_eq` show that replacing a maximal eigenvector by coordinatewise absolute values cannot improve the Rayleigh quotient unless every sign-defect vanishes. The orientation lemma `exists_sign_vectorNonnegative_of_abs_defect_zero`, together with `matrixEntrywisePositive_mulVec_pos_of_nonnegative_nonzero` and `matrixEntrywisePositive_eigenvector_pos_of_nonnegative_nonzero`, upgrades the oriented nonnegative eigenvector to a strictly positive one. The capstone `RealOrthogonalSpectralData.signedPositiveColumn_maxEigenIndex` constructs `E.SignedPositiveColumn E.maxEigenIndex`; `layerSymmetricTransfer_signedPositiveColumn_maxEigenIndex` applies it to the positive balanced layer transfer matrix. This proves the sign-invariant finite maximal-column PF output, not raw un-oriented column positivity, and still leaves the quantitative finite prefactor smallness, open-slab geometry, thermodynamic limits, and final hyperplane exponential decay to later Issue #3511 work. Reference: Glimm--Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304--306; §17.5, pp. 311--312. |
| §17.1 | **Max-index signed-positive certificate constructors** (Issue #3511) | **Superseded — route obstructed (#4081) and replaced by the §18 cluster-expansion route (#4228, option ii); conditional finite scaffolding only, not an open book gap (the infinite-volume mass gap / no-phase-transition conclusion is Done via #4228/#4255). Partial (automatic finite max-index certificate route)** | `TransferMatrix/LayerPerronExistence.lean`. Connects the signed-positive maximal spectral column to the existing balanced min-separation certificate API. The new wrappers `RealOrthogonalSpectralData.eigenvalue_pos_maxEigenIndex`, `eigenvalue_abs_le_maxEigenIndex`, `eigenspace_simple_maxEigenIndex`, and `eigenvalue_abs_lt_maxEigenIndex` expose the Perron-facing conclusions at `E.maxEigenIndex`. The canonical finite ratio `RealOrthogonalSpectralData.subdominantRatio_maxEigenIndex` is chosen from the strict finite subdominant-ratio theorem, with nonnegativity, `< 1`, and non-top absolute-eigenvalue bounds packaged as accessor lemmas; `layerSymmetricTransfer_subdominantRatio_maxEigenIndex` exposes the same ratio for the Hermitian balanced layer transfer matrix. The constructors `layerBalancedMinSpectralGapCertificate_of_orthogonalMaxEigenIndex`, `layerBalancedMinSpectralGapCertificate_of_layerHermitianMaxEigenIndex`, and their spin-flip variants set `top := E.maxEigenIndex`, `scale := E.eigenvalue E.maxEigenIndex`, and `theta := E.subdominantRatio_maxEigenIndex`, while deriving signed-positive flip-even cancellation automatically for spin observables. The finite prefactor smallness condition `(Fintype.card Ω - 1) * theta < 1` remains an explicit quantitative hypothesis; this still does not prove open-slab geometry, thermodynamic limits, or final hyperplane exponential decay. Reference: Glimm--Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304--306; §17.5, pp. 311--312. |
| §17.1 | **Finite prefactor smallness bridge** (Issue #3511) | **Superseded — route obstructed (#4081) and replaced by the §18 cluster-expansion route (#4228, option ii); conditional finite scaffolding only, not an open book gap (the infinite-volume mass gap / no-phase-transition conclusion is Done via #4228/#4255). Partial (quantitative sufficient conditions)** | `TransferMatrix/{LayerSpectral,LayerPerronExistence}.lean`. Adds the honest finite-cardinality bridge used by the balanced min-separation certificate route. The arithmetic lemmas `finiteSpectralPartitionPrefactor_small_of_card_eq_one` and `finiteSpectralPartitionPrefactor_small_of_lt_inv_cardSubOne` derive the required prefactor condition `((Fintype.card Ω - 1) * theta) < 1` only from stronger hypotheses: either `Fintype.card Ω = 1`, where the prefactor multiplier vanishes, or `1 < Fintype.card Ω` together with `theta < ((Fintype.card Ω - 1 : ℕ) : ℝ)⁻¹`. The max-index certificate wrappers `layerBalancedMinSpectralGapCertificate_of_orthogonalMaxEigenIndex_cardOne`, `layerBalancedMinSpectralGapCertificate_of_orthogonalMaxEigenIndex_ratioSmall`, `layerBalancedMinSpectralGapCertificate_of_layerHermitianMaxEigenIndex_cardOne`, `layerBalancedMinSpectralGapCertificate_of_layerHermitianMaxEigenIndex_ratioSmall`, and their `FlipSpin` variants feed these sufficient conditions into the existing max-index constructors. This deliberately does not infer finite prefactor smallness from `theta < 1` alone, and still leaves the physical quantitative estimate for concrete layer systems, open-slab geometry, thermodynamic limits, and final hyperplane exponential decay for later Issue #3511 work. Reference: Glimm--Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304--306; §17.5, pp. 311--312. |
| §17.1 | **One-site transverse small-ratio bridge** (Issue #3511) | **Superseded — route obstructed (#4081) and replaced by the §18 cluster-expansion route (#4228, option ii); conditional finite scaffolding only, not an open book gap (the infinite-volume mass gap / no-phase-transition conclusion is Done via #4228/#4255). The cardinality lemmas `layerState_card_eq_two_pow` and `layerState_card_eq_two_of_card_eq_one` record `Fintype.card (LayerState S) = 2 ^ Fintype.card S`, hence a one-site transverse layer has exactly two layer states. The arithmetic lemmas `finiteSpectralPartitionPrefactor_small_of_card_eq_two` and `finiteSpectralPartitionPrefactor_small_of_layerState_card_eq_one` show that in this two-state case the prefactor smallness condition follows from the strict ratio bound `theta < 1`. The new accessor `layerSymmetricTransfer_subdominantRatio_maxEigenIndex_lt_one` and the spin-certificate wrappers `layerBalancedMinSpectralGapCertificate_of_orthogonalMaxEigenIndexFlipSpin_oneSite` and `layerBalancedMinSpectralGapCertificate_of_layerHermitianMaxEigenIndexFlipSpin_oneSite` therefore discharge finite prefactor smallness for one-site transverse layers from the already proved max-index strict subdominant ratio. This does not claim `theta < 1` suffices for larger layer state spaces, and it does not prove open-slab geometry, thermodynamic limits, or final hyperplane exponential decay. Reference: Glimm--Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304--306; §17.5, pp. 311--312. |
| §17.1 | **Layer-cardinality small-ratio bridge** (Issue #3511) | **Superseded — route obstructed (#4081) and replaced by the §18 cluster-expansion route (#4228, option ii); conditional finite scaffolding only, not an open book gap (the infinite-volume mass gap / no-phase-transition conclusion is Done via #4228/#4255). Rewrites the finite prefactor smallness hypothesis for nonempty transverse layers using the concrete layer-state cardinality `Fintype.card (LayerState S) = 2 ^ Fintype.card S`. The helper `layerState_card_nontrivial_of_nonempty` records that a nonempty transverse layer has a nontrivial layer-state space, and `finiteSpectralPartitionPrefactor_small_of_layerState_lt_inv_two_pow_cardSubOne` turns the expanded hypothesis `theta < ((2 ^ Fintype.card S - 1 : ℕ) : ℝ)⁻¹` into the certificate prefactor condition. This is not a physical proof that the canonical subdominant ratio satisfies the threshold, and it still does not make `theta < 1` sufficient for larger layer state spaces, nor prove open-slab geometry, thermodynamic limits, or final hyperplane exponential decay. Reference: Glimm--Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304--306; §17.5, pp. 311--312. |

| §17.1 | **Layer spectral-window small-ratio bridge** (Issue #3511) | **Superseded — route obstructed (#4081) and replaced by the §18 cluster-expansion route (#4228, option ii); conditional finite scaffolding only, not an open book gap (the infinite-volume mass gap / no-phase-transition conclusion is Done via #4228/#4255). | §17.1 | **Infinite-temperature layer spectral-window bridge** (Issue #3511) | **Superseded — route obstructed (#4081) and replaced by the §18 cluster-expansion route (#4228, option ii); conditional finite scaffolding only, not an open book gap (the infinite-volume mass gap / no-phase-transition conclusion is Done via #4228/#4255). Partial (beta-zero physical spectral window)** | _(scaffolding removed in PR-B1: route obstructed #4081, superseded by §18 #4228/#4255; archived at git tag `archive/transfermatrix-spectral-gap-scaffolding`)_. Added the first concrete physical spectral-window estimate feeding the explicit `theta` bridge. History only: the module's declarations went with it, none has a successor in the current tree, and the all-ones matrix it introduced occurs nowhere in `IsingModel/` under any name, so none of its names is cited here; the proofs survive only in the archived tag. The physical weights it evaluated do survive as definitions — `layerInternalWeight` and `layerTransitionWeight` (`TransferMatrix/LayerCylinder.lean`) — but their `p.β = 0` evaluations do not. The finite all-ones matrix API proved that every spectral-data eigenvalue is either `Fintype.card Ω` or `0`; using the existing positive max-index route, all non-maximal eigenvalues then satisfy the spectral window with `theta = 0`. The physical layer lemmas identified the balanced layer transfer matrix at `p.β = 0` with the all-ones matrix, and four wrappers fed `theta = 0` into the inverse-cardinality spectral-window certificate route — orthogonal and Hermitian spectral data, for nonempty transverse layers and for cubic transverse boxes. This was only the infinite-temperature slice: it did not prove a high-temperature neighborhood, a Walsh diagonalization, arbitrary multi-site `theta < 1` sufficiency, open slabs, thermodynamic limits, or final hyperplane exponential decay. Reference: Glimm--Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304--306; §17.5, pp. 311--312. |
| §17.1 | **One-site layer spectral-window bridge** (Issue #3511) | **Superseded — route obstructed (#4081) and replaced by the §18 cluster-expansion route (#4228, option ii); conditional finite scaffolding only, not an open book gap (the infinite-volume mass gap / no-phase-transition conclusion is Done via #4228/#4255). Partial (one-site positive-temperature physical window)** | `TransferMatrix/OneSiteLayerSpectralWindow.lean`. Adds the first positive-temperature physical layer spectral-window bridge beyond the beta-zero slice. For the one-site transverse layer `PUnit`, zero field, no internal edges, and identity longitudinal transition pairs, `layerSymmetricTransferMatrix_punit_eq_reindex_isingTransferMatrix1D` identifies the balanced layer transfer matrix with the reindexed 1D transfer matrix `isingTransferMatrix1D (p.β * p.J)`. The file packages the normalized Hadamard basis as `isingTransferMatrix1DOrthogonalSpectralData`, proves the eigenvalue relation `transferEigenvalueBot_eq_tanh_mul_top`, transports the explicit top-index spectral window to the one-site layer via `oneSiteLayerSpectralWindow_tanh`, and records flip-evenness of the top Hadamard column. The wrapper `oneSiteLayerBalancedMinGapCertificate_tanh` feeds the concrete parameter `theta = Real.tanh (p.β * p.J)` into the balanced min-gap certificate for `0 < p.β * p.J`. This is deliberately only a one-site bridge: it does not prove a multi-site high-temperature estimate, does not make `theta < 1` sufficient for larger layer state spaces, and does not prove open-slab geometry, thermodynamic limits, or final hyperplane exponential decay. Reference: Glimm--Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304--306; §17.5, pp. 311--312. |
| §17.1 | **Two-site free-layer spectral-window bridge** (Issue #3511) | **Superseded — route obstructed (#4081) and replaced by the §18 cluster-expansion route (#4228, option ii); conditional finite scaffolding only, not an open book gap (the infinite-volume mass gap / no-phase-transition conclusion is Done via #4228/#4255). Partial (two-site positive-temperature physical window)** | `TransferMatrix/TwoSiteFreeLayerSpectralWindow.lean`. Extends the explicit positive-temperature spectral-window route beyond the one-site layer for the free two-site transverse layer `Fin 2`: zero field, empty transverse graph, and identity transition pairs. `layerSymmetricTransferMatrix_fin2_bot_eq_reindex_kronecker` identifies the balanced physical layer matrix with the reindexed Kronecker product `isingTransferMatrix1D (p.β * p.J) ⊗ₖ isingTransferMatrix1D (p.β * p.J)`. The tensor normalized-Hadamard basis is packaged as `twoSiteFreeTransferOrthogonalSpectralData`, with eigenvalues `λ_i λ_j`; `twoSiteFreeTransferSpectralWindow_tanh` proves that every non-top tensor eigenvalue is bounded by `Real.tanh (p.β * p.J)` times the top eigenvalue. The final wrapper `twoSiteFreeLayerBalancedMinGapCertificate_tanh` uses the honest four-state finite-prefactor threshold `Real.tanh (p.β * p.J) < (3 : ℝ)⁻¹`. This is a concrete larger-layer free case: it does not prove the interacting cubic-layer spectral window, an arbitrary finite-layer Walsh diagonalization, `theta < 1` sufficiency for larger state spaces, open-slab geometry, thermodynamic limits, or final hyperplane exponential decay. Reference: Glimm--Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304--306; §17.5, pp. 311--312. |
| §17.1 | **Finite free-layer Walsh bridge** (Issue #3511) | **Done (finite free-layer Walsh spectral window)** | `TransferMatrix/FreeLayerWalshSpectralWindow.lean` (declaration-free facade; the declarations live in `FreeLayerWalshSpectralWindow/{WalshBasisOrthogonality,WalshDiagonalization,PhysicalBridgeSpectralWindow,FlipParityMinGapCertificates}.lean`). Adds the arbitrary finite free-layer Walsh infrastructure. A layer state is encoded by its down-spin set (`layerStateDownSetEquivFinset`), the all-up Walsh index is `freeLayerWalshTop`, and `freeLayerWalshMatrix` has columns `card(LayerState S)^(-1/2) * spinProduct A`. Orthogonality is proved from the existing spin-product cancellation lemmas (`freeLayerWalshMatrix_orthogonal_left/right`). The physical free-layer matrix is the product of independent one-dimensional transfer entries (`freeLayerTransferMatrix`), and `layerSymmetricTransferMatrix_bot_identity_eq_freeLayerTransferMatrix` identifies it with the zero-field, empty-transverse-graph, identity-transition balanced layer matrix. The module proves the arbitrary finite free-layer Walsh diagonalization: `spinProduct_eq_univ_prod_ite` and `sum_layerState_prod_eq_prod_sum_spin` factor the Walsh character sum into one-site rows, `isingTransferMatrix1D_sum_spin` and `isingTransferMatrix1D_sum_spin_sign` compute the top and signed row sums, `freeLayerTransferMatrix_mulVec_spinProduct` and `freeLayerTransferMatrix_mulVec_freeLayerWalshColumn` prove the character-column eigenvector identities, `freeLayerTransferMatrix_mul_freeLayerWalshMatrix` packages the column relation, and `freeLayerTransferMatrix_diagonalizes` gives `T = Q * diagonal λ * Qᵀ`. The spectral-data wrappers (`freeLayerTransferOrthogonalSpectralData`, `freeLayerPhysicalOrthogonalSpectralData`) use this diagonalization directly. The non-top estimate is now proved by `freeLayerWalshEigenvalue_abs_eq_tanh_pow_mul_top_pow` and `freeLayerWalshSpectralWindow_tanh`: for `0 ≤ a`, if the down-spin set is nonempty, then `|λ_A(a)| = Real.tanh a ^ A.card * transferEigenvalueTop a ^ Fintype.card S ≤ Real.tanh a * transferEigenvalueTop a ^ Fintype.card S`. The final wrapper `freeLayerBalancedMinGapCertificate_tanh` feeds this window into the positive-temperature assumptions `p.h = 0`, `0 < p.β * p.J`, `[Nonempty S]`, and the honest finite threshold `Real.tanh (p.β * p.J) < (((2 ^ Fintype.card S - 1 : ℕ) : ℝ))⁻¹`; the conditional wrapper `freeLayerBalancedMinGapCertificate_tanh_of_walshBounds` remains as a reusable entry point. This does not prove the interacting cubic-layer spectral window, `theta < 1` sufficiency for larger state spaces, open-slab geometry, thermodynamic limits, or final hyperplane exponential decay. Reference: Glimm--Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304--306; §17.5, pp. 311--312. |
| §17.1 | **Finite layer-cylinder spectral-decay consumer** (Issue #3511) | **Done (finite periodic cylinder certificate consumer)** | _(scaffolding removed in PR-B1: route obstructed #4081, superseded by §18 #4228/#4255; archived at git tag `archive/transfermatrix-spectral-gap-scaffolding`)_. Consumed existing balanced min-gap certificates as actual project-level finite periodic layer-cylinder correlation bounds. All six of the removed module's declarations went with it, so none of them is a declaration of the current tree, and three of the six were composed from others of the six, so that internal chain went with them as well; every lemma the module composed from outside itself does survive, however, and each of the six was a one-line composition, so the route is recorded here through those external names. Strict positivity of the exponential one-layer and transition weights was `Real.exp_pos` verbatim, and surviving callers discharge that hypothesis inline the same way; the named concrete instances are `cubicLayerInternalWeight_pos` and `cubicLayerTransitionWeight_pos` (`TransferMatrix/CubicLayerCardinalitySmallRatio.lean`). One-layer positivity is the only input needed to transport balanced certificates to ordinary layer traces, so the abstract consumer is the reindexing `layerCylinderSpinTwoPoint_eq_layerSpinTwoPoint` (`TransferMatrix/LayerCylinder.lean`) followed by `layerSpinTwoPoint_abs_le_min_of_balancedMinSpectralGapCertificate` (`TransferMatrix/LayerSpectral/BalancedSpectralGap.lean`), which bounds the concrete cyclic layer-cylinder spin two-point function by `(prefactor / partitionPrefactor) * theta ^ min a b`. Rewriting that bound along the graph-to-layer equality `correlation_layerCylinderGraph_same_transverse_eq_layerCylinderSpinTwoPoint` (`TransferMatrix/LayerCylinderGraph.lean`) gives the same bound for the project-level Gibbs correlation on the finite cylinder graph itself; the cubic specialization needs only the definitional identification of `cubicLayerCylinderGraph` with `layerCylinderGraph`, the step already taken by the surviving `correlation_cubicLayerCylinderGraph_same_transverse_eq_trace_ratio` (`TransferMatrix/CubicLayerCylinder.lean`), and the free-layer Walsh instance is that same composition fed by the surviving certificate `freeLayerBalancedMinGapCertificate_tanh` (`TransferMatrix/FreeLayerWalshSpectralWindow/FlipParityMinGapCertificates.lean`), whose decay rate is `Real.tanh (p.β * p.J)` under the finite Walsh smallness condition. This is still finite and periodic: it does not prove an interacting cubic-layer spectral window, open-slab/free-boundary geometry, thermodynamic limits, or final hyperplane exponential decay. Reference: Glimm--Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304--306; §17.5, pp. 311--312. |
| §17.1 | **Finite open layer-slab transfer bridge** (Issue #3511) | **Done (finite open/free-boundary bridge)** | `TransferMatrix/LayerOpenSlab.lean` and `TransferMatrix/LayerOpenSlabGraph.lean`. | §17.1 | **Finite open-boundary spectral bridge** (Issue #3511) | **Done (finite open denominator bridge)** | `TransferMatrix/LayerOpenSpectral.lean`. The constructors `layerOpenMinSpectralGapCertificate_of_transferBounds` and `layerOpenMinSpectralGapCertificate_of_matrixPartitionBounds` package explicit open transfer bounds into `LayerOpenMinSpectralGapCertificate`, with the latter allowing the denominator lower bound to be proved in boundary-vector matrix-power form while the marked numerator remains the existing open-path numerator input. Reference: Glimm--Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304--306; §17.5, pp. 311--312. |
| §17.1 | **Finite open marked matrix-product expansion** (Issue #3511) | **Done (finite matrix/path bridge)** | `TransferMatrix/LayerOpenSpectral.lean`. Adds the finite open two-insertion matrix expression `layerOpenTwoPointMatrixProductNumerator`, the boundary-vector product `u^T T^left D_f T^sep D_f T^right 1`, and its expanded four-endpoint matrix-power sum `layerOpenTwoPointMatrixPowerNumerator`. The theorem `layerOpenTwoPointMatrixProductNumerator_eq_matrixPower` proves the finite matrix-algebra expansion by `Matrix.mul_apply` and diagonal-matrix simplification. The helper `openMarkedPathTripleNumerator` records the three-open-path expansion with endpoint gluing constraints, and `openMarkedMatrixPowerSum_eq_pathTripleNumerator` proves that the four-endpoint matrix-power sum expands to this three-path form using `pow_apply_eq_sum` and finite-sum reordering. The open-path glue/split helpers then identify the three-path expansion with the existing single open path numerator: `openMarkedPathTripleNumerator_eq_singlePathSum`, `layerOpenTwoPointMatrixPowerNumerator_eq_transferTwoPointNumerator`, and `layerOpenTwoPointMatrixProductNumerator_eq_transferTwoPointNumerator`. This remains a finite path/matrix bridge: it does not prove a marked spectral-basis formula, an interacting cubic-layer spectral window, Perron--Frobenius input, thermodynamic-limit decay, or final hyperplane exponential decay. Reference: Glimm--Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304--306; §17.5, pp. 311--312. |

They reuse `layerOpenMinSpectralGapCertificate_of_matrixPartitionBounds` for the denominator and rewrite the numerator through `layerOpenTwoPointMatrixPowerNumerator_eq_transferTwoPointNumerator` or `layerOpenTwoPointMatrixProductNumerator_eq_transferTwoPointNumerator`. The generic estimate bounds the triple spectral numerator by the finite coefficient prefactor times `scale^(left + sep + right) * theta^sep`, assuming a full eigenvalue scale bound, a subdominant bound off a chosen `top`, and explicit central-channel cancellation through `top`. The open wrappers define `layerOpenBalancedBoundaryVector`, identify the open matrix-product numerator with the balanced boundary-vector marked product (`layerOpenTwoPointMatrixProductNumerator_eq_balancedBoundaryMarkedProduct`), expose the spectral sum (`layerOpenTwoPointMatrixProductNumerator_eq_boundarySpectralSum`), prove the absolute bound (`layerOpenTwoPointMatrixProductNumerator_abs_le_boundarySpectralPrefactor`), and package it into `layerOpenMinSpectralGapCertificate_of_matrixPartition_orthogonalBoundaryDominantBounds`. Reference: Glimm--Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304--306; §17.5, pp. 311--312. |
| §17.1 | **Finite open-boundary spectral denominator bridge** (Issue #3511) | **Superseded — route obstructed (#4081) and replaced by the §18 cluster-expansion route (#4228, option ii); conditional finite scaffolding only, not an open book gap (the infinite-volume mass gap / no-phase-transition conclusion is Done via #4228/#4255). The open wrappers identify `layerOpenMatrixPartition` with the balanced boundary power product (`layerOpenMatrixPartition_eq_balancedBoundaryPower`), expose the spectral sum (`layerOpenMatrixPartition_eq_boundarySpectralSum`), and construct the matrix-partition lower bound via `layerOpenMatrixPartition_lower_of_orthogonalBoundaryDominantBounds`. The constructor `layerOpenMinSpectralGapCertificate_of_orthogonalBoundaryDominantBounds` feeds this denominator bridge into the existing open numerator certificate, while the new boundary spectral denominator consumers in `LayerOpenSpectralDecay.lean` remove the explicit `partition_lower_matrix` and eigenvalue-scale hypotheses from the project-level open-slab and cubic wrappers. This remains finite and conditional: it does not prove Perron--Frobenius existence, positivity of the boundary prefactor, central-channel cancellation, an interacting cubic-layer spectral window, thermodynamic-limit decay, or final hyperplane exponential decay. | §17.1 | **Open-boundary Perron-facing dominance constructors** (Issue #3511) | **Superseded — route obstructed (#4081) and replaced by the §18 cluster-expansion route (#4228, option ii); conditional finite scaffolding only, not an open book gap (the infinite-volume mass gap / no-phase-transition conclusion is Done via #4228/#4255). The generic spectral lemmas `RealOrthogonalSpectralData.boundarySpectralPartitionPrefactor_pos_of_small`, `sign_mul_boundaryCoordinates_pos_of_vectorPositive_signedPositiveColumn`, `boundaryCoordinates_ne_zero_of_vectorPositive_signedPositiveColumn`, and `boundaryCoordinates_sq_pos_of_vectorPositive_signedPositiveColumn` show that a signed-positive dominant column has a nonzero open boundary coordinate and that the explicit smallness condition `theta * ∑_{i≠top} b_i^2 < b_top^2` makes the denominator prefactor positive. The constructors `layerOpenMinSpectralGapCertificate_of_orthogonalSubdominantBounds_signedPositiveColumn`, `layerOpenMinSpectralGapCertificate_of_orthogonalMaxEigenIndex`, and `layerOpenMinSpectralGapCertificate_of_layerOrthogonalSubdominantBounds_signedPositiveColumn` fix the scale to the signed-positive dominant eigenvalue and feed the explicit prefactor smallness into the PR #4037 denominator bridge. The project-level consumers `correlation_layerOpenSlabGraph_same_transverse_abs_le_of_signedPositiveBoundaryDominance` and `correlation_cubicLayerOpenSlabGraph_same_transverse_abs_le_of_signedPositiveBoundaryDominance` expose the resulting finite open-slab/cubic estimates. This remains conditional: it still requires the quantitative boundary-prefactor smallness, the subdominant spectral window, and the central marked-channel cancellation; it does not prove an interacting cubic-layer physical spectral window, thermodynamic-limit decay, or final hyperplane exponential decay. Reference: Glimm--Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304--306; §17.5, pp. 311--312. |
| §17.1 | **Open-boundary flip-parity central cancellation** (Issue #3511) | **Superseded — route obstructed (#4081) and replaced by the §18 cluster-expansion route (#4228, option ii); conditional finite scaffolding only, not an open book gap (the infinite-volume mass gap / no-phase-transition conclusion is Done via #4228/#4255). Partial (conditional parity-adapted open cancellation)** | `TransferMatrix/LayerSpectral.lean` and `TransferMatrix/LayerOpenPerron.lean`. Adds spectral-column parity vocabulary `RealOrthogonalSpectralData.ColumnFlipEven`, `ColumnFlipOdd`, and `ColumnFlipParity`, plus the selection rules in `IsingModel/TransferMatrix/LayerSpectral/FlipParitySelectionRules.lean`: `boundaryCoordinates_zero_of_equiv_even_odd` (an even boundary vector has zero boundary coordinate against an odd column), and `markedMatrix_zero_of_equiv_odd_even_even` together with `markedMatrix_zero_of_equiv_odd_odd_odd` (an odd observable has zero marked-matrix entry between two even columns and between two odd columns). The two same-parity rules are separate statements — the pair of column signs cancels in the odd–odd case and is absent in the even–even one, and no vanishing is claimed for a mixed even–odd entry — and `ColumnFlipParity` is what says every column falls into one of the two sectors, so the two rules between them cover every same-parity entry. The generic theorem `boundaryMarkedCentral_zero_of_equiv_evenBoundary_columnParity` proves the open central-channel cancellation coefficientwise when the top column is flip-even and the chosen orthogonal spectral basis is adapted to the flip parity. The open wrapper `layerOpenBoundaryMarkedCentral_zero_of_layerSpinAt_flipParity` specializes this to `layerSpinAt`, and the constructors `layerOpenMinGapCert_of_subdominant_signedPositiveFlipParitySpin` and `layerOpenMinGapCert_of_layerSubdominant_signedPositiveFlipParitySpin` discharge the `central_dominant_channel_zero` input in the signed-positive open route from zero field, flip-invariant weights, and the parity-adapted basis hypothesis. The project-level consumers `correlation_layerOpenSlabGraph_same_transverse_abs_le_of_signedPositiveFlipParity` and `correlation_cubicLayerOpenSlabGraph_same_transverse_abs_le_of_signedPositiveFlipParity` expose the finite open-slab/cubic estimates. This deliberately does not claim that top-column flip-evenness alone suffices, does not prove that arbitrary Hermitian spectral-theorem data is parity-adapted, and still leaves boundary-prefactor smallness, the subdominant spectral window, thermodynamic limits, and final hyperplane exponential decay explicit or open. Reference: Glimm--Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304--306; §17.5, pp. 311--312. |

| §17.1 | **Infinite-temperature open physical norm-window route** (Issue #3511) | **Superseded — route obstructed (#4081) and replaced by the §18 cluster-expansion route (#4228, option ii); conditional finite scaffolding only, not an open book gap (the infinite-volume mass gap / no-phase-transition conclusion is Done via #4228/#4255). Partial (beta-zero finite physical window)** | _(scaffolding removed in PR-B1: route obstructed #4081, superseded by §18 #4228/#4255; archived at git tag `archive/transfermatrix-spectral-gap-scaffolding`)_. Closed the finite physical open-boundary norm-window inequality at the infinite-temperature slice `p.β = 0`. History only, like the beta-zero spectral-window row above: the module's declarations went with it, they belong to the same all-ones-matrix family that has no occurrence left in `IsingModel/` under any name, and none of the names it introduced is a declaration of the current tree, so none of them is cited here; the proofs survive only in the archived tag. The base objects it evaluated do survive — the balanced open boundary vector `layerOpenBalancedBoundaryVector` (`TransferMatrix/LayerOpenSpectral/SpectralForm.lean`) and the cap `layerOpenPhysicalBoundaryNormWindowCap` (`TransferMatrix/LayerOpenPhysicalNormWindow.lean`) — but their beta-zero evaluations do not. Its constant-vector helpers identified the square of the maximal all-ones boundary coordinate with `Fintype.card Ω`; its physical lemmas made the balanced open boundary vector constant one, computed the one-layer internal partition sum as the number of layer states, and hence showed the physical norm-window cap to be exactly `1`, which combined with the existing canonical Perron ratio `< 1` closed the input, with cubic specializations recording the same cap and ratio conclusions for cubic transverse boxes. This was only a finite `β = 0` quantitative input: it did not prove a high-temperature neighborhood, an interacting cubic-layer spectral window, arbitrary degenerate-eigenspace parity adaptation, thermodynamic-limit passage, or final hyperplane decay. Reference: Glimm--Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304--306; §17.5, pp. 311--312. |
| §17.1 | **High-temperature open physical norm-window bridge** (Issue #3511) | **Superseded — route obstructed (#4081) and replaced by the §18 cluster-expansion route (#4228, option ii); conditional finite scaffolding only, not an open book gap (the infinite-volume mass gap / no-phase-transition conclusion is Done via #4228/#4255). Turned the beta-zero strict physical norm-window inequality into a finite high-temperature bridge under explicit scalar continuity hypotheses for a chosen spectral-data family. History only: the module's declarations went with it and none of the names it introduced is a declaration of the current tree, so none is cited here; the proofs survive only in the archived tag. The two scalars it compared do survive — `subdominantRatio_maxEigenIndex` (`TransferMatrix/LayerPerronExistence/OrthogonalSpectralData.lean`) and `layerOpenPhysicalBoundaryNormWindowCap` (`TransferMatrix/LayerOpenPhysicalNormWindow.lean`) — and the persistence step itself was elementary mathlib order-topology (`Filter.Tendsto.eventually_lt` and neighbourhood unwrapping), but the strict `β = 0` seed inequality it consumed was removed with the same beta-zero cluster and has no successor, so nothing in the current tree carries this bridge. Its generic theorems stated that if the canonical max-index ratio and physical cap tend to their beta-zero values, then the strict inequality holds eventually near `β = 0`, on an absolute-value neighborhood, and on some one-sided interval `0 ≤ β ≤ βmax`, with cubic-transverse-layer specializations. The spectral-data continuity, eigenbasis perturbation theory, `maxEigenIndex` stability, simple-eigenspace/parity input, concrete interacting cubic-layer spectral window, thermodynamic-limit passage, and final hyperplane decay remain open. Reference: Glimm--Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304--306; §17.5, pp. 311--312. |

Those pointwise constructors do survive and are what a reconstruction would use: `layerOpenMinGapCert_of_layerMaxEigenIndexSimpleParityCanonicalRatioPhysicalNormWindow`, `correlation_layerOpenSlabGraph_same_transverse_abs_le_of_physicalNormWindow_simpleParity`, and `correlation_cubicLayerOpenSlabGraph_same_transverse_abs_le_of_physicalNormWindow_simpleParity` (`TransferMatrix/LayerOpenParitySimple.lean`); what does not survive is the interval statement itself, since the removed bridge's `β = 0` seed is gone with the beta-zero cluster. The pointwise consumers it fed do survive: `layerOpenMinGapCert_of_layerSubdominant_signedPositiveSimpleParitySpin_boundaryWindow`, `correlation_layerOpenSlabGraph_abs_le_of_signedPositiveSimpleParity_boundaryWindow`, and `correlation_cubicLayerOpenSlabGraph_abs_le_of_signedPositiveSimpleParityBoundaryWindow` (`TransferMatrix/LayerOpenBoundaryWindowSimple.lean`); only the interval-local packaging around them is gone. This did not prove spectral-data continuity, eigenbasis perturbation, `maxEigenIndex` stability, concrete local simplicity or signed positivity, a concrete interacting cubic-layer spectral window, thermodynamic-limit passage, or final hyperplane decay. Reference: Glimm--Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304--306; §17.5, pp. 311--312. |
| §17.1 | **High-temperature canonical boundary-window simple-parity consumers** (Issue #3511) | **Superseded — route obstructed (#4081) and replaced by the §18 cluster-expansion route (#4228, option ii); conditional finite scaffolding only, not an open book gap (the infinite-volume mass gap / no-phase-transition conclusion is Done via #4228/#4255). Partial (canonical finite high-temperature consumers)** | _(scaffolding removed in PR-B1: route obstructed #4081, superseded by §18 #4228/#4255; archived at git tag `archive/transfermatrix-spectral-gap-scaffolding`)_. Specialized the local high-temperature boundary-window route to `top := maxEigenIndex` and the canonical ratio `subdominantRatio_maxEigenIndex` (`TransferMatrix/LayerPerronExistence/OrthogonalSpectralData.lean`). History only: the module's declarations went with it and none of the names it introduced is a declaration of the current tree, so none is cited here; the proofs survive only in the archived tag. Its beta-zero strict seed was proved from the physical norm-window seed and the physical-to-boundary cap bridge, and its scalar wrappers exposed the resulting high-temperature boundary-window interval under `ContinuousAt` hypotheses for the canonical ratio and cap. That seed has no successor, because the strict `β = 0` physical norm-window inequality it consumed was removed with the beta-zero cluster; what does survive is the boundary cap `layerOpenBoundarySpectralWindowCap` (`TransferMatrix/LayerOpenBoundaryWindow.lean`), the physical-to-boundary cap bridge `theta_lt_layerOpenBoundarySpectralWindowCap_of_lt_physicalNormWindowCap` (`TransferMatrix/LayerOpenPhysicalNormWindow.lean`), and the pointwise constructor its interval-local wrappers delegated to, `layerOpenMinGapCert_of_layerMaxEigenIndexSimpleParitySpin_boundaryWindow` (`TransferMatrix/LayerOpenBoundaryWindowSimple.lean`); only the interval-local packaging around them is gone. That pointwise constructor uses existing max-index nonnegativity and eigenvalue-bound accessors, so the remaining local user input was only `ColumnSimpleEigenspaces` (`TransferMatrix/LayerOpenParitySimple.lean`). This does not prove spectral-data continuity, perturbative stability of the chosen spectral data, concrete local simple eigenspaces, a concrete interacting cubic-layer spectral window, thermodynamic-limit passage, or final hyperplane exponential decay. Reference: Glimm--Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304--306; §17.5, pp. 311--312. |
| §17.1 | **Simple-spectrum canonical boundary-window consumers** (Issue #3511) | **Superseded — route obstructed (#4081) and replaced by the §18 cluster-expansion route (#4228, option ii); conditional finite scaffolding only, not an open book gap (the infinite-volume mass gap / no-phase-transition conclusion is Done via #4228/#4255). Partial (finite simple-spectrum parity input)** | `TransferMatrix/LayerOpenSimpleSpectrum.lean` and _(scaffolding removed in PR-B1: route obstructed #4081, superseded by §18 #4228/#4255; archived at git tag `archive/transfermatrix-spectral-gap-scaffolding`)_. Replaces the remaining local `ColumnSimpleEigenspaces` input in the canonical high-temperature boundary-window route by the more elementary, checkable simple-spectrum hypothesis. `RealOrthogonalSpectralData.SimpleSpectrum` is defined as `Function.Injective E.eigenvalue`, and `columnSimpleEigenspaces_of_simpleSpectrum` proves that simple spectrum implies columnwise simple eigenspaces by expanding an eigenvector in the orthogonal spectral basis: the eigenvector equation forces every spectral coordinate whose eigenvalue differs from the selected column to vanish, and injectivity leaves only the selected column. The wrappers `columnFlipParity_of_commuting_involution_simpleSpectrum` and `layerSymmetricTransfer_columnFlipParity_of_simpleSpectrum` reuse the existing column-simple parity bridge. Those four declarations are the surviving part of this row and all live in `TransferMatrix/LayerOpenSimpleSpectrum.lean`. The consumer layer built on them is history only: its interval-local open-slab and cubic open-slab variants took local punctured `SimpleSpectrum` (on a punctured one-sided interval, a punctured absolute-value neighborhood, or eventually near but away from `β = 0`) instead of `ColumnSimpleEigenspaces` and delegated to the canonical boundary-window simple-parity consumers after the conversion, but those consumers were themselves removed with the canonical boundary-window cluster of the row above, so none of the names that layer introduced is a declaration of the current tree and none is cited here; the proofs survive only in the archived tag. This does not prove spectral-data continuity, perturbative stability, that an interacting cubic-layer family has a simple spectrum, a concrete interacting spectral window, thermodynamic-limit passage, or final hyperplane exponential decay. Reference: Glimm--Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304--306; §17.5, pp. 311--312. |
| §17.1 | **One-site open boundary-window discharge** (Issue #3511) | **Done (first concrete simple-spectrum open-boundary discharge)** | `TransferMatrix/OneSiteLayerOpenBoundaryWindow.lean`. Gives the first concrete discharge of the simple-spectrum open-boundary boundary-window stack for an actual transfer matrix. The one-site transverse layer (`S = PUnit`, internal graph `⊥`, identity longitudinal transition) is the genuine 2×2 one-dimensional Ising transfer matrix. `isingTransferMatrix1DOrthogonalSpectralData_simpleSpectrum` and `oneSiteLayerOrthogonalSpectralData_simpleSpectrum` prove the spectrum is simple because `transferEigenvalueBot = tanh(βJ)·transferEigenvalueTop < transferEigenvalueTop` (`transferEigenvalueBot_lt_top`). `oneSiteLayerOrthogonalSpectralData_top_signedPositiveColumn` orients the constant `1/√2` top Hadamard column as signed-positive, `oneSiteLayerOrthogonalSpectralData_boundaryCoordinates_nonTop_zero` shows the off-top boundary coordinate of the constant balanced boundary vector vanishes, hence `oneSiteLayerOrthogonalSpectralData_boundaryCoordinateRestSq_zero` and `oneSiteLayerBoundarySpectralWindowCap_eq_one` give boundary-window cap `1`. Feeding these through the PR #4057 `columnSimpleEigenspaces_of_simpleSpectrum` bridge and the explicit signed-positive simple-parity boundary-window consumer, `correlation_oneSiteLayerOpenSlabGraph_abs_le_of_simpleSpectrum` gives an unconditional finite one-site open-slab same-transverse-site bound with decay parameter `tanh(βJ)` for `0 < βJ`. The sharp coefficient-one form of the same one-site open-slab inequality already exists as the `S = PUnit` specialization of `correlation_freeLayerOpenSlabGraph_same_transverse_abs_le_tanh_clean`; this file instead contributes the first concrete witness that the abstract simple-spectrum boundary-window discharge fires on a real longitudinal transfer matrix. This does not prove an interacting transverse-layer spectral window, a thermodynamic limit, or final hyperplane exponential decay. Reference: Glimm--Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304--306; §17.5, pp. 311--312. |
| §17.1 | **Two-site interacting layer spectral data** (Issue #3511) | **Done (explicit interacting transverse-edge diagonalization)** | `TransferMatrix/TwoSiteInteractingLayerSpectralData.lean`. Constructs the first *interacting* transverse-edge layer transfer matrix and its explicit orthogonal diagonalization, the first case the free Walsh route cannot diagonalize. The layer is `S = Fin 2` with internal graph `completeGraph (Fin 2)` (one transverse edge) and identity longitudinal transition; `layerInternalWeight_completeGraph_fin2` and `layerSymmetricTransferMatrix_fin2_complete_eq_reindex_twoSiteInteracting` identify the balanced layer matrix with the explicit `4×4` matrix `twoSiteInteractingTransferMatrix`. The even spin sector mixes the two constant/antidiagonal modes through a square-root rotation: with `A = e^{3a}+e^{-a}`, `B = e^{a}+e^{-3a}`, `Δ = A−B`, `rad = √(Δ²+16)`, the rotation `c = √((rad+Δ)/(2 rad))`, `s = √((rad−Δ)/(2 rad))` satisfies `c²+s²=1`, `c·s·rad=2`, and the eigenvector identities `c·(rad−Δ)=4 s`, `s·(rad+Δ)=4 c` (`twoSiteK2_rotC_mul`, `twoSiteK2_rotS_mul`, proved by squaring and the nonnegativity of both sides). The four eigenvalues are `top, evenBot = (A+B ± rad)/2`, `flipOdd = e^{3a}−e^{-a}`, `swapOdd = e^{a}−e^{-3a}` (`twoSiteInteractingTransferEigenvalue`). `twoSiteInteractingChangeOfBasis` is orthogonal in both directions, the columns are eigenvectors (`twoSiteInteractingTransferMatrix_mul_changeOfBasis`), and `twoSiteInteractingTransferMatrix_diagonalizes` packages `M = Q diag(λ) Qᵀ`, yielding `RealOrthogonalSpectralData` for both the abstract matrix (`twoSiteInteractingTransferOrthogonalSpectralData`) and the physical zero-field layer (`twoSiteInteractingLayerOrthogonalSpectralData`). This file only packages explicit finite spectral data; it does not prove simple spectrum, signed positivity, the open-boundary decay discharge, a thermodynamic limit, or final hyperplane decay. Reference: Glimm--Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304--306; §17.5, pp. 311--312. |
| §17.1 | **Two-site interacting layer simple spectrum and spectral window** (Issue #3511) | **Done (interacting simple spectrum + signed-positive dominant column + spectral window)** | `TransferMatrix/TwoSiteInteractingLayerSpectralWindow.lean`. Supplies the structural inputs of the open-boundary simple-parity route for the interacting `K2` layer of the preceding entry. The four eigenvalues are strictly ordered `top > flipOdd > swapOdd > evenBot > 0` for `0 < βJ`: `twoSiteK2Top_gt_FlipOdd` (via `rad ≥ Δ`), `twoSiteK2FlipOdd_gt_SwapOdd` (via `flipOdd = e^{2a}·swapOdd`), and the determinant relation `twoSiteK2_det` (`top·evenBot = flipOdd·swapOdd`, from `rad²=Δ²+16` and `e^{3a}e^{-3a}=e^{a}e^{-a}=1`) give `twoSiteK2EvenBot_pos` and `twoSiteK2SwapOdd_gt_EvenBot`. Hence `twoSiteInteractingTransferOrthogonalSpectralData_simpleSpectrum` and its physical-layer wrapper `twoSiteInteractingLayerOrthogonalSpectralData_simpleSpectrum`. The dominant rotation column `(c,s,s,c)/√2` is strictly positive (`twoSiteK2RotC_pos`, `twoSiteK2RotS_pos` from `rad > |Δ|`), giving signed-positive top columns for the abstract matrix and the physical layer. The decay parameter `twoSiteInteractingTheta = flipOdd/top` satisfies `theta < 1` (`twoSiteInteractingTheta_lt_one`) and `theta · top = flipOdd`, and the spectral window `twoSiteInteractingLayerSpectralWindow_theta` bounds every non-top eigenvalue by `theta · top` in absolute value. This file supplies the `SimpleSpectrum`, signed-positive, and subdominant-window inputs; the open boundary-window cap and the open-slab decay discharge are a later step (the interacting balanced boundary vector is not constant, so the cap argument differs from the one-site/free cases). It does not prove the open-slab decay bound, a thermodynamic limit, or final hyperplane decay. Reference: Glimm--Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304--306; §17.5, pp. 311--312. |
| §17.1 | **Two-site interacting open boundary-window discharge** (Issue #3511) | **Done (first interacting transverse-edge open-slab decay)** | `TransferMatrix/TwoSiteInteractingLayerOpenBoundaryWindow.lean`. Completes the interacting `K2` open-boundary discharge, the first unconditional finite open-slab same-transverse-site decay for a genuinely interacting transverse-edge layer. The balanced boundary vector `v(ω) = sqrt(internalWeight ω) = exp((βJ/2)·s(ω0)s(ω1))` is not constant but is flip-even (`twoSiteInteractingLayer_boundaryVector_flip_even`); the two odd rotation columns are `ColumnFlipOdd` (`twoSiteInteractingLayer_flipOdd_columnFlipOdd`, `..._swapOdd_columnFlipOdd`), so their boundary coordinates vanish (`boundaryCoordinates_zero_of_equiv_even_odd`). Only the even-bottom coordinate survives, so by Parseval (`sum_boundaryCoordinates_sq`) the off-top boundary mass is `vectorSqNorm − coord_top²` (`twoSiteInteractingLayer_boundaryCoordinateRestSq_eq`). The explicit top coordinate `√2(c·e^{βJ/2}+s·e^{-βJ/2})` (`twoSiteInteractingLayer_boundaryCoordinates_top`) and `vectorSqNorm = 2(e^{βJ}+e^{-βJ})` satisfy `vectorSqNorm ≤ 2·coord_top²` (the difference reduces to `(Δ·sinh(βJ)+4)/rad > 0` via the rotation identities and `Δ > 0` for `βJ > 0`), so the off-top mass is at most `coord_top²`, the boundary-window cap is `1`, and it strictly exceeds `theta` (`twoSiteInteractingLayer_theta_lt_cap`). Feeding the #4060 simple-spectrum / signed-positive / spectral-window inputs and the #4057 `columnSimpleEigenspaces_of_simpleSpectrum` bridge through `correlation_layerOpenSlabGraph_abs_le_of_signedPositiveSimpleParity_boundaryWindow`, `correlation_twoSiteInteractingLayerOpenSlabGraph_abs_le_of_simpleSpectrum` gives the unconditional finite interacting open-slab bound in prefactor form with decay parameter `theta = flipOdd/top` for `0 < βJ`. It does not prove a closed-form decay rate, a thermodynamic limit, or final hyperplane decay. Reference: Glimm--Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304--306; §17.5, pp. 311--312. |
| §17.1 | **Two-site interacting open strip mass decay** (Issue #3511) | **Done (mass-form interacting open-slab decay)** | `TransferMatrix/TwoSiteInteractingLayerOpenDecay.lean`. Packages the preceding interacting `K2` open-slab decay in mass form, matching the `exp(-m·sep)` language of §17.5 and the free-layer wrappers. The decay parameter `twoSiteInteractingTheta a = flipOdd/top` is positive (`twoSiteInteractingTheta_pos`) and strictly below `1`, so the decay mass `twoSiteInteractingMass a = -log(twoSiteInteractingTheta a) = log(top/flipOdd)` (`twoSiteInteractingMass_eq_log_top_div_flipOdd`) is positive (`twoSiteInteractingMass_pos`), and `twoSiteInteractingTheta a ^ n = exp(-(twoSiteInteractingMass a)·n)` (`twoSiteInteractingTheta_pow_eq_exp_neg_mass`, via `Real.exp_log` and `Real.exp_nat_mul`). Rewriting the #4061 bound, `correlation_twoSiteInteractingLayerOpenSlabGraph_abs_le_exp_neg_mass` gives the finite interacting open-slab same-transverse-site bound as `(prefactor)·exp(-(mass)·sep)` for `0 < βJ`. This is finite: it does not prove a closed-form rate beyond `-log θ`, a thermodynamic limit, or final hyperplane decay. Reference: Glimm--Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304--306; §17.5, pp. 311--312. |
| §17.1 | **Two-site interacting open slab as a lattice strip** (Issue #3511) | **Done (interacting open-slab ↔ induced lattice-strip correlation transport)** | `TransferMatrix/TwoSiteInteractingOpenStripTransport.lean`. Identifies the interacting `K2` open-slab graph `layerOpenSlabGraph (completeGraph (Fin 2)) (layerIdentityTransitionPairs (Fin 2)) n` with the induced finite volume of the ambient nearest-neighbour `latticeGraph 2` on a two-row strip `twoSiteOpenStrip n = {z : Fin 2 → ℤ | 0 ≤ z 0 ≤ n ∧ 0 ≤ z 1 ≤ 1}` (longitudinal layer coordinate `z 0`, transverse rung `z 1`). Unlike the free-layer transport (`FreeLayerWalshOpenInfiniteVolume`, longitudinal-only axis graph), the interacting layer has a transverse edge, so the ambient graph is the full `latticeGraph 2`: `twoSiteOpenSlabGraph_adj_iff` shows the open-slab adjacency is a transverse rung (same layer, distinct transverse sites, from `completeGraph`) or a longitudinal nearest-neighbour step (consecutive layers, same transverse site, from the identity transition), and `latticeGraph_two_strip_adj_iff` matches this with `∑_i |z_a i − z_b i| = 1` on the strip points. `twoSiteOpenStripEquiv` is the site equivalence, `twoSiteOpenSlabGraph_map_stripEquiv` the graph identification, and `correlation_induced_latticeGraph_two_strip_eq_openSlab` (with its absolute-value form) transports finite interacting open-slab correlations into the project's induced lattice-graph correlation form (mirroring the free-layer `correlation_induced_freeLayerAxisGraph_cubicBox_eq_openSlab`). This is a finite graph identification: it does not pass to a thermodynamic limit or prove final hyperplane exponential decay. Reference: Glimm--Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304--306; §17.5, pp. 311--312. |
| §17.1 | **Two-site interacting open strip decay on the ambient lattice** (Issue #3511) | **Done (interacting decay on induced latticeGraph-2 strip)** | `TransferMatrix/TwoSiteInteractingOpenStripDecay.lean`. Transports the finite interacting `K2` open-slab decay bounds (#4061/#4062) onto the induced finite volume of the ambient `latticeGraph 2` on `twoSiteOpenStrip`, using the #4063 correlation transport. The helper `abs_correlation_induced_latticeGraph_two_strip_le_of_openSlab` carries any finite open-slab correlation bound across the equivalence. The two correlated endpoints become the strip points `![(left : ℤ), (x : ℤ)]` and `![(left + sep : ℤ), (x : ℤ)]` (`twoSiteOpenStripPoint_left`, `twoSiteOpenStripPoint_right`), with the transported observable `twoSiteInteractingOpenStripTwoPoint`. The transported bounds `correlation_induced_latticeGraph_two_strip_abs_le_of_simpleSpectrum` (prefactor·θ^sep) and `correlation_induced_latticeGraph_two_strip_abs_le_exp_neg_mass` ((prefactor)·exp(-(mass)·sep), `m = -log(flipOdd/top)`) hold at zero field (`h = 0`) for `0 < βJ`, now stated directly for `correlation (Ambient.inducedGraph (latticeGraph 2) (twoSiteOpenStrip (left+sep+right))) p (transported two-point)`. This is finite: it does not pass to a thermodynamic limit or prove final hyperplane decay. Reference: Glimm--Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304--306; §17.5, pp. 311--312. |
| §17.1 | **Finite arbitrary transverse layer open-slab decay via Hermitian spectral data** (Issue #3511) | **Done (generic finite transverse layer, conditional on boundary-window gap + parity)** | `TransferMatrix/LayerOpenFiniteTransverseHermitian.lean`. Generalizes the open-boundary same-transverse-site decay consumer from the closed-form `Fin 2` (`K2`) transverse fiber to an **arbitrary finite transverse layer** `S`. The spectral data is supplied generically by the real spectral theorem (`finiteTransverseHermitianData := layerSymmetricTransferOrthogonalSpectralData …`, requiring only transition-weight symmetry `hk_symm`), and the decay parameter is fixed to the canonical maximal-index subdominant ratio `finiteTransverseHermitianRatio := (…).subdominantRatio_maxEigenIndex …` (with accessors `finiteTransverseHermitianRatio_lt_one`, `_nonneg`). Because the balanced transfer matrix is entrywise positive (`finiteTransverseHermitian_entrywisePositive`), the maximal column is automatically signed-positive and the canonical ratio automatically bounds every non-maximal eigenvalue in absolute value, so (beyond the zero-field condition `h = 0`) the only remaining user inputs are the boundary-window gap (`hwindow`) and a columnwise-simple-eigenspace (`hsimple`) or simple-spectrum (`hsimple_spectrum`) parity input. Headlines `correlation_layerOpenSlabGraph_abs_le_of_hermitianCanonicalRatioSimpleParityWindow` and `…SimpleSpectrumWindow` (at zero field `h = 0`) give `\|correlation (layerOpenSlabGraph H transitionPairs (left+sep+right)) p {endpoints}\| ≤ (boundaryMarkedSpectralPrefactor / boundarySpectralPartitionPrefactor)·ratio^sep`, and `correlation_cubicLayerOpenSlabGraph_abs_le_of_hermitianCanonicalRatioSimpleParityWindow` specializes to the cubic transverse layer `cubicLayerGraph d R`. This is finite and conditional on the boundary-window gap and parity input: it does not construct the window uniformly, prove a thermodynamic limit, or prove final hyperplane exponential decay. Reference: Glimm--Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304--306; §17.5, pp. 311--312. |
| §17.1 | **Cubic transverse open slab as an ambient lattice box** (Issue #3511) | **Done (interacting cubic decay on induced latticeGraph (d+1) box)** | `TransferMatrix/CubicLayerOpenBoxTransport.lean`. Identifies the cubic open slab graph `cubicLayerOpenSlabGraph d R n` with the induced finite volume of the ambient `latticeGraph (d+1)` on the longitudinal box `cubicLayerOpenBox d R n` (longitudinal `0 ≤ z 0 ≤ n` in coordinate `0`, transverse cube `[-R,R]^d` in coordinates `1..d`), generalizing the `Fin 2` two-row strip transport (#4063/#4064) to a transverse cube of any dimension. Unlike the free-layer transport, the cubic transverse layer carries genuine transverse nearest-neighbour edges, so the ambient graph is the **full** `latticeGraph (d+1)` (transverse lattice edges *and* longitudinal rungs). General reusable edge lemmas `layerOpenSlab_internal_mem` (`s(a,b) ∈ internal ↔ a.1 = b.1 ∧ H.Adj a.2 b.2`), `layerOpenSlab_identityTransition_mem`, and `layerOpenSlabIdentityGraph_adj_iff` (stated for an arbitrary finite transverse layer `(S,H)`), plus the cubic equivalence `cubicLayerOpenBoxEquiv`, the ambient-adjacency match `latticeGraph_cubicLayerOpenBox_adj_iff` (via `Fin.cons` / `Fin.sum_univ_succ` ℓ¹ splitting), the graph-map `cubicLayerOpenSlabGraph_map_boxEquiv`, and the correlation transport `correlation_induced_latticeGraph_cubicLayerOpenBox_eq_openSlab` (+ abs form). The headline `correlation_cubicLayerOpenBox_abs_le_of_hermitianCanonicalRatioSimpleParityWindow` transports the `LayerOpenFiniteTransverseHermitian` cubic decay onto `Ambient.inducedGraph (latticeGraph (d+1)) (cubicLayerOpenBox d R (left+sep+right))` for the observable `cubicLayerOpenBoxTwoPoint`, with the transition-weight symmetry discharged by `cubicLayerTransitionWeight_symm`, at zero field, conditional only on the boundary-window gap and a columnwise-simple-eigenspace parity input. This is finite: it does not pass to a thermodynamic limit or prove final hyperplane exponential decay. Reference: Glimm--Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304--306; §17.5, pp. 311--312. |
| §17.1 | **Mass-form decay on the ambient cubic open box** (Issue #3511) | **Done (`exp(−mass·sep)` form of the cubic box decay)** | `TransferMatrix/CubicLayerOpenBoxMass.lean`. Recasts the Phase-6 cubic open-box decay (`correlation_induced_latticeGraph_cubicLayerOpenBox_abs_le_of_...`, `CubicLayerOpenBoxTransport.lean`) in the mass form `exp(−mass·sep)`, mirroring the K2 mass form `correlation_induced_latticeGraph_two_strip_abs_le_exp_neg_mass`. `cubicLayerHermitianMass d R p := −log(cubicLayerHermitianRatio d R p)`; `cubicLayerHermitianMass_pos` (`0 < mass` when `0 < ratio`, since `0 ≤ ratio < 1`); `cubicLayerHermitianRatio_pow_eq_exp_neg_mass` (`ratio^n = exp(−mass·n)` for `0 < ratio`, via `Real.exp_nat_mul` + `Real.exp_log`). The headline `correlation_induced_latticeGraph_cubicLayerOpenBox_abs_le_exp_neg_mass` (zero field, plus the hypotheses `0 < ratio` and the same boundary-window gap + columnwise-simple-eigenspace parity inputs): `|correlation (inducedGraph (latticeGraph (d+1)) (cubicLayerOpenBox d R (left+sep+right))) p (cubicLayerOpenBoxTwoPoint d R x left sep right)| ≤ prefactor · exp(−(cubicLayerHermitianMass d R p)·sep)` (built through the shorter-named open-slab theorem + transport to stay warning-free). Finite and conditional on the boundary-window gap + parity; does not pass to a thermodynamic limit. Since the canonical subdominant ratio is only known to be `0 ≤ ratio < 1`, the strict positivity `0 < ratio` is taken as a hypothesis. Reference: Glimm–Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304–306; §17.5, pp. 311–312. |
| §17.1 | **Mass-form cubic open-box decay, explicit ratio** (Issue #3511) | **Done (`exp(−mass·sep)` form with the explicit subdominant ratio)** | `TransferMatrix/CubicLayerOpenBoxExplicitMass.lean`. The explicit-ratio companion of the canonical mass form (#4138): recasts the explicit-ratio cubic open-box decay (`correlation_cubicLayerOpenBox_abs_le_of_hermitianExplicitRatio...`, `LayerOpenExplicitSubdominantRatio.lean`) in the mass form `exp(−mass·sep)`, `mass = −log(explicit ratio)`. Since the explicit ratio is controlled directly by any quantitative eigenvalue estimate, this is the **mass-form consumer a transverse-volume-uniform high-temperature estimate will feed**. `cubicLayerHermitianExplicitMass`, `cubicLayerHermitianExplicitMass_pos` (`0 < mass` when `0 < ratio`, via `cubicLayerHermitianExplicitRatio_lt_one`), `cubicLayerHermitianExplicitRatio_pow_eq_exp_neg_mass` (`ratio^n = exp(−mass·n)` for `0 < ratio`), and the headline `correlation_induced_latticeGraph_cubicLayerOpenBox_abs_le_exp_neg_explicitMass` (zero field, + `0 < ratio` and the boundary-window gap + columnwise-simple-eigenspace parity inputs; built through the explicit-ratio open-slab theorem + transport). Finite and conditional; does not construct the uniform window or pass to a thermodynamic limit. Reference: Glimm–Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304–306; §17.5, pp. 311–312. |
| §17.1 | **Uniform stage bound ⟹ infinite-volume correlation bound** (Issue #3511) | **Done (general bridge lemma for transfer-matrix infinite-volume consumers)** | `AmbientLattice/CorrelationInfinite/Bounds.lean`. `abs_correlationInfinite_le_of_forall_abs_correlationAlongExhaustion_le`: if every exhaustion stage satisfies `|correlationAlongExhaustion G Λ p A N| ≤ C`, then `|correlationInfinite G Λ p A| ≤ C` (the supremum is `≤ C` by `ciSup_le`, and `≥ −C` from the stage-`0` lower bound via `le_ciSup` + `correlationAlongExhaustion_bddAbove`). This generalizes the unconditional `abs_correlationInfinite_le_one` (the `C = 1` case) and is the **general bridge from any uniform finite-volume decay bound to the infinite-volume correlation** — the reusable tool that the transfer-matrix infinite-volume consumers (cubic open-box decay, etc.) feed their per-stage decay bounds into. Reference: Glimm–Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304–306. |
| §17.1 | **Infinite-volume `K2` strip exponential decay** (Issue #3511, #4141) | **Done (first interacting infinite-volume hyperplane decay; unconditional, fixed transverse width 2)** | `TransferMatrix/TwoSiteInteractingOpenStripInfiniteVolume.lean`. The interacting infinite-volume capstone of the `K2` (fixed transverse width 2) programme: passes the length-uniform finite `K2` open-strip decay (`correlation_induced_latticeGraph_two_strip_abs_le_exp_neg_mass`, #4064) to `correlationInfinite` on the ambient `2 × ∞` strip lattice `ℤ × Fin 2`, via the #4140 bridge. Unlike the conditional cubic box decay (#4138/#4139, which needs the obstructed transverse-volume-uniform window), the `K2` window + simple spectrum are discharged unconditionally in closed form, so this decay is **unconditional** (only `h = 0`, `0 < βJ`). **Strip graph** `stripGraph : SimpleGraph (ℤ × Fin 2)` (`Adj (z,s) (z',s') ↔ (z = z' ∧ s ≠ s') ∨ (\|z − z'\| = 1 ∧ s = s')`: transverse rung + longitudinal step) with a **centred strip exhaustion** `stripExhaustion` (`stripBox N = Icc (−N) N ×ˢ univ`). **Box ≅ strip isomorphism** `stripBoxEquiv : ↑(stripBox N) ≃ ↑(twoSiteOpenStrip (2N))` (the shift `(z,s) ↦ ![z + N, s]`) and `stripGraph_induce_map_eq`: `(inducedGraph stripGraph (stripBox N)).map stripBoxEquiv = inducedGraph (latticeGraph 2) (twoSiteOpenStrip (2N))` (both adjacencies reduce to `\|z − z'\| + \|s − s'\| = 1` on `Fin 2`). The length-general explicit-pair bound `strip_pair_abs_le` (proved by `obtain ⟨r, rfl⟩ : ∃ r, M = c + sep + r`, dissolving the Nat-subtraction dependent type) feeds the per-stage bound `abs_correlationAlongExhaustion_stripGraph_axis_le` (a stage either contains both endpoints — transport via `correlation_map_equiv` to the finite strip decay — or the correlation is `0 ≤ prefactor·exp`), and the headline `abs_correlationInfinite_stripGraph_axis_le`: `\|correlationInfinite stripGraph stripExhaustion p (stripAxisTwoPoint x sep)\| ≤ k2StripPrefactor p hp x · exp(−(twoSiteInteractingMass (βJ))·sep)` for `sep > 0`, `h = 0`, `0 < βJ` — the **first genuinely infinite-volume interacting exponential decay in the project** (fixed transverse width 2; a `2 × ∞` strip rather than a full hyperplane), with rate `m = −log(flipOdd/top)` independent of the strip length. Not subject to the #4081 transverse-volume-uniform spectral-gap obstruction because the transverse width is fixed at 2. Reference: Glimm--Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304--306; §17.5, pp. 311--312. |
| §17.1 / §17.5 | **`K2` strip infinite-volume exponential clustering + axis summability** (Issue #3511) | **Done (clustering → 0 + geometric directional susceptibility bound)** | `TransferMatrix/TwoSiteInteractingOpenStripInfiniteVolumeClustering.lean`. Lifts the unconditional infinite-volume `K2` strip decay (#4142) into the §17.5 clustering/summability framework, all from `abs_correlationInfinite_stripGraph_axis_le` (`h = 0`, `0 < βJ`). `k2StripPrefactor_nonneg` (prefactor `≥ 0`, from the `sep = 1` bound and `exp > 0`), `exp_neg_mass_lt_one` (`exp(−m) < 1` since `m = twoSiteInteractingMass (βJ) > 0`). **Exponential clustering** `tendsto_correlationInfinite_stripGraph_axis_zero`: `⟨σ_(0,x)σ_(sep,x)⟩_∞ → 0` as `sep → ∞` (squeeze on `|·| ≤ prefactor·exp(−m·sep) → 0`). **Geometric summability** `summable_k2StripPrefactor_exp_neg_mass` and `tsum_k2StripPrefactor_exp_neg_mass` (`∑_{sep} prefactor·exp(−m·sep) = prefactor/(1 − exp(−m))`, via `exp(−m·sep) = (exp(−m))^sep` + `tsum_geometric_of_lt_one`). **One-sided same-transverse-site axis sum** `summable_abs_correlationInfinite_stripGraph_axis` (comparison, via the `sep+1` shift `summable_nat_add_iff`) and `tsum_abs_correlationInfinite_stripGraph_axis_le`: `∑_{sep ≥ 0} \|⟨σ_(0,x)σ_(sep,x)⟩_∞\| ≤ \|⟨σ_(0,x)⟩_∞\| + prefactor/(1 − exp(−m))` (the `sep = 0` term `correlationInfinite … (stripAxisTwoPoint x 0)` is the single-site observable, since the `Finset {(0,x),(0,x)}` collapses to the singleton `{(0,x)}`; it is kept explicit). This is the one-sided, fixed-anchor, same-transverse-site axis-direction absolute sum — not a full or two-sided lattice susceptibility — giving the exponential clustering of the interacting `K2` strip in the spirit of the 1D `InfiniteVolumeOneDSusceptibility`/`InfiniteVolumeOneDMass` picture. Reference: Glimm--Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304--306; §17.5, pp. 311--312. |
| §17.1 | **Two-mark open-slab spectral decay + K2 cross-transverse-site finite decay** (Issue #4144) | **Done (asymmetric-endpoint spectral decay; K2 cross-site `x ≠ y`)** | `TransferMatrix/LayerOpenTwoMarkedSpectralDecay.lean` + `TransferMatrix/TwoSiteInteractingLayerOpenCrossBoundaryWindow.lean`. Generalizes the single-mark open-slab spectral decay (same spin at both endpoints) to **two distinct marks** `f, g`, to bound cross-transverse-site correlations `⟨σ_(left,x) σ_(left+sep,y)⟩` with `x ≠ y` (needed to cover all distinct strip pairs for the strip cluster property, Issue #4144). `boundaryTwoMarkedSpectralPrefactor E f g vL vR := ∑_{i,j,l} \|bc vL i · markedMatrix f i j · markedMatrix g j l · bc vR l\|` (`_nonneg`; `_self` recovers the single-mark prefactor by `rfl`). The two-mark numerator chain (`layerOpenTwoMarkedMatrixProductNumerator`, `openTwoMarkedPathTripleNumerator`, `…_eq_boundarySpectralSum`, `…_abs_le_boundaryTwoMarkedSpectralPrefactor`) reuses **all** mark-agnostic path-glue and denominator/partition infrastructure, twinning only the lemmas where the right-endpoint mark appears (`markedMatrix f → markedMatrix g` at the `j–l` cut). Core bound `boundaryTwoMarkedSpectralSum_abs_le_spectralPrefactor`; the correlation↔numerator equate `correlation_layerOpenSlabGraph_two_transverse_eq_layerOpenSlabSpinTwoMarkedTwoPoint` (the two endpoints are distinct since their **layer coordinates** differ when `0 < sep`, independent of `x, y`; `spinProduct` expands to `Spin.sign(σ(left,x))·Spin.sign(σ(left+sep,y))`); headline general-layer theorem `correlation_layerOpenSlabGraph_two_transverse_abs_le_of_boundarySpectralDenominator`: `|correlation (layerOpenSlabGraph H transitionPairs (left+sep+right)) p {(leftIdx,x),(rightIdx,y)}| ≤ (boundaryTwoMarkedSpectralPrefactor (layerSpinAt x)(layerSpinAt y) v v / boundarySpectralPartitionPrefactor v top theta)·theta^sep`. **K2 specialization** `correlation_twoSiteInteractingLayerOpenSlabGraph_cross_abs_le_of_simpleSpectrum` (`h = 0`, `0 < βJ`, all `x, y ∈ Fin 2`): the cross-site bound at rate `twoSiteInteractingTheta^sep`, with the two-mark central-channel-zero parity hypothesis discharged in closed form by `twoSiteInteractingLayer_twoMarkedCentral_zero_of_layerSpinAt_flipParity` (via `boundaryTwoMarkedCentral_zero_of_equiv_evenBoundary_columnParity`: the **left-boundary** vector's flip-evenness together with the left mark's column flip-parity give `boundaryCoordinates v i · markedMatrix (layerSpinAt x) i top = 0` for **all** `i`, killing the entire central channel; the right mark `layerSpinAt y` is unused in the cancellation). Finite and (for general layers) conditional on the boundary-window gap + parity; the K2 case is unconditional. Reference: Glimm--Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304--306; §17.5, pp. 311--312. |
| §17.1 / §17.5 | **`K2` strip infinite-volume general two-point (cross-site)** (Issue #4144) | **Done (arbitrary longitudinal/transverse endpoints; unconditional)** | `TransferMatrix/TwoSiteInteractingOpenStripCrossInfiniteVolume.lean`. Generalizes the same-transverse-site axis decay (#4142, anchored at 0) to the **general** infinite-volume two-point of the `K2` strip — arbitrary longitudinal positions `a, b : ℤ` and transverse sites `x, y : Fin 2` — feeding the cross-site finite decay (#4145) into the #4142 centred-box machinery. `k2CrossStripPrefactor p hp x y` (the #4142 prefactor with `boundaryTwoMarkedSpectralPrefactor (layerSpinAt x)(layerSpinAt y) v v` in the numerator; `k2CrossStripPrefactor_self` reduces to `k2StripPrefactor` at `x = y`). The transported finite cross strip decay `correlation_induced_latticeGraph_two_strip_cross_abs_le_exp_neg_mass` (prefactor independent of length, rate `twoSiteInteractingTheta^sep = exp(−mass·sep)`), the length-general explicit cross pair `strip_pair_cross_abs_le` (via `obtain ⟨r, rfl⟩ : ∃ r, M = c + sep + r`), the per-stage ordered bound, and the prefactor symmetry `k2CrossStripPrefactor_comm` (from `boundaryTwoMarkedSpectralPrefactor_comm_of_eq_boundary`: swapping the two marks reindexes the spectral triple sum `(i,j,l) ↦ (l,j,i)` via `markedMatrix_comm`, valid since both boundary vectors coincide). **Headline** `abs_correlationInfinite_stripGraph_cross_le` (`h = 0`, `0 < βJ`, `a ≠ b`): `|correlationInfinite stripGraph stripExhaustion p (stripTwoPoint x y a b)| ≤ k2CrossStripPrefactor p hp x y · exp(−(twoSiteInteractingMass (βJ))·\|a − b\|)` (via `lt_trichotomy`: `a > b` swaps the pair by `stripTwoPoint_comm` + `k2CrossStripPrefactor_comm`). Unconditional (fixed transverse width 2). This covers all strip pairs with **nonzero longitudinal separation** (`a ≠ b`); the same-column rung `a = b, x ≠ y` is a single finite exception per site, bounded by `1` via `abs_correlationInfinite_le_one`. Together these give the strip cluster property (Issue #4144 PR-3). Reference: Glimm--Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304--306; §17.5, pp. 311--312. |
| §17.1 / §17.5 | **`K2` strip cluster property** (Issue #4144, CAPSTONE) | **Done (graph-general `clusterProperty` for the interacting `2 × ∞` strip; unconditional)** | `TransferMatrix/TwoSiteInteractingOpenStripClusterProperty.lean`. **Capstone of Issue #4144**: the project's graph-general cluster property `clusterProperty stripGraph stripExhaustion ⟨J,0,β⟩` for the interacting `K2` (`2 × ∞`) strip — the cluster property for the first interacting *fixed-width strip* (a genuinely 2D-graph infinite-volume geometry, beyond the 1D chain `clusterProperty_one`), unconditional (`0 ≤ J`, `0 < β`, `0 < βJ`). This is the `clusterProperty` predicate (cofinite vanishing of the truncated two-point); it does not by itself close the §17.5 `latticeMass`/`HasExponentialDecay` quantities (which are defined only for `latticeGraph d`). Proved via `clusterProperty_of_summable`: at zero field the magnetization vanishes so the truncated (Ursell) two-point function equals the full two-point (`truncated2Infinite_h_zero`), and `∀ i, Summable (j ↦ truncated2Infinite … i j)` follows from absolute summability. **Per-fiber summability** `summable_abs_correlationInfinite_stripGraph_cross_fiber`: for fixed `i = (a,x)` and transverse `y`, `∑_{b : ℤ} |⟨σ_(a,x) σ_(b,y)⟩_∞|` converges — off-diagonal `b ≠ a` dominated by the cross-site decay (#4146) `k2CrossStripPrefactor · exp(−mass·|a−b|)` (bilateral geometric via `summable_exp_neg_int_natAbs` + the shift `b ↦ a − b`), the single diagonal `b = a` term (the same-column rung) majorised by the `(k2CrossStripPrefactor + 1)`-scaled bound using `abs_correlationInfinite_le_one`. **Product summability** `summable_abs_correlationInfinite_stripGraph_cross_prod`: lifts to `∑_{j : ℤ × Fin 2}` via `summable_prod_of_nonneg` (inner sum over the finite `Fin 2`, outer over `ℤ` as `(fiber 0).add (fiber 1)`). **Headline** `clusterProperty_stripGraph`. This closes the §17.1 `K2`-strip programme (1D exact → finite slab → infinite-volume two-point → clustering → cluster property), realising the §17.5 exponential clustering picture for the interacting fixed-width strip. Reference: Glimm--Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304--306; §17.5, pp. 311--312. |
| §17.1 | **Explicit subdominant ratio for open-slab spectral decay** (Issue #3511) | **Done (explicit decay ratio + quantitative-estimate hook)** | `TransferMatrix/LayerOpenExplicitSubdominantRatio.lean`. The canonical decay ratio `subdominantRatio_maxEigenIndex` is obtained by `Classical.choose`, so a quantitative eigenvalue estimate `∀ i ≠ top, \|λ_i\| ≤ θ·λ_top` does not bound it directly. This file defines the **explicit** subdominant absolute ratio `RealOrthogonalSpectralData.subdominantAbsRatio_maxEigenIndex` = the genuine finite maximum of `\|λ_i\|/λ_top` over the non-maximal spectral indices (`0` for a single index), with `_nonneg`, `_lt_one`, the defining bound `eigenvalue_abs_le_subdominantAbsRatio_maxEigenIndex` (`∀ i ≠ top, \|λ_i\| ≤ ratio·λ_top`), and the key hook `subdominantAbsRatio_maxEigenIndex_le_of_eigenvalue_abs_le` (`0 ≤ θ ∧ (∀ i ≠ top, \|λ_i\| ≤ θ·λ_top) → ratio ≤ θ`; the `0 ≤ θ` is needed for the single-index case where the ratio is `0` and the estimate is vacuous). Sibling consumers `finiteTransverseHermitianExplicitRatio` (+ `_lt_one`, `_nonneg`) and `correlation_layerOpenSlabGraph_abs_le_of_hermitianExplicitRatioSimpleParityWindow` reproduce the `LayerOpenFiniteTransverseHermitian` open-slab decay with the explicit ratio as decay parameter, with a cubic specialization `cubicLayerHermitianExplicitRatio` and the ambient-box headline `correlation_cubicLayerOpenBox_abs_le_of_hermitianExplicitRatioSimpleParityWindow`. This is the entry point for a transverse-volume-uniform high-temperature spectral window: a later Dobrushin/Perron-gap estimate supplying `θ(β,J,d)` independent of the box radius will bound the explicit ratio uniformly. Finite and conditional on the boundary-window gap + parity input; does not construct the window uniformly or prove a thermodynamic limit. Reference: Glimm--Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304--306; §17.5, pp. 311--312. |
| §17.1 | **Quadratic-form spectral gap for the explicit subdominant ratio** (Issue #3511) | **Done (Rayleigh-gap reduction of the second-eigenvalue bound)** | _(scaffolding removed in PR-B1: route obstructed #4081, superseded by §18 #4228/#4255; archived at git tag `archive/transfermatrix-spectral-gap-scaffolding`)_. Reduced the explicit subdominant ratio bound to a **quadratic-form gap** on the subspace orthogonal to the maximal spectral column, using only the existing orthogonal-diagonalization API (no Courant--Fischer). History only: all five of the module's declarations went with it and none of the names it introduced is a declaration of the current tree, so none is cited here; the proofs survive only in the archived tag. The objects it related do survive: the quadratic form `matrixQuadraticForm`, the squared norm `vectorSqNorm` and the spectral coordinates `spectralCoord` (`TransferMatrix/LayerPerronExistence/QuadraticForm.lean` and `TransferMatrix/LayerPerronExistence/OrthogonalSpectralData.lean`), the orthogonality field `orthogonal_left` of `RealOrthogonalSpectralData` (`TransferMatrix/LayerSpectral/HermitianBridge.lean`), and the ratios `subdominantAbsRatio_maxEigenIndex`, `finiteTransverseHermitianExplicitRatio`, `cubicLayerHermitianExplicitRatio` (`TransferMatrix/LayerOpenExplicitSubdominantRatio.lean`); what is gone is every implication the module drew between them.
