It first proved that non-maximal columns are orthonormal, then that a gap `∀ v with spectralCoord v top = 0, \|⟨v,Mv⟩\| ≤ ρ·‖v‖²` forces `∀ i ≠ top, \|λ_i\| ≤ ρ` (by applying the gap to the `i`-th unit spectral column, whose quadratic form is `λ_i`), then that `(0 ≤ θ) ∧ (∀ v with spectralCoord v maxEigenIndex = 0, \|⟨v,Mv⟩\| ≤ θ·λ_max·‖v‖²) → subdominantAbsRatio_maxEigenIndex ≤ θ` by composing with the #4067 hook, with layer and cubic wrappers on top. This converted the transverse-volume-uniform target from an eigenvalue list into a single Rayleigh bound on the top-orthogonal subspace — what a later Dobrushin/Hilbert-metric estimate would have had to produce. Finite and conditional on the quadratic-form gap; did not construct it or prove a thermodynamic limit. Reference: Glimm--Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304--306; §17.5, pp. 311--312. |
| §17.1 | **Row-sum Rayleigh bound for the transfer-matrix quadratic form** (Issue #3511) | **Done (unconditional Schur/Gershgorin-type Rayleigh bound)** | _(scaffolding removed in PR-B1: route obstructed #4081, superseded by §18 #4228/#4255; archived at git tag `archive/transfermatrix-spectral-gap-scaffolding`)_. An unconditional Rayleigh bound for the quadratic form of a symmetric matrix in terms of its maximal absolute row sum (a discrete Schur-test / Gershgorin estimate by direct double-sum expansion + AM--GM, no operator-norm machinery). History only: all seven of the module's declarations went with it, the absolute-row-sum vocabulary it introduced (`∑ j \|M i j\|` and its maximum over rows) has no occurrence left in `IsingModel/` under any name, and none of the names it introduced is a declaration of the current tree, so none is cited here; the proofs survive only in the archived tag. Only the quadratic form `matrixQuadraticForm` and squared norm `vectorSqNorm` it was phrased in (`TransferMatrix/LayerPerronExistence/QuadraticForm.lean`) survive. Its core statement was `Mᵀ = M ∧ (∀ i, ∑ j \|M i j\| ≤ C) → \|⟨v,Mv⟩\| ≤ C·‖v‖²`, proved by `\|∑ᵢⱼ vᵢMᵢⱼvⱼ\| ≤ ∑ᵢⱼ\|Mᵢⱼ\|\|vᵢ\|\|vⱼ\| ≤ ∑ᵢⱼ\|Mᵢⱼ\|(vᵢ²+vⱼ²)/2 = ∑ᵢ rowSumᵢ·vᵢ² ≤ C·‖v‖²`, folding the column sum into the row sum via symmetry, with a maximal-row-sum corollary and a hook `0 ≤ θ ∧ maxAbsRowSum M ≤ θ·λ_max → subdominantAbsRatio_maxEigenIndex ≤ θ` feeding the #4068 quadratic-form-gap hook. The bound held for all vectors so it bounded the top eigenvalue too and did not by itself give a strict gap; the off-diagonal-mass splitting on the top-orthogonal subspace of the next rows was the intended sequel. Finite and unconditional; did not construct a strict spectral gap or prove a thermodynamic limit. Reference: Glimm--Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304--306; §17.5, pp. 311--312. |
| §17.1 | **Off-diagonal split of the transfer-matrix quadratic form** (Issue #3511) | **Done (diagonal-separated Gershgorin bound)** | _(scaffolding removed in PR-B1: route obstructed #4081, superseded by §18 #4228/#4255; archived at git tag `archive/transfermatrix-spectral-gap-scaffolding`)_. Refined the row-sum Rayleigh bound by separating the diagonal: for symmetric `M`, `\|⟨v,Mv⟩ − ∑ i M i i·v i²\| ≤ (max_i ∑_{j≠i}\|M i j\|)·‖v‖²`. History only: all nine of the module's declarations went with it, the off-diagonal-part and off-diagonal-row-sum vocabulary it introduced has no occurrence left in `IsingModel/` under any name, and none of the names it introduced is a declaration of the current tree, so none is cited here; the proofs survive only in the archived tag. The proof reused #4069's row-sum bound on the **off-diagonal part** `fun i j => if i = j then 0 else M i j` (diagonal zeroed), avoiding `j≠i` bookkeeping: structural lemmas said that the off-diagonal part of a symmetric matrix is symmetric, that its absolute row sum is the off-diagonal absolute row sum, and that its quadratic form is the quadratic form minus the diagonal, after which applying #4069 to it gave the headline bound and its maximal form. This was the structural step before the strict gap: on the subspace orthogonal to the entrywise-positive top eigenvector the diagonal and top contributions are subtracted, leaving the off-diagonal mass (later file). Finite and unconditional; did not construct a strict spectral gap or prove a thermodynamic limit. Reference: Glimm--Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304--306; §17.5, pp. 311--312. |
| §17.1 | **Diagonal bounds for the transfer-matrix quadratic form** (Issue #3511) | **Done (two-sided diagonal Rayleigh bounds)** | _(scaffolding removed in PR-B1: route obstructed #4081, superseded by §18 #4228/#4255; archived at git tag `archive/transfermatrix-spectral-gap-scaffolding`)_. The diagonal companion to the off-diagonal split: the diagonal contribution `∑ i, M i i·v i²` is two-sidedly controlled by the extremal diagonal entries. History only: all nine of the module's declarations went with it, the extremal-diagonal vocabulary it introduced (the sup, inf and absolute sup of the diagonal) has no occurrence left in `IsingModel/` under any name, and none of the names it introduced is a declaration of the current tree, so none is cited here; the proofs survive only in the archived tag. It defined those three extremal diagonal quantities with their pointwise accessors, then proved `∑ M i i·v i² ≤ max diag·‖v‖²`, `min diag·‖v‖² ≤ ∑ M i i·v i²`, and `\|∑ M i i·v i²\| ≤ max\|diag\|·‖v‖²`. Unconditional, requiring neither symmetry nor spectral data. Combined with the off-diagonal mass bound (#4070), these subtracted the diagonal/top contributions on the top-orthogonal subspace toward a strict gap. Finite and unconditional; did not construct a strict spectral gap or prove a thermodynamic limit. Reference: Glimm--Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304--306; §17.5, pp. 311--312. |
| §17.1 | **Gershgorin Rayleigh envelope for the transfer-matrix quadratic form** (Issue #3511) | **Done (two-sided Gershgorin envelope + ratio hook)** | _(scaffolding removed in PR-B1: route obstructed #4081, superseded by §18 #4228/#4255; archived at git tag `archive/transfermatrix-spectral-gap-scaffolding`)_. Combined the diagonal (#4071) and off-diagonal (#4070) bounds by triangle inequality into a two-sided Gershgorin Rayleigh envelope for symmetric `M`, with `offMass` the maximal off-diagonal absolute row sum. History only: all four of the module's declarations went with it, the two clusters it combined are themselves gone (rows above), and none of the names it introduced is a declaration of the current tree, so none is cited here; the proofs survive only in the archived tag. It proved `\|⟨v,Mv⟩\| ≤ (max\|diag\| + offMass)·‖v‖²`, the upper form `⟨v,Mv⟩ ≤ (max diag + offMass)·‖v‖²`, the lower form `(min diag − offMass)·‖v‖² ≤ ⟨v,Mv⟩`, and a hook `0 ≤ θ ∧ max\|diag\| + offMass ≤ θ·λ_max → subdominantAbsRatio_maxEigenIndex ≤ θ` feeding the #4068 quadratic-form-gap hook. The envelope held for all vectors so it bounded the top eigenvalue too and was NOT by itself a strict spectral gap; it was the Rayleigh form from which the top-eigenvalue contribution is subtracted on the top-orthogonal subspace. Finite and unconditional; did not construct a strict spectral gap or prove a thermodynamic limit. Reference: Glimm--Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304--306; §17.5, pp. 311--312. |
| §17.1 | **Top-column deflation and the strict-gap reduction** (Issue #3511) | **Done (circularity-breaking deflated Gershgorin hook)** | _(scaffolding removed in PR-B1: route obstructed #4081, superseded by §18 #4228/#4255; archived at git tag `archive/transfermatrix-spectral-gap-scaffolding`)_. The all-vector Gershgorin envelope cannot give a strict gap (it bounds the top eigenvalue too). This file deflated the top eigenpair, forming `fun i j => M i j − λ_top·(changeOfBasis i top · changeOfBasis j top)`, i.e. `M − λ_top·w_top w_topᵀ` with `w_top` the maximal spectral column. History only: all five of the module's declarations went with it, the whole top-deflation family has no occurrence left in `IsingModel/` under any name, and none of the names it introduced is a declaration of the current tree, so none is cited here; the proofs survive only in the archived tag. Only the ambient vocabulary survives — `changeOfBasis` and `spectralCoord` of `RealOrthogonalSpectralData` (`TransferMatrix/LayerSpectral/HermitianBridge.lean` and `TransferMatrix/LayerPerronExistence/OrthogonalSpectralData.lean`) and the ratio `subdominantAbsRatio_maxEigenIndex` (`TransferMatrix/LayerOpenExplicitSubdominantRatio.lean`). The deflated matrix was symmetric, its quadratic form differed from `M`'s by exactly the top contribution (`⟨v,Mv⟩ − ⟨v,(deflated)v⟩ = λ_top·(spectralCoord v top)²`, proved via `Fintype.sum_mul_sum`), hence agreed with `M` on the top-orthogonal subspace. The headline hook `0 ≤ θ ∧ maxAbsDiag(deflated) + maxOffDiagAbsRowSum(deflated) ≤ θ·λ_max → subdominantAbsRatio_maxEigenIndex ≤ θ` applied the #4072 envelope to the **deflated** matrix and fed the #4068 gap, **breaking the circularity**: the hypothesis is the deflated matrix's entrywise Gershgorin quantity (with the dominant eigenvalue removed), not the second eigenvalue. This reduced the strict gap to controlling the deflated entries — i.e. the top eigenvector concretely (a later quantitative Perron/Dobrushin estimate). Finite and conditional; did not construct a strict gap unconditionally or prove a thermodynamic limit. Reference: Glimm--Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304--306; §17.5, pp. 311--312. |
| §17.1 | **Entries and annihilation identity of the top deflation** (Issue #3511) | **Done (deflated-entry accessors + annihilation)** | _(scaffolding removed in PR-B1: route obstructed #4081, superseded by §18 #4228/#4255; archived at git tag `archive/transfermatrix-spectral-gap-scaffolding`)_. Exposed the entrywise structure of the top deflation and the defining annihilation identity. History only, like the top-deflation row above: all four of the module's declarations went with it, the top-deflation family has no occurrence left in `IsingModel/` under any name, and none of the names it introduced is a declaration of the current tree, so none is cited here; the proofs survive only in the archived tag. Its entry accessors read the deflated entry as `M i j − λ_top·(w_i·w_j)`, the deflated diagonal as `M i i − λ_top·w_i²` and the deflated off-diagonal absolute row sum as `∑_{j≠i} \|M i j − λ_top·w_i w_j\|`, reducing the deflated Gershgorin quantity (which controls the strict gap via #4073) to explicit expressions in `M`'s entries and the top eigenvector components `w_x = changeOfBasis x top`. The annihilation identity `(deflated)·w_top = 0`, from the eigen-equation `M·w_top = λ_top·w_top` and unit norm `∑ x w_x² = 1`, confirmed that the maximal eigenvalue is removed, isolating the genuinely remaining task — a quantitative entrywise bound on `λ_top·w_i w_j` (a Perron--Frobenius / Hilbert-metric estimate) for a later file. Finite, unconditional algebraic identities; did not bound the deflated entries quantitatively, construct a strict gap, or prove a thermodynamic limit. Reference: Glimm--Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304--306; §17.5, pp. 311--312. |
| §17.1 | **Doob (Perron) stochastic normalization of the transfer matrix** (Issue #3511) | **Done (row-stochastic conjugation)** | _(scaffolding removed in PR-B1: route obstructed #4081, superseded by §18 #4228/#4255; archived at git tag `archive/transfermatrix-spectral-gap-scaffolding`)_. For a positive matrix `M` with a strictly positive right eigenvector `w` of eigenvalue `λ > 0` (the Perron eigenpair), the Doob transform `M i j·w j/(λ·w i)` is an entrywise positive, row-stochastic matrix similar to `M/λ`. History only: all five of the module's declarations went with it, the Doob transform of a positive matrix has no occurrence left in `IsingModel/` under any name, and none of the names it introduced is a declaration of the current tree, so none is cited here; the proofs survive only in the archived tag. It gave the entry formula, entrywise positivity when `M` is positive, `w` positive and `λ > 0`, the row sums `∑ j P i j = 1` (from `∑ j M i j w j = λ w i`), and the eigenvector conjugation `M·v = μ·v → P·(v/w) = (μ/λ)·(v/w)`. This was the structural reduction underlying a future quantitative Perron--Frobenius / Dobrushin estimate: the spectral gap of `M` becomes the gap of the stochastic `P`, controlled by a Dobrushin / Hilbert-projective-metric contraction coefficient (not proved there either). Finite algebraic identities for an abstract positive Perron eigenpair; did not prove a quantitative contraction, a strict gap, or a thermodynamic limit. Reference: Glimm--Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304--306; §17.5, pp. 311--312. |
| §17.1 | **Dobrushin oscillation contraction for row-stochastic matrices** (Issue #3511) | **Done (the contraction estimate)** | _(scaffolding removed in PR-B1: route obstructed #4081, superseded by §18 #4228/#4255; archived at git tag `archive/transfermatrix-spectral-gap-scaffolding`)_. The central Dobrushin contraction: a row-stochastic matrix `P` contracts the oscillation `osc(v) = max_i v_i − min_i v_i` by its Dobrushin coefficient `δ(P) = ½·max_{i,i'} ∑_j \|P i j − P i' j\|`, i.e. `osc(P·v) ≤ δ(P)·osc(v)` for `∀ i ∑_j P i j = 1`. History only: all eighteen of the module's declarations went with it, the matrix-level vocabulary it introduced (the row-stochasticity predicate, the oscillation of a finite real vector, the Dobrushin row distance and the Dobrushin coefficient of a matrix) has no occurrence left in `IsingModel/` under any name, and none of the names it introduced is a declaration of the current tree, so none is cited here (the live single-site Dobrushin influence and site-oscillation work under `IsingModel/Dobrushin/` is a different object); the proofs survive only in the archived tag. The contraction was proved via the constant-shift `c = (max v + min v)/2`: `(Pv)_i − (Pv)_{i'} = ∑_j (P i j − P i' j)(v_j − c)` (using `∑_j(P i j − P i' j) = 0`), bounded by `∑_j \|P i j − P i' j\|·(osc/2) ≤ 2δ·(osc/2) = δ·osc`, on top of oscillation basics, row-distance/coefficient comparisons and a lemma connecting the coefficient to the Doob normalization. The eigenvalue corollary (#4077) added `osc(μ·v) = \|μ\|·osc(v)`, then that a nonconstant eigenvector of a row-sum-one matrix has `\|μ\| ≤ δ(P)`, and that for `M` with positive Perron eigenpair `(λ,w)` any eigenvalue `μ` with nonconstant `v/w` satisfies `\|μ/λ\| ≤ δ(Doob)`. This was the route to the spectral gap: the second eigenvalue of the stochastic Doob matrix is bounded by `δ`, so a uniform `δ < 1` yields a uniform gap (the quantitative `δ < 1` from the layer entries was a later file, also removed). Finite, unconditional; did not bound `δ` quantitatively, prove a strict gap, or a thermodynamic limit. Reference: Glimm--Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304--306; §17.5, pp. 311--312. |
| §17.1 | **Doeblin minorization and a strict Dobrushin bound** (Issue #3511) | **Done (δ < 1 for positive stochastic matrices)** | _(scaffolding removed in PR-B1: route obstructed #4081, superseded by §18 #4228/#4255; archived at git tag `archive/transfermatrix-spectral-gap-scaffolding`)_. Bounded the Dobrushin coefficient below `1` by the Doeblin mass `∑_j min_i P i j`. History only: all twelve of the module's declarations went with it, the column minimum and the Doeblin mass it introduced have no occurrence left in `IsingModel/` under any name, and none of the names it introduced is a declaration of the current tree, so none is cited here; the proofs survive only in the archived tag. It defined the column minimum and the Doeblin mass, proved `\|a−b\| = a+b−2 min a b` and hence `½∑_j\|P i j−P i' j\| = 1−∑_j min(P i j,P i' j)` for row-stochastic `P`, and concluded `δ(P) ≤ 1 − ∑_j min_i P i j`. For an entrywise positive stochastic matrix the Doeblin mass is positive, giving `δ(P) < 1`, specialized to the Doob transform. With the eigenvalue bound (#4077) this gave a strict second-eigenvalue gap for the stochastic Doob matrix. The bound was not yet uniform in the transverse box size: a uniform high-temperature lower bound on the Doob entries (the quantitative Perron-vector estimate) was a later file. Finite, unconditional; did not give a transverse-volume-uniform gap or prove a thermodynamic limit. Reference: Glimm--Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304--306; §17.5, pp. 311--312. |
| §17.1 | **Subdominant ratio bounded by the Doob Dobrushin coefficient** (Issue #3511) | **Done (abstract strict-gap capstone)** | _(scaffolding removed in PR-B1: route obstructed #4081, superseded by §18 #4228/#4255; archived at git tag `archive/transfermatrix-spectral-gap-scaffolding`)_. Tied the abstract spectral-gap machinery end-to-end: the explicit subdominant absolute ratio of a real orthogonally diagonalized entrywise positive matrix is bounded by the Dobrushin coefficient of its Doob transform, hence strictly below one. History only: all four of the module's declarations went with it, they rest on the matrix-level Doob/Doeblin/Dobrushin vocabulary of the three rows above, which has no occurrence left in `IsingModel/` under any name, and none of the names it introduced is a declaration of the current tree, so none is cited here; the proofs survive only in the archived tag. Two of its inputs survive: the orthonormality lemma `changeOfBasis_columns_not_smul` (`TransferMatrix/LayerPerron/PositiveColumn.lean`) and the ratio `subdominantAbsRatio_maxEigenIndex` together with its unconditional strict bound `subdominantAbsRatio_maxEigenIndex_lt_one` (`TransferMatrix/LayerOpenExplicitSubdominantRatio.lean`), so the `< 1` conclusion itself is still available along the Perron route — only this Doob proof of it is gone. The module proved that zero oscillation forces constancy, then the crux that for `i ≠ top` the vector `v_i/w_top` is nonconstant (else `v_i = c·w_top` contradicts orthonormality), then `subdominantAbsRatio_maxEigenIndex ≤ δ(Doob)` by feeding `\|λ_i/λ_top\| ≤ δ(Doob)` (#4077) into the #4067 hook, then `< 1` by composing with the strict Doeblin bound `δ(Doob) < 1` of #4078. This was an alternative, abstract proof of the strict subdominant bound, expressed through `δ(Doob)` — the quantity a later high-temperature estimate was to make uniform in the transverse box size; the bound there was not uniform. Finite, unconditional; did not give a transverse-volume-uniform gap or prove a thermodynamic limit. Reference: Glimm--Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304--306; §17.5, pp. 311--312. |
| §17.1 | **Projective cross-ratio and its row/column scaling invariance** (Issue #3511) | **Done (route-independent Hilbert-metric scaffolding)** | _(scaffolding removed in PR-B1: route obstructed #4081, superseded by §18 #4228/#4255; archived at git tag `archive/transfermatrix-spectral-gap-scaffolding`)_. The Hilbert projective (Birkhoff) metric route is governed by the projective cross-ratio `(A i a·A j b)/(A i b·A j a)`. History only: all five of the module's declarations went with it, the projective cross-ratio it introduced has no occurrence left in `IsingModel/` under any name, and none of the names it introduced is a declaration of the current tree, so none is cited here; the proofs survive only in the archived tag. Only the balanced layer matrix `layerSymmetricTransferMatrix` (`TransferMatrix/LayerSpectral/BalancedMatrix.lean`) it was evaluated at survives. The defining invariance — scaling `A i j ↦ r i·A i j·c j` by positive row/column factors leaves the cross-ratio unchanged — yielded two cancellations isolating the gap-controlling quantity from the unknown Perron data: the balanced `M a b = √(u a)·k a b·√(u b)` has the cross-ratio of the bare kernel `k` (the `√u` cancel), and the Doob transform `M i j·w j/(λ·w i)` has the cross-ratio of `M` (`w`,`λ` cancel), composed into: Doob of the balanced matrix has the cross-ratio of `k` alone, independent of both `u` and the Perron vector `w`. This was route-independent infrastructure for any later Hilbert-metric / Birkhoff--Hopf contraction argument; the contraction theorem `δ ≤ tanh(Δ/4)` and the (subtle, tensorized) uniform high-temperature estimate were not proved there, and the raw global projective diameter was not claimed uniformly small. Finite algebraic identities; did not bound the Dobrushin coefficient, give a transverse-volume-uniform gap, or prove a thermodynamic limit. Reference: Glimm--Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304--306; §17.5, pp. 311--312. |
| §17.1 | **Site factorization of the identity-transition projective cross-ratio** (Issue #3511) | **Done (site-additive log cross-ratio; obstruction made explicit)** | _(scaffolding removed in PR-B1: route obstructed #4081, superseded by §18 #4228/#4255; archived at git tag `archive/transfermatrix-spectral-gap-scaffolding`)_. The identity-transition layer kernel factorizes over transverse sites as `k ω η = exp(βJ ∑_x s(ω x)·s(η x))`, so its projective cross-ratio is a single exponential of a **sum over sites**, `cr(k;ω,ω',η,η') = exp(βJ ∑_x (s(ω x)−s(ω' x))·(s(η x)−s(η' x)))`. History only: all three of the module's declarations went with it, they are stated in the projective cross-ratio of the row above which has no occurrence left in `IsingModel/` under any name, and none of the names it introduced is a declaration of the current tree, so none is cited here; the proofs survive only in the archived tag. The kernel it evaluated, `layerTransitionWeight` (`TransferMatrix/LayerCylinder.lean`), survives, but its identity-transition exponential-sum evaluation does not; the nearest live evaluation of the same left-hand side is the site-product form `layerTransitionWeight_identity_eq_freeLayerTransferMatrix` (`TransferMatrix/FreeLayerWalshSpectralWindow/PhysicalBridgeSpectralWindow.lean`), which is one `Real.exp_sum` step from the exponential-sum value. By the #4080 scaling invariance, the Doob transform of the balanced **cubic** transfer matrix had the same cross-ratio — site-additive, independent of both the layer weight `u` and the Perron vector `w`. This made the Hilbert-metric structure explicit: the log cross-ratio is additive over transverse sites (each contributing at most `4·\|βJ\|`), so the *global* projective diameter grows linearly in the number of transverse sites — precisely why a transverse-volume-uniform gap cannot come from the global Dobrushin/diameter bound alone; a uniform estimate must exploit the site-tensorized structure (a local/tensorized contraction, a later research arc). Finite algebraic identities; did not give a transverse-volume-uniform gap or prove a thermodynamic limit. Reference: Glimm--Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304--306; §17.5, pp. 311--312. |
| §17.1 | **Finite free-layer Walsh open flip-parity route** (Issue #3511) | **Done (finite free-layer open certificate)** | `TransferMatrix/FreeLayerWalshSpectralWindow.lean` and `TransferMatrix/FreeLayerWalshOpenPerron.lean`. Supplies the concrete free-layer inputs required by the open-boundary flip-parity route. The Walsh character identity `spinProduct_layerStateFlipEquiv` proves that a Walsh column changes by `(-1) ^ |A|` under global spin flip, giving `freeLayerTransferOrthogonalSpectralData_columnFlipParity` and its physical zero-field wrapper without invoking arbitrary Hermitian spectral-theorem data. The top Walsh column is packaged as signed-positive (`freeLayerTransferOrthogonalSpectralData_top_signedPositiveColumn`, `freeLayerPhysicalOrthogonalSpectralData_top_signedPositiveColumn`). For the free open boundary, `layerOpenBalancedBoundaryVector_bot_h_zero` makes the boundary vector constant `1`, `freeLayerPhysical_boundaryCoordinates_nonTop_zero` shows all non-top Walsh boundary coordinates vanish, and `freeLayerPhysical_boundaryPrefactor_small_tanh` closes the open denominator prefactor condition for `theta = tanh (βJ)`. The constructor `freeLayerOpenMinGapCertificate_tanh` combines these facts with the existing free-layer Walsh subdominant window and the PR #4039 flip-parity constructor, and `correlation_freeLayerOpenSlabGraph_same_transverse_abs_le_tanh` exposes the resulting finite open-slab same-transverse-site bound. This is finite and free-layer only: it does not prove an interacting cubic-layer spectral window, a thermodynamic limit, or final hyperplane exponential decay. Reference: Glimm--Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304--306; §17.5, pp. 311--312. |
| §17.1 / §17.5 | **Finite free-layer Walsh open sharp prefactor** (Issue #3511) | **Done (finite free-layer coefficient-one decay)** | `TransferMatrix/FreeLayerWalshOpenDecay.lean`. Sharpens the finite free-layer open estimate by eliminating the remaining open spectral prefactor ratio. The singleton Walsh index `freeLayerWalshSingleton` has down-spin set `{x}`, and `layerSpinAt_eq_spinProduct_singleton` identifies a fixed-site spin with that singleton Walsh character. The column identity `freeLayerPhysical_top_column_mul_layerSpinAt` and orthogonality give `freeLayerPhysical_markedMatrix_layerSpinAt_top_eq_ite` and its symmetric form: the marked matrix sends the top Walsh mode only to the singleton mode. Since the free open boundary has only the top coordinate, `freeLayerPhysical_boundarySpectralPartitionPrefactor_eq_top_sq` and `freeLayerPhysical_boundaryMarkedSpectralPrefactor_eq_top_sq` prove that the denominator and marked numerator prefactors are the same top-coordinate square; `freeLayerPhysical_openPrefactorRatio_eq_one` packages their ratio as `1`. The consumer `correlation_freeLayerOpenSlabGraph_same_transverse_abs_le_tanh_clean` gives the clean finite open bound `|<σ_(left,x) σ_(left+sep,x)>| ≤ tanh(βJ)^sep`, and `correlation_freeLayerOpenSlabGraph_same_transverse_abs_le_exp_neg_mass` rewrites it using `correlationMass`. This is finite and free-layer only: it does not prove an interacting cubic-layer spectral window, a thermodynamic-limit bridge, or final hyperplane decay. Reference: Glimm--Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304--306; §17.5, pp. 311--312. |
| §17.1 / §17.5 | **Free-layer open cubic-box transport bridge** (Issue #3511) | **Done (finite free-layer axis-graph transport)** | `TransferMatrix/FreeLayerWalshOpenInfiniteVolume.lean`. Adds the finite geometric bridge needed before lifting the free-layer open estimate toward ambient finite volumes. The new `freeLayerAxisGraph` is the longitudinal-only graph on `Z^(d+1)`: adjacent vertices differ by one in the first coordinate and agree in all transverse coordinates. The embedding `freeLayerOpenSlabCubicBoxEquiv` sends a `2N`-step open free-layer slab over `cubicBox d N` to the ambient cubic box `cubicBox (d+1) N` by `(i,x) ↦ (i-N,x)`. The graph identity `freeLayerOpenSlabGraph_map_cubicBoxEquiv` proves that the transported finite open free-layer slab is exactly `Ambient.inducedGraph (freeLayerAxisGraph d) (Ambient.cubicBox (d+1) N)`. The correlation transport theorem `correlation_induced_freeLayerAxisGraph_cubicBox_eq_openSlab`, its absolute-value form, and `abs_correlation_induced_freeLayerAxisGraph_cubicBox_le_of_openSlab` move finite open-slab estimates onto the induced cubic-box finite volume. This is deliberately a free-layer axis-graph bridge only: it does not claim decay for the full `latticeGraph (d+1)` with transverse edges, does not prove an interacting spectral window, and does not complete the thermodynamic-limit step. Reference: Glimm--Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304--306; §17.5, pp. 311--312. |
| §17.1 / §17.5 | **Finite free-layer axis-graph cubic-box decay consumer** (Issue #3511) | **Done (finite zero-field axis-graph cubic-box decay)** | `TransferMatrix/FreeLayerWalshOpenAxisDecay.lean`. Consumes the sharp finite free-layer open estimate from `FreeLayerWalshOpenDecay.lean` together with the cubic-box transport bridge from `FreeLayerWalshOpenInfiniteVolume.lean`. The two-point definitions `freeLayerOpenCubicSlabTwoPoint` and `freeLayerOpenCubicAxisTwoPoint` select the centre layer and the layer at longitudinal separation `sep` in the `2N`-step slab, assuming `sep ≤ N`, and then transport that observable to the induced finite volume of `freeLayerAxisGraph`. The finite wrappers `correlation_freeLayerOpenSlabGraph_cubicBox_same_transverse_abs_le_tanh_clean` and `correlation_freeLayerOpenSlabGraph_cubicBox_same_transverse_abs_le_exp_neg_mass` specialize the clean open-slab theorem with `left = N` and `right = N - sep`; a private length-cast helper handles the equality `N + sep + (N - sep) = 2N`. The induced consumers `correlation_induced_freeLayerAxisGraph_cubicBox_same_transverse_abs_le_tanh_clean` and `correlation_induced_freeLayerAxisGraph_cubicBox_same_transverse_abs_le_exp_neg_mass` give the corresponding finite cubic-box bounds on `Ambient.inducedGraph (freeLayerAxisGraph d) (Ambient.cubicBox (d+1) N)`. This remains finite, zero-field, and longitudinal-axis only: it does not prove decay for the full cubic lattice with transverse edges, an interacting spectral window, or a thermodynamic-limit theorem. Reference: Glimm--Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304--306; §17.5, pp. 311--312. |
| §17.1 / §17.5 | **Free-layer axis-graph cubic-exhaustion infinite-volume decay** (Issue #3511) | **Done (axis-graph cubic-exhaustion limit)** | `TransferMatrix/FreeLayerWalshOpenAxisInfiniteVolume.lean`. Passes the finite free-layer axis-graph cubic-box estimates to the concrete cubic exhaustion and then to `Ambient.correlationInfinite`. The ambient observable `freeLayerAxisTwoPoint d x sep` is the pair `{freeLayerAxisPoint d 0 x, freeLayerAxisPoint d sep x}` on the longitudinal free-layer axis. The stagewise theorems `abs_correlationAlongExhaustion_freeLayerAxisGraph_axis_le_tanh` and `abs_correlationAlongExhaustion_freeLayerAxisGraph_axis_le_exp_neg_mass` split on whether this pair is contained in the `N`th cubic volume: in the contained case they identify the lifted pair with `freeLayerOpenCubicAxisTwoPoint` and reuse the finite cubic-box consumer; in the missed case the along-exhaustion convention gives zero. The infinite-volume absolute-value theorems `abs_correlationInfinite_freeLayerAxisGraph_axis_le_tanh` and `abs_correlationInfinite_freeLayerAxisGraph_axis_le_exp_neg_mass` use `Ambient.correlationInfinite_eq_ciSup`, `ciSup_le`, and the stage-0 lower side of the absolute-value bound; the one-sided wrappers `correlationInfinite_freeLayerAxisGraph_axis_le_tanh` and `correlationInfinite_freeLayerAxisGraph_axis_le_exp_neg_mass` follow from `le_abs_self`. This remains zero-field, free-layer, and longitudinal-axis only: it does not assert decay for the full `latticeGraph (d+1)` with transverse edges and does not prove an interacting spectral window. Reference: Glimm--Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304--306; §17.5, pp. 311--312. |
| §17.1 | **Finite open-boundary spectral-decay consumer** (Issue #3511) | **Done (finite conditional open spectral consumer)** | `TransferMatrix/LayerOpenSpectralDecay.lean`. Consumes the finite open boundary-vector spectral numerator constructor as project-level open-slab correlation bounds. The wrapper `layerOpenMinSpectralGapCertificate_of_layerOrthogonalBoundaryDominantBounds` specializes `layerOpenMinSpectralGapCertificate_of_matrixPartition_orthogonalBoundaryDominantBounds` to physical layer weights and discharges the boundary-weight positivity input by strict positivity of exponentials. The theorem `correlation_layerOpenSlabGraph_same_transverse_abs_le_of_orthogonalBoundaryDominantBounds` feeds that certificate into the existing open-slab graph consumer, yielding the same-transverse-site bound with prefactor `spec.boundaryMarkedSpectralPrefactor (layerSpinAt x) (layerOpenBalancedBoundaryVector (layerInternalWeight H p)) (layerOpenBalancedBoundaryVector (layerInternalWeight H p)) / partitionPrefactor` and rate `theta^sep`. The cubic wrapper `correlation_cubicLayerOpenSlabGraph_same_transverse_abs_le_of_orthogonalBoundaryDominantBounds` specializes the result to `cubicLayerOpenSlabGraph`. This remains finite and conditional: the open matrix-partition denominator lower bound, eigenvalue scale bounds, subdominant bound, and central-channel cancellation are explicit hypotheses, and the module does not prove Perron--Frobenius existence, an interacting cubic-layer spectral window, thermodynamic-limit decay, or final hyperplane exponential decay. Reference: Glimm--Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304--306; §17.5, pp. 311--312. |
| §17.1 | **Top-deflated Gershgorin spectral-gap route for the layer transfer matrix** (Issue #3511, P5 foundation) | **Done (deflation-route reduction + explicit deflated entries)** | _(scaffolding removed in PR-B1: route obstructed #4081, superseded by §18 #4228/#4255; archived at git tag `archive/transfermatrix-spectral-gap-scaffolding`)_. The non-circular route to a subdominant ratio `θ < 1` for the balanced layer transfer matrix `M a b = √(u a)·k a b·√(u b)`. The all-vector Gershgorin envelope `max|diag| + offMass` upper-bounds the spectral radius `= λ_max`, so it cannot give `θ < 1` (circular); the **top-deflated** matrix `M − λ_max·w wᵀ` (`w` the Perron column) has the maximal eigenvalue removed, so its Gershgorin envelope can be strictly below `λ_max`. History only: all six of the module's declarations went with it, the whole top-deflation layer has no occurrence left in `IsingModel/` under any name, and none of the names it introduced is a declaration of the current tree, so none is cited here; the proofs survive only in the archived tag. Two of its inputs survive. Its symmetry lemma for the balanced matrix was a duplicate, with the same statement, of the still-live `layerSymmetricTransferMatrix_transpose` (`TransferMatrix/LayerSpectral/BalancedMatrix.lean`); and the quantity the route bounded, `finiteTransverseHermitianExplicitRatio` with its cubic form `cubicLayerHermitianExplicitRatio` (`TransferMatrix/LayerOpenExplicitSubdominantRatio.lean`), is still a definition of the tree. What is gone is the reduction between them: the explicit deflated entries `√(u i)·k i j·√(u j) − λ_top·w_i·w_j` (the quantity the uniform bound had to control), their diagonal and off-diagonal row-sum forms, and the layer and cubic route wrappers taking the ratio bound down to a top-deflated Gershgorin envelope `≤ θ·λ_max`. This was the P5 foundation: the remaining core math is the transverse-volume-uniform high-temperature bound discharging the deflated-Gershgorin hypothesis with `θ(βJ) < 1` independent of the box radius. Reference: Glimm–Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304–306. |
| §17.1 | **Rayleigh lower bound on the layer transfer matrix's maximal eigenvalue** (Issue #3511, P5) | **Done (Rayleigh λ_max lower bound + constant-vector specialization)** | _(scaffolding removed in PR-B1: route obstructed #4081, superseded by §18 #4228/#4255; archived at git tag `archive/transfermatrix-spectral-gap-scaffolding`)_. Supplied the lower bound on the `λ_max` denominator of the top-deflated Gershgorin gap (#4187): the **Rayleigh lower bound** `λ_max ≥ ⟨v,M v⟩/‖v‖²`, its constant-vector specialization `λ_max ≥ (∑_{a,b} M a b)/|Ω|` (the average entry sum bounds `λ_max` below; positive for an entrywise positive matrix), and the layer form `λ_max ≥ (∑_{a,b} √(u a)·k a b·√(u b))/|LayerState S|` for the actual balanced layer transfer matrix. All four of the module's declarations went with it, so none of their names is a declaration of the current tree. The Rayleigh lower bound is one division away from a lemma that survives: the Rayleigh upper bound `matrixQuadraticForm_le_maxEigenIndex` (`TransferMatrix/LayerPerronExistence/OrthogonalSpectralData.lean`) is exactly the removed statement cleared of the denominator `‖v‖² = ∑ v_i²`, whose positivity for `v ≠ 0` was the module's own elementary finite-sum lemma. The layer form was that inequality at the spectral datum `finiteTransverseHermitianData` (`TransferMatrix/LayerOpenFiniteTransverseHermitian.lean`), which survives, but the inequality itself does not. Abstract and reusable; the remaining P5 core math is the matching upper bound on the deflated-Gershgorin quantity via the Perron column. Reference: Glimm–Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304–306. |
| §17.1 | **Top-deflation row cancellation identity** (Issue #3511, P5) | **Done (Perron-weighted diagonal identity)** | _(scaffolding removed in PR-B1: route obstructed #4081, superseded by §18 #4228/#4255; archived at git tag `archive/transfermatrix-spectral-gap-scaffolding`)_. The structural form of the deflated spectral gap, encoding the `M_ij` vs `λ_max·w_i·w_j` cancellation via the eigen-equation (a triangle inequality would destroy it). History only: all three of the module's declarations went with it, they belong to the same top-deflation family as the two rows above, that family has no occurrence left in `IsingModel/` under any name, and none of the names it introduced is a declaration of the current tree, so none is cited here; the proofs survive only in the archived tag. Since `M_def · w = 0` for the Perron column `w = changeOfBasis·top`, splitting row `i` gave `M_def_ii = − ∑_{j≠i} M_def_ij·(w_j/w_i)` (needing `w_i ≠ 0`), with absolute-value corollary `|M_def_ii| ≤ ∑_{j≠i}|M_def_ij|·|w_j/w_i|`. The hypothesis `w_i ≠ 0` was discharged from the signed-positive Perron column of an entrywise positive matrix, which does survive as `signedPositiveColumn_maxEigenIndex` (`TransferMatrix/LayerPerronExistence/OrthogonalSpectralData.lean`); only the nonvanishing corollary drawn from it went with the module. This reduced the deflated-Gershgorin diagonal to the off-diagonal mass weighted by Perron ratios (the algebraic step packaged generically in the flatness-envelope row as `subdominantAbsRatio_maxEigenIndex ≤ (1+ρ)·η`, itself removed in PR-B1). The remaining core is the Perron flatness/ratio `ρ = sup|w_j/w_i|` together with the deflated off-diagonal mass; for the interacting layer `ρ` is **not** transverse-volume-uniform (`ρ = exp(Θ(|S|))` for the product channel `k ≡ 1`, a documented obstruction), so this is the genuinely analytic high-temperature core, not a one-step finite estimate. Reference: Glimm–Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304–306. |
| §17.1 | **Perron eigenvector flatness from an entry-ratio bound** (Issue #3511, P5) | **Done (abstract flatness mechanism + Perron-column/layer wrappers)** | _(scaffolding removed in PR-B1: route obstructed #4081, superseded by §18 #4228/#4255; archived at git tag `archive/transfermatrix-spectral-gap-scaffolding`)_. The abstract mechanism turning an entry-ratio bound into eigenvector flatness: for a strictly positive eigenvector `v` (`M v = λ v`, `λ > 0`), a row entry-ratio bound `M_ik ≤ ρ·M_jk` forces `v_i ≤ ρ·v_j` (since `λ v_i = ∑_k M_ik v_k ≤ ρ ∑_k M_jk v_k = ρ λ v_j`). History only: all three of the module's declarations went with it, no lemma of the current tree derives eigenvector flatness from an entry-ratio bound under any name, and none of the names it introduced is a declaration of the current tree, so none is cited here; the proofs survive only in the archived tag. It applied the mechanism to the signed-positive maximal spectral column, and the three facts that made that column admissible all survive in `TransferMatrix/LayerPerronExistence/OrthogonalSpectralData.lean` — the column is signed positive (`signedPositiveColumn_maxEigenIndex`), it is an eigenvector (`mulVec_signedColumn`), and `λ_max > 0` (`eigenvalue_pos_maxEigenIndex`); only the flatness conclusion and its layer specialization for `M a b = √(u a)·k a b·√(u b)` are gone. **The entry-ratio route is NOT transverse-volume-uniform**: the layer entry ratio `M_ik/M_jk = √(u_i)/√(u_j)` scales like `exp(Θ(βJ·|S|))`, so this mechanism (though correct and reusable) does not by itself produce a volume-uniform `ρ(βJ)`; the uniform flatness input is the documented analytic obstruction (single-step methods insufficient — cf. the `k ≡ 1` product-channel counterexample). Reference: Glimm–Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304–306. |
| §17.1 | **Column-oscillation reduction of the Doeblin mass / Dobrushin coefficient** (Issue #3511, P5) | **Done (row-similarity reduction)** | _(scaffolding removed in PR-B1: route obstructed #4081, superseded by §18 #4228/#4255; archived at git tag `archive/transfermatrix-spectral-gap-scaffolding`)_. Reduced the transverse-volume-uniform spectral gap to a per-column row-variation estimate. History only: all five of the module's declarations went with it, the matrix-level Doob/Doeblin/Dobrushin layer they rest on has no occurrence left in `IsingModel/` under any name (the live single-site Dobrushin uniqueness work under `IsingModel/Dobrushin/` is a different object), and none of the names it introduced is a declaration of the current tree, so none is cited here; the proofs survive only in the archived tag. The per-box strict bound `cubicLayerHermitianExplicitRatio < 1` does survive, as `cubicLayerHermitianExplicitRatio_lt_one` (`TransferMatrix/LayerOpenExplicitSubdominantRatio.lean`), but along the Perron route, not along the Doob–Dobrushin chain recorded here, whose per-box conclusion is gone with the rest. That chain reduced the uniform `θ(βJ)` to a uniform lower bound on the Doeblin mass `∑_j min_i P_ij` of the Doob transform `P`. A crude per-entry floor `card·min P_ij` is useless (entries ~ `exp(−O(|S|))`); the volume-uniform handle is **row similarity**: exactly, for row-stochastic `P`, `DoeblinMass = 1 − ∑_j(P_ij − min_i P_ij)` for any row `i`, hence `DoeblinMass ≥ 1 − ∑_j(max_i P_ij − min_i P_ij)` and `DobrushinCoeff ≤ ∑_j(max_i P_ij − min_i P_ij)`, the column oscillation. This honestly turned the uniform Dobrushin/subdominant-ratio bound into a per-column oscillation sum on the Doob transform; the remaining P5 core is the layer-specific estimate that the Doob columns vary little (rows nearly identical) at high temperature, uniform in the box radius. Reference: Glimm–Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304–306. |
| §17.1 | **The single-site Ising influence bound (Dobrushin uniqueness)** (Issue #3511) | **Done (single-site influence = tanh(βJ))** | `Dobrushin/SingleSiteInfluence.lean`. Toward a transverse-volume-uniform spectral gap via **single-site Dobrushin uniqueness** (the one-step full-layer Dobrushin coefficient is provably insufficient — its TV→1 as |S|→∞). The analytic heart: `isingSingleSiteUpProb a = e^a/(e^a+e^{-a})` (single-site up-probability of local field `a = β(h+J·neighbour-sum)`), `isingSingleSiteUpProb_eq_tanh` (`= (1+tanh a)/2`), and the influence bound `isingSingleSiteUpProb_flip_neighbour_dist_le`: flipping one neighbour (shifting the field by `±t`, `t=βJ`) changes the up-probability by at most `tanh t`. Proof via the sharp tanh difference `|tanh(a+t)−tanh(a−t)| ≤ 2 tanh t` (`tanh_add_sub_tanh_sub_abs_le`), itself from `tanh(a+t)−tanh(a−t) = sinh(2t)/(cosh(a+t)cosh(a−t))` with `cosh(a+t)cosh(a−t) = cosh²t+sinh²a ≥ cosh²t` (`cosh_add_mul_cosh_sub`). So the nearest-neighbour Ising Dobrushin influence is `tanh(βJ)`; Dobrushin's condition `tanh(βJ)·deg < 1` will give volume-uniform decay (next PRs). Reference: Glimm–Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304–306. |
| §17.1 | **The single-site Hamiltonian decomposition (Dobrushin uniqueness)** (Issue #3511) | **Done (single-site energy gap = −2·local field)** | `Dobrushin/SingleSiteConditional.lean`. Toward the lattice single-site conditional Gibbs distribution: updating one site `x` to `up` vs `down` (rest fixed to `η`) changes the Ising Hamiltonian by exactly `−2·(J·∑_{y∼x} sign(η_y) + h)` (twice the local field). `externalFieldEnergy_update_up_sub_down` — the field gap `−2h` (only site `x`'s field term changes); `interactionEnergy_update_up_sub_down` — the interaction gap `−2·J·∑_{y∼x} sign(η_y)` (only the edges incident to `x` change, via the incident-edge ↔ neighbour reindex `Finset.sum_bij'` with `Sym2.Mem.other`, the non-incident edges unchanged by `edgeSpin_update_of_not_mem`); `hamiltonian_update_up_sub_down` — the total. With `isingSingleSiteUpProb_eq_tanh` (#4193), the single-site conditional up-probability is `(1+tanh(β·local-field))/2` (next PR), and the influence between configs differing at one neighbour is `tanh(βJ)`. Reference: Glimm–Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304–306. |
| §17.1 | **Single-site conditional infrastructure (Dobrushin uniqueness)** (Issue #3511) | **Done (config-sum collapse + Hamiltonian J-bridge)** | `Dobrushin/SingleSiteConditionalProb.lean`. The bookkeeping toward the single-site conditional Gibbs probability. `sum_indicator_agreesOff_singleton` — a sum over all configurations weighted by the indicator of agreement with `η` off `{x}` collapses to the two single-site updates `g(η[x↦up]) + g(η[x↦down])` (a config agreeing with `η` off `{x}` is determined by its value at `x`; proved by a `Finset.sum_bij'` to `Spin` + `sum_spin`). `hamiltonianJ_const_eq_hamiltonian` — the inhomogeneous Hamiltonian with constant coupling `J` equals the homogeneous Ising Hamiltonian `hamiltonian G ⟨J,h,β⟩`, bridging the boundary-condition Boltzmann weight (`boltzmannWeightJ`) to the single-site energy gap (`hamiltonian_update_up_sub_down`, #4194). Together with `isingSingleSiteUpProb_eq_tanh` (#4193), the single-site conditional up-probability `= (1+tanh(β·local-field))/2` follows (next PR, via the weight ratio `exp(2β(J·neighbour-sum+h))`). Reference: Glimm–Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304–306. |
| §17.1 | **The single-site conditional Gibbs distribution (Dobrushin uniqueness)** (Issue #3511) | **Done (up/down probability + magnetization = tanh)** | `Dobrushin/SingleSiteConditionalDistribution.lean`. The headline single-site conditional distribution at a free site `x` with the rest of the lattice frozen to the boundary condition `η` (constant coupling `J`, field `h`). `isingLocalField G β J h x η = β·(J·∑_{y∼x} sign(η_y) + h)` — the local field `a` seen by the spin at `x`. `gibbsExpectationBC_singleton_up_eq_upProb` — the conditional probability that `σ_x = up` is `isingSingleSiteUpProb(a)`; `gibbsExpectationBC_singleton_down_eq` — that `σ_x = down` is `1 − isingSingleSiteUpProb(a)` (complementary, via linearity); `gibbsExpectationBC_singleton_sign_eq_tanh` — the conditional magnetization `⟨sign(σ_x)⟩ = tanh(a)`. Proof: the `{x}`-conditioned weighted sum collapses to the two single-site updates (`sum_indicator_agreesOff_singleton`, #4195), giving numerator `w_up` (resp. `w_up − w_down`) and partition function `w_up + w_down`; the weights are `e^{−βH}` via `hamiltonianJ_const_eq_hamiltonian` (#4195) and the energy gap `H_up − H_down = −2·(J·∑ sign(η_y) + h)` (`hamiltonian_update_up_sub_down`, #4194); the ratio is closed by the Boltzmann-ratio identities `isingSingleSiteUpProb((p−q)/2) = e^p/(e^p+e^q)` and `tanh((p−q)/2) = (e^p−e^q)/(e^p+e^q)`. The formulas hold for **arbitrary real** `β, J, h` (no high-temperature hypothesis). These are the per-site building blocks of the single-site Dobrushin influence matrix `c_{xy} = tanh(βJ)·[y∼x]` (next PR), whose row sum `tanh(βJ)·deg < 1` is the high-temperature hypothesis of the (not-yet-formalized) Dobrushin uniqueness theorem. Reference: Glimm–Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304–306. |
| §17.1 | **The single-site Dobrushin influence matrix** (Issue #3511) | **Done (influence bound `tanh(βJ)·[y∼x]` + Dobrushin interaction sum)** | `Dobrushin/SingleSiteInfluenceMatrix.lean`. The Dobrushin influence of a boundary site `y` on the single-site conditional law at `x`. `singleSiteUpProbBC G β J h x η = isingSingleSiteUpProb(isingLocalField …)` — the conditional up-probability as a boundary functional (= the BC-Gibbs up-indicator expectation, `singleSiteUpProbBC_eq_gibbsExpectationBC`). `tanh_le_tanh_of_le` — monotonicity of `tanh` (not in Mathlib; via `tanh b − tanh a = sinh(b−a)/(cosh b·cosh a) ≥ 0`). `isingSingleSiteUpProb_dist_le_tanh_half` — the sharp Lipschitz-`tanh` bound `|upProb a − upProb a'| ≤ tanh(|a−a'|/2)` (centering the symmetric flip bound `isingSingleSiteUpProb_flip_neighbour_dist_le` at the midpoint). `singleSiteUpProbBC_eq_of_not_neighbour` — **no influence** from a non-neighbour (or from `x` itself): the local field depends on the boundary only through the neighbour spins. `singleSiteUpProbBC_neighbour_dist_le` / `singleSiteUpProbBC_dist_le` — the **influence bound** `≤ tanh(βJ)` for `0 ≤ βJ` (sharp for a neighbour, trivially `0` otherwise): two boundary configs agreeing off `{y}` differ in the neighbour-sign sum by at most `2` (`abs_sign_sub_le_two`, supported at `y` by `neighbourSignSum_sub`), so the local field moves by at most `2βJ`. `isingInfluence G β J x y = tanh(βJ)·[y ∈ neighborFinset x]` — the influence matrix `c_{xy}`; `isingInfluence_rowSum` — the Dobrushin interaction sum `∑_y c_{xy} = deg(x)·tanh(βJ)`; `isingDobrushin_condition` (`tanh(βJ)·deg(x) < 1`) with `isingDobrushin_condition_iff` (= row sum `< 1`). At high temperature (`tanh(βJ)·deg(x) < 1`) Dobrushin's condition is the hypothesis of the standard Dobrushin uniqueness theorem (uniqueness of the infinite-volume Gibbs state and volume-uniform exponential decay); the per-pair bounds here are its inputs. The single-site Dobrushin comparison theorem is now formalized (`Dobrushin/ComparisonTheorem.lean`, #4220); the infinite-volume uniqueness/decay corollary remains. Reference: Glimm–Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304–306. |
| §17.1 | **High-temperature sufficiency of the single-site Dobrushin condition** (Issue #3511) | **Done (`tanh t ≤ t` + the `βJ·deg < 1` threshold implies the Dobrushin condition)** | `Dobrushin/DobrushinHighTemp.lean`. Shows the single-site Dobrushin condition `tanh(βJ)·deg(x) < 1` (`isingDobrushin_condition`, #4197) is met in the high-temperature regime. `tanh_le_self` — `tanh t ≤ t` for `0 ≤ t` (absent from Mathlib, which has no `tanh` derivative or `tanh ≤ id` lemma): equivalent to `sinh t ≤ t·cosh t`, proved via the auxiliary function `g(t) = t·cosh t − sinh t`, monotone on `[0,∞)` (`monotoneOn_of_deriv_nonneg`; `HasDerivAt` giving `g'(t) = t·sinh t ≥ 0`) with `g(0) = 0`. `isingInfluence_rowSum_le` — the Dobrushin interaction sum is `≤ βJ·deg(x)` (for `0 ≤ βJ`). `isingDobrushin_condition_of_high_temp` — for `0 ≤ βJ`, the threshold `βJ·deg(x) < 1` implies `tanh(βJ)·deg(x) < 1`; `isingDobrushin_condition_of_high_temp_maxDegree` — the uniform form `βJ·Δ(G) < 1` (with `Δ(G) = G.maxDegree`) implies the condition at every site. This is the **same** `βJ·deg < 1` high-temperature threshold as the repository's Simon–Lieb decay results; the Dobrushin comparison theorem that consumes this condition is now formalized (`Dobrushin/ComparisonTheorem.lean`, #4220). Reference: Glimm–Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304–306. |
| §17.1 | **Exponential decay of the Dobrushin influence-matrix powers** (Issue #3511) | **Done (`rowSum(Cⁿ) ≤ αⁿ` + `→ 0` at high temperature)** | `Dobrushin/InfluenceMatrixDecay.lean`. The analytic seed of Dobrushin exponential decay. `matrix_pow_rowSum_le` — abstract: a matrix `M` with nonnegative entries and all row sums `≤ α` (`0 ≤ α`) has `∑_y (Mⁿ)_{xy} ≤ αⁿ` (induction: `∑_z (M^{n+1})_{xz} = ∑_y M_{xy}·(∑_z (Mⁿ)_{yz}) ≤ (∑_y M_{xy})·αⁿ ≤ α·αⁿ`). `isingInfluenceMatrix G β J` — the influence matrix `C_{xy} = isingInfluence G β J x y` as a `Matrix`; `isingDobrushinCoeff = Δ(G)·tanh(βJ)` — the Dobrushin coefficient (uniform maximum-degree bound on every row sum, `isingInfluenceMatrix_rowSum_le`). `isingInfluenceMatrix_pow_rowSum_le` — `∑_y (Cⁿ)_{xy} ≤ (Δ(G)·tanh(βJ))ⁿ` (for `0 ≤ βJ`): the boundary influence decays geometrically with the number of steps `n`. `isingDobrushinCoeff_lt_one_of_high_temp` — `α < 1` under `βJ·Δ(G) < 1` (via `tanh(βJ) ≤ βJ`); `isingInfluenceMatrix_pow_rowSum_tendsto_zero` — the row sums of `Cⁿ` tend to `0` at high temperature (squeezed between `0` and `αⁿ` with `0 ≤ α < 1`). This is the geometric decay underlying the Dobrushin comparison/uniqueness theorem; that comparison theorem is now formalized as `gibbsExpectationBC_dist_le_resolvent_sum` (`Dobrushin/ComparisonTheorem.lean`, #4220). Reference: Glimm–Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304–306. |
| §17.1 | **The Dobrushin total influence (Neumann-series row-sum bound)** (Issue #3511) | **Done (summable + `≤ (1−α)⁻¹`)** | `Dobrushin/InfluenceMatrixResolvent.lean`. Sums the geometric decay of #4199 into a convergent total. `matrix_summable_pow_rowSum` / `matrix_tsum_pow_rowSum_le` — abstract: for a nonnegative matrix with row sums `≤ α` (`0 ≤ α < 1`), the power row sums `∑_y (Mⁿ)_{xy}` are summable (dominated by `αⁿ`) with `∑'_n ∑_y (Mⁿ)_{xy} ≤ (1−α)⁻¹` (via `summable_geometric_of_lt_one` / `tsum_geometric_of_lt_one` and `Summable.tsum_mono`). `isingTotalInfluence G β J x = ∑'_n ∑_y (Cⁿ)_{xy}` — the **total Dobrushin influence** at `x`, the row sum of the Neumann series `∑_n Cⁿ` (formally the resolvent of `I−C`; the matrix identity `(I−C)⁻¹ = ∑_n Cⁿ` is not formalized). `isingInfluenceMatrix_summable_pow_rowSum` / `isingTotalInfluence_le` — for `0 ≤ βJ` and `βJ·Δ(G) < 1`, the row sums are summable with total `≤ (1 − Δ(G)·tanh(βJ))⁻¹`; `one_le_isingTotalInfluence` — the total is `≥ 1` (the identity `n = 0` contribution). This resolvent row sum is the coefficient of the Dobrushin comparison theorem `|⟨f⟩_η − ⟨f⟩_{η'}| ≤ ∑_{x,y} ((I−C)⁻¹)_{xy}·osc_x(f)·[η,η' differ at y]`; that comparison theorem is now formalized as `gibbsExpectationBC_dist_le_resolvent_sum` (`Dobrushin/ComparisonTheorem.lean`, #4220). Reference: Glimm–Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304–306. |
| §17.1 | **The Dobrushin resolvent matrix `R = ∑ₙ Cⁿ` and its fixed point** (Issues #4201, #3511) | **Done (entry-wise resolvent + `R = I + C·R`)** | `Dobrushin/DobrushinResolvent.lean`. The entry-wise Neumann series of the influence matrix, the coefficient of the Dobrushin comparison theorem. `dobrushinResolvent G β J x y = ∑ₙ (Cⁿ)_{xy}`; `isingInfluenceMatrix_summable_pow_apply` — per-entry summability (each entry `≤` the row sum `≤ αⁿ`, dominated by the geometric series, for `0 ≤ βJ` and `βJ·Δ(G) < 1`). `dobrushinResolvent_nonneg`; `dobrushinResolvent_rowSum` — `∑_y R_{xy} = isingTotalInfluence x` (swapping the finite row sum with the Neumann series via `Summable.tsum_finsetSum`); `one_le_dobrushinResolvent_diag` — `R_{xx} ≥ 1` (the identity `n = 0` term). The substantive result `dobrushinResolvent_fixed_point` — the **resolvent equation** `R = I + C·R`, i.e. `R_{xy} = [x = y] + ∑_z C_{xz}·R_{zy}` (split off the `n = 0` identity via `Summable.tsum_eq_zero_add`, write `C^{n+1} = C·Cⁿ` via `pow_succ'`, swap the finite `z`-sum with the series). The Neumann series solves `(I − C)R = I`; the Dobrushin comparison theorem `|⟨f⟩_η − ⟨f⟩_{η'}| ≤ ∑_{x,y} R_{xy}·osc_x(f)·[η,η' differ at y]` is now formalized (`Dobrushin/ComparisonTheorem.lean`, #4220); the matrix-inverse identity `R = (I − C)⁻¹` is not separately formalized. Reference: Glimm–Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304–306. |
| §17.1 | **The single-site conditional expectation of a general observable** (Issues #4201, #3511) | **Done (two-point formula + local-observable comparison)** | `Dobrushin/SingleSiteObservableComparison.lean`. Extends the single-site conditional distribution from indicators to arbitrary observables. `gibbsExpectationBC_singleton_eq` — for the free site `{x}` with the rest frozen to `η`, the boundary-condition Gibbs expectation of any `f` is the two-point convex combination `⟨f⟩^η_{x} = p·f(η[x↦up]) + (1−p)·f(η[x↦down])` with `p = singleSiteUpProbBC` (holds for arbitrary real `β, J, h`; via the collapse `sum_F_boltzmannBC_singleton` and the up/down-probability identities). `isingSingleSiteUpProb_nonneg` / `isingSingleSiteUpProb_le_one` — `p ∈ [0,1]`. `LocalAtSite x f := ∀ σ σ', σ x = σ' x → f σ = f σ'` — `f` depends on the configuration only through `σ_x`. `gibbsExpectationBC_singleton_localObs_dist_le` — the single-site comparison: for `f` local at `x`, `0 ≤ βJ`, and boundary conditions agreeing off `{y}`, `|⟨f⟩^η_{x} − ⟨f⟩^{η'}_{x}| ≤ tanh(βJ)·|f(η[x↦up]) − f(η[x↦down])|` (the difference factors as `(p(η) − p(η'))·(f_up − f_down)`, bounded by the influence `singleSiteUpProbBC_dist_le`). This lifts the single-site influence bound from the up-indicator to a general local observable — the per-site step of the Dobrushin comparison theorem. The full multi-site comparison theorem is now formalized (`Dobrushin/ComparisonTheorem.lean`, #4220). Reference: Glimm–Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304–306. |
| §17.1 | **Heat-bath single-site invariance of the BC Gibbs measure** (Issues #4201, #3511) | **Done (μ^η_Λ(K_x f) = μ^η_Λ(f))** | `Dobrushin/HeatBathInvariance.lean`. The first telescoping step of the Dobrushin comparison theorem: the finite-volume boundary-condition Gibbs measure is invariant under the single-site **heat-bath operator** `heatBath = K_x`, `K_x f (σ) = ⟨f⟩^σ_{x}` (the single-site conditional expectation at `x` with the rest of `σ` as boundary). `gibbsExpectationBC_heatBath_invariant` — `⟨K_x f⟩^η_Λ = ⟨f⟩^η_Λ` for any free site `x ∈ Λ` (re-sampling the spin at `x` from its conditional law does not change the measure: finite-volume heat-bath/DLR consistency). Key supporting results: `sum_indicator_agreesOff_erase` — the `{x}`-coordinate split `∑_σ 1[agreesOff Λ η] g = ∑_τ 1[agreesOff (Λ.erase x) η] (g(τ[x↦↑]) + g(τ[x↦↓]))` for `x ∈ Λ` (via `Finset.sum_bij'` to a `Finset` product), and `gibbsExpectationBC_singleton_boundary_update` (with the `boltzmannWeightBC`/`partitionFunctionBC`/`agreesOff` analogues) — the single-site conditional at `x` ignores the boundary value at `x` itself. The proof collapses each numerator over `x` and reduces to the per-coordinate heat-bath identity `K_x f(τ)·(w(τ↑)+w(τ↓)) = f(τ↑)·w(τ↑)+f(τ↓)·w(τ↓)`. The full multi-site Dobrushin comparison theorem is now formalized (`Dobrushin/ComparisonTheorem.lean`, #4220). Reference: Glimm–Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304–306. |
| §17.1 | **The single-site oscillation of an observable** (Issues #4201, #3511) | **Done (oscillation API + `siteOsc x (K_x f) = 0`)** | `Dobrushin/SiteOscillation.lean`. The Dobrushin per-site oscillation `siteOsc x f = sup_σ |f(σ[x↦↑]) − f(σ[x↦↓])|` (the maximum change in `f` from flipping the spin at `x`; a `Finset.sup'` over the finite configuration space), the quantity propagated by the heat-bath operator in the comparison theorem. `abs_sub_update_le_siteOsc` — the per-configuration bound `|f(σ[x↦↑]) − f(σ[x↦↓])| ≤ siteOsc x f`; `siteOsc_nonneg`; `siteOsc_le_of_forall` — an upper bound from a uniform per-configuration bound; `siteOsc_heatBath_self` — `siteOsc x (K_x f) = 0` (the heat-bath operator removes the oscillation at its own site, since `K_x f` ignores the spin at `x`). Equivalent to the usual oscillation over pairs of configurations agreeing off `{x}` (two spin values). Next: the oscillation propagation `siteOsc y (K_x f) ≤ siteOsc y f + C_{xy}·siteOsc x f` toward the full comparison theorem (now formalized, `Dobrushin/ComparisonTheorem.lean`, #4220). Reference: Glimm–Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304–306. |
| §17.1 | **Oscillation propagation under the heat-bath operator** (Issues #4201, #3511) | **Done (the per-step Dobrushin inequality)** | `Dobrushin/OscillationPropagation.lean`. The heart of the Dobrushin comparison theorem: applying the single-site heat-bath operator `K_x` controls the oscillation vector by the influence matrix. `siteOsc_heatBath_le` — for `0 ≤ βJ`, `siteOsc y (K_x f) ≤ siteOsc y f + C_{xy}·siteOsc x f` with `C_{xy} = isingInfluence = tanh(βJ)·[y∼x]`. Proof (for `y ≠ x`, σ₊/σ₋ = σ flipped at `y`): with the convex-combination formula `K_x f σ = p(σ)·f(σ[x↦↑]) + (1−p(σ))·f(σ[x↦↓])` (`gibbsExpectationBC_singleton_eq`), the difference decomposes as `p₊(f₊↑−f₋↑) + (1−p₊)(f₊↓−f₋↓) + (p₊−p₋)(f₋↑−f₋↓)` (`heatBath_diff_abs_le_aux`); the first two terms are a convex combination of `y`-oscillations (`≤ siteOsc y f` since `p ∈ [0,1]`, with the update-commutation `(σ[y↦s])[x↦t] = (σ[x↦t])[y↦s]`), the third is `|p₊−p₋|·|f₋↑−f₋↓| ≤ C_{xy}·siteOsc x f` (influence bound `singleSiteUpProbBC_dist_le` for `y∼x`, `= 0` for `y≁x` via `singleSiteUpProbBC_eq_of_not_neighbour`). At `y = x` the oscillation is removed (`siteOsc_heatBath_self`). Also `isingInfluence_nonneg`. This is the per-step inequality `d(K_x f) ≤ d(f) + C·column`; iterating it over the sites of `Λ` and feeding the resolvent `R = ∑ Cⁿ` gives the comparison theorem (now formalized, `Dobrushin/ComparisonTheorem.lean`, #4220). Reference: Glimm–Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304–306. |
| §17.1 | **The Dobrushin resolvent comparison inequality** (Issues #4201, #3511) | **Done (`d ≤ b + C·d ⇒ d ≤ R·b`)** | `Dobrushin/ResolventComparison.lean`. The matrix-analytic core of the comparison theorem: solving the one-step Dobrushin inequality via the Neumann resolvent. `vector_le_resolvent_of_le_add_mul` — abstract: for a nonnegative matrix `C` whose powers are per-entry summable with row sums `∑_y (Cⁿ)_{xy} → 0`, a nonnegative `d` with `d_x ≤ b_x + ∑_y C_{xy} d_y` satisfies `d_x ≤ ∑_y (∑_n (Cⁿ)_{xy}) b_y`. Proof: the finite Neumann partial bound `d_x ≤ ∑_y (∑_{k<n} (Cᵏ)_{xy}) b_y + ∑_y (Cⁿ)_{xy} d_y` (monotone in `n` from the one-step inequality, base `n=0` is `d_x ≤ d_x`), then `n → ∞`: the first term `→ ∑_y R_{xy} b_y` (`Summable.tendsto_sum_tsum_nat` + `tendsto_finset_sum`), the tail `∑_y (Cⁿ)_{xy} d_y → 0` (squeezed by `(∑_z d_z)·∑_y (Cⁿ)_{xy} → 0`), closed by `ge_of_tendsto'`. `dobrushin_resolvent_comparison` — the Ising specialization (`C = isingInfluenceMatrix`, `R = dobrushinResolvent`) for `0 ≤ βJ` and `βJ·Δ(G) < 1`, consuming the influence matrix's geometric decay (#4199). Combined with the heat-bath telescoping (which produces the `d ≤ b + C·d` hypothesis from a boundary-condition difference) this gives the comparison theorem (now formalized, `Dobrushin/ComparisonTheorem.lean`, #4220). Reference: Glimm–Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304–306. |
| §17.1 | **The multi-site boundary influence bound** (Issues #4201, #3511) | **Done (boundary-direct term of the comparison telescoping)** | `Dobrushin/BoundaryInfluence.lean`. The "boundary direct term" `b_x` of the Dobrushin comparison theorem: how the single-site conditional law at `x` responds to several differing boundary sites. `singleSiteUpProbBC_eq_of_agreesOn_neighbour` — locality: agreement on the neighbours of `x` gives equal conditional up-probability. `singleSiteUpProbBC_agreesOff_dist_le` — for `0 ≤ βJ`, two boundary configs agreeing off a set `S` change the up-probability by at most `#(S ∩ nbr(x))·tanh(βJ)` (the total influence of the differing boundary neighbours; proved by telescoping the single-flip influence bound `singleSiteUpProbBC_neighbour_dist_le` over `S ∩ nbr(x)` one site at a time, by `Finset.induction_on` with the second configuration generalized). `gibbsExpectationBC_singleton_localObs_agreesOff_dist_le` — the local-observable version `|⟨f⟩^η_{x} − ⟨f⟩^{η'}_{x}| ≤ #(S ∩ nbr(x))·tanh(βJ)·siteOsc x f`. This is `b_x` for the comparison assembly; combined with the heat-bath invariance and the resolvent comparison (#4204/#4207) it gives the comparison theorem (now formalized, `Dobrushin/ComparisonTheorem.lean`, #4220). Reference: Glimm–Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304–306. |
| §17.1 | **The general single-site Dobrushin comparison inequality** (Issue #4214) | **Done (per-site Dobrushin inequality for general observables)** | `Dobrushin/SingleSiteGeneralComparison.lean`. Generalizes the boundary-direct term from observables local at `x` to **arbitrary** observables `f` — needed by the comparison capstone, which applies the heat-bath operator `K_x` to non-local partial-sweep results. `abs_sub_update_spin_le_siteOsc` — changing the spin at `x` between any two values changes `f` by at most `siteOsc x f`. `agreesOff_dist_le_sum_siteOsc` — the **observable telescoping** `|f η − f η'| ≤ ∑_{y∈S} siteOsc y f` for `η, η'` agreeing off `S` (flip the differing spins one at a time via `Finset.induction_on`, each flip bounded by the previous lemma). `heatBath_agreesOff_dist_le` — the **general single-site comparison** (per-site Dobrushin inequality): `|K_x f η − K_x f η'| ≤ #(S ∩ nbr(x))·tanh(βJ)·siteOsc x f + ∑_{y∈S} siteOsc y f`, via the convex-combination decomposition of `K_x f = p·f(·[x↦↑]) + (1−p)·f(·[x↦↓])`, the probability-difference bound (`singleSiteUpProbBC_agreesOff_dist_le`), and the observable telescoping. `sum_isingInfluence_eq` (`∑_{y∈S} C_{xy} = #(S ∩ nbr(x))·tanh(βJ)`) and `heatBath_agreesOff_dist_le_influence` give the influence-matrix form `≤ (∑_{y∈S} C_{xy})·siteOsc x f + ∑_{y∈S} siteOsc y f`, the shape consumed by `dobrushin_resolvent_comparison`. First PR of the comparison-theorem assembly (Section A of #4214). Reference: Glimm–Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304–306. |
| §17.1 | **The heat-bath Gibbs sweep and oscillation tracking** (Issue #4214) | **Done (sweep invariance + oscillation-vector dynamics)** | `Dobrushin/HeatBathSweep.lean`. The Gibbs-sampler telescoping infrastructure for the Dobrushin comparison theorem. Because the sweep order matters, the sweep is indexed by a `List` of sites. `heatBathList G β J h xs f := xs.foldl (fun g x => heatBath G β J h x g) f` — the composite sweep `K_{x_n}∘⋯∘K_{x_1}`. `gibbsExpectationBC_heatBathList_invariant` — the finite-volume Gibbs measure is invariant under a sweep whose sites all lie in the free volume `Λ` (list induction over the single-site invariance `gibbsExpectationBC_heatBath_invariant`). `heatBathOscStep G β J x v := (y ↦ if y = x then 0 else v y + C_{xy}·v x)` and `heatBathListOscBound G β J xs v := xs.foldl (heatBathOscStep) v` — the **oscillation-vector dynamics** (a heat-bath step zeroes the oscillation at `x` and propagates `C_{xy}·v x` to the others). `heatBathListOscBound_mono` — the dynamics is monotone (nonnegative `C`-propagation, `isingInfluence_nonneg`). `siteOsc_heatBathList_le_oscBound` — the **key tracking estimate** `siteOsc y (heatBathList xs f) ≤ heatBathListOscBound xs (z ↦ siteOsc z f) y`, by list induction using `siteOsc_heatBath_self` (the swept site's oscillation is zero) and `siteOsc_heatBath_le` (the others grow by at most `C_{xz}·siteOsc x`) — exactly the `heatBathOscStep`. Second PR of the comparison assembly (Section A of #4214); next is the oscillation-vector fixed-point `d_x ≤ b_x + ∑_y C_{xy} d_y` and the capstone via `dobrushin_resolvent_comparison`. Reference: Glimm–Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304–306. |
| §17.1 | **The Dobrushin boundary weight and oscillation Lyapunov bound** (Issue #4214) | **Done (boundary fixed-point + Lyapunov bound)** | `Dobrushin/BoundaryWeight.lean`. The boundary fixed-point structure underlying the comparison bound, independent of the Gibbs measure. `dobrushinBoundaryWeight G β J S x := ∑_{y∈S} R_{xy}` — the resolvent harmonic weight of the differing boundary set `S`. `dobrushinBoundaryWeight_fixed_point` — `w_x = 1_{x∈S} + ∑_z C_{xz} w_z`, inherited from the resolvent fixed point `R = I + C·R` (`dobrushinResolvent_fixed_point`) summed over `S` (`Finset.sum_ite_eq` for the diagonal, `Finset.sum_comm` for the off-diagonal). `dobrushinBoundaryWeight_superharmonic` (`∑_z C_{xz} w_z ≤ w_x`) and `indicator_le_dobrushinBoundaryWeight` (`1_{x∈S} ≤ w_x`) are corollaries. `heatBathOscStep_nonneg` / `heatBathListOscBound_nonneg` — the oscillation-vector dynamics preserves nonnegativity. `heatBathOscStep_boundaryWeight_sum_le` — **one Lyapunov step**: `∑_y (heatBathOscStep x v)_y·w_y ≤ ∑_y v_y·w_y` (the boundary-weighted total is non-increasing, by superharmonicity, `C_{xx}=0`, and `v_x ≥ 0`; proved by erase-splitting at `x` via `Finset.add_sum_erase`). `heatBathListOscBound_boundaryWeight_sum_le` — the Lyapunov bound over any sweep. `heatBathListOscBound_boundary_sum_le_boundaryWeight` — the **boundary-sum bound** `∑_{y∈S} heatBathListOscBound xs v y ≤ ∑_x v_x·w_x` (combines the Lyapunov bound with `1_{·∈S} ≤ w`). Third PR of the comparison assembly (Section A of #4214); the capstone (PR4) connects this to the Gibbs measure and takes the sweep limit (`isingInfluenceMatrix_pow_rowSum_tendsto_zero`). Reference: Glimm–Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304–306. |
| §17.1 | **The full-volume Gibbs boundary comparison** (Issue #4214) | **Done (support-diameter bound)** | `Dobrushin/GibbsBoundaryComparison.lean`. The Gibbs-side input to the comparison capstone (PR4a). The naive "boundary-only" bound `|⟨g⟩^η_Λ − ⟨g⟩^{η'}_Λ| ≤ ∑_{y∈S} siteOsc y g` is **false** (the boundary changes the Gibbs weight, not just the observable); the correct bound is over the support diameter `Λ ∪ S`. `agreesOff_union_of_agreesOff_boundary` — a support config `σ` (for `η`) and `τ` (for `η'`) agree off `Λ ∪ S`. `gibbsExpectationBC_dist_le_sum_siteOsc_union` — `|⟨g⟩^η_Λ − ⟨g⟩^{η'}_Λ| ≤ ∑_{z∈Λ∪S} siteOsc z g`, proved from the definition via the double-sum identity `⟨g⟩^η − ⟨g⟩^{η'} = (Z_η Z_{η'})⁻¹ ∑_σ∑_τ w_η(σ)·w_{η'}(τ)·(g σ − g τ)` (`Finset.sum_mul_sum`), bounding each weight-positive support pair by `agreesOff_dist_le_sum_siteOsc` (weight-zero pairs vanish via `boltzmannWeightBC_of_not_agrees`), and `∑_σ∑_τ w_η w_{η'} = Z_η Z_{η'}`. `gibbsExpectationBC_dist_le_volume_add_boundary_siteOsc` — the interior/boundary split `≤ (∑_{x∈Λ} siteOsc x g) + ∑_{y∈S} siteOsc y g`. No high-temperature condition or sweep limit is needed for this step. Reference: Glimm–Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304–306. |
| §17.1 | **The interior-mass contraction of the heat-bath sweep** (Issue #4214) | **Done (Lyapunov sweep limit)** | `Dobrushin/SweepContraction.lean`. The sweep-limit input to the comparison capstone (PR4b). Under the Dobrushin condition `tanh(βJ)·Δ(G) < 1`, a full heat-bath sweep over `Λ` contracts the **interior mass** `MΛ(v) = ∑_{x∈Λ} v_x` of a nonnegative oscillation vector by the Dobrushin coefficient `α = Δ(G)·tanh(βJ) < 1`. `isingInfluence_rowSum_le_dobrushinCoeff` — `∑_y C_{xy} = deg(x)·tanh ≤ α`. `heatBathOscStep_interiorMass_drop` — one heat-bath step at `x∈Λ` drops the interior mass by at least `(1−α)·v_x` (it zeroes `v_x` and re-distributes at most `α·v_x`). `heatBathListOscBound_interiorMass_le` — the telescoped sweep bound `MΛ(sweep xs v) ≤ MΛ(v) − (1−α)·∑_{x∈xs} v_x` (each site, swept once, has value at sweep time ≥ its initial value, since the propagation only grows unvisited sites; list induction over `xs.toFinset`). `heatBathListOscBound_toList_interiorMass_le` — a full sweep contracts: `MΛ(sweep Λ.toList v) ≤ α·MΛ(v)`. `repeatedFullSweep Λ n := (List.replicate n Λ.toList).flatten` with `repeatedFullSweep_succ`/`_subset`; `interiorMass_repeatedFullSweep_le_pow` — `MΛ(repeated n) ≤ αⁿ·MΛ(v)`; `interiorMass_repeatedFullSweep_tendsto_zero` — the interior mass vanishes in the sweep limit (`squeeze_zero` + `αⁿ → 0`). This is the Lyapunov/potential route (far shorter than a Gauss-Seidel/`Cⁿ` matrix bridge); it discharges the interior term of the comparison capstone (PR4c). Reference: Glimm–Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304–306. |
| §17.1 | **The single-site Dobrushin comparison theorem** (Issue #4214) | **Done (capstone — the full comparison theorem, unconditional)** | `Dobrushin/ComparisonTheorem.lean`. The **capstone** of the GJ §17.1 single-site Dobrushin comparison programme (Issue #4214 §A, PRs #4215–#4220, no `sorry`/`axiom`). Under the high-temperature condition `βJ·Δ(G) < 1` (whence the Dobrushin coefficient `tanh(βJ)·Δ(G) < 1`), two finite-volume Gibbs expectations under boundary conditions `η, η'` agreeing off `S` satisfy `gibbsExpectationBC_dist_le_dobrushinBoundaryWeight`: `|⟨f⟩^η_Λ − ⟨f⟩^{η'}_Λ| ≤ ∑_x siteOsc x f · dobrushinBoundaryWeight S x`, and the resolvent form `gibbsExpectationBC_dist_le_resolvent_sum`: `≤ ∑_x ∑_{y∈S} R_{xy}·siteOsc x f` — the literal Dobrushin comparison inequality `|⟨f⟩_η − ⟨f⟩_{η'}| ≤ ∑_{x,y} R_{xy}·osc_x(f)·[η,η' differ at y]` with `R = (I−C)⁻¹` the resolvent of the single-site influence matrix `C_{xy} = tanh(βJ)·[y∼x]`. Proof: heat-bath sweep invariance (`gibbsExpectationBC_heatBathList_invariant`) gives `⟨f⟩ = ⟨g_n⟩` for `g_n` the `n`-fold sweep; the support-diameter Gibbs comparison (`gibbsExpectationBC_dist_le_volume_add_boundary_siteOsc`) bounds `|⟨g_n⟩^η − ⟨g_n⟩^{η'}|` by an interior `Λ`-sum plus a boundary `S`-sum; the oscillation-tracking estimate (`siteOsc_heatBathList_le_oscBound`) dominates both by the oscillation-vector dynamics; the boundary-sum Lyapunov bound (`heatBathListOscBound_boundary_sum_le_boundaryWeight`) caps the boundary part by `∑_x siteOsc x f·w_x`; and the interior part vanishes in the sweep limit (`interiorMass_repeatedFullSweep_tendsto_zero`, `αⁿ → 0`), discharged by `ge_of_tendsto'`. References: Glimm–Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304–306; Georgii, *Gibbs Measures*, Ch. 8. |
| §17.1 | **Dobrushin locality of single-site observables** (Issue #4214) | **Done (locality corollary + uniform bound)** | `Dobrushin/Locality.lean`. Consequences of the comparison theorem for observables local at a single site `x₀` (`LocalAtSite x₀ f`). `siteOsc_eq_zero_of_localAtSite` — `siteOsc x f = 0` for `x ≠ x₀`. `gibbsExpectationBC_localObs_dist_le_resolvent_row` — the comparison sum collapses to the single `x₀` row: `|⟨f⟩^η_Λ − ⟨f⟩^{η'}_Λ| ≤ siteOsc x₀ f · ∑_{y∈S} R_{x₀ y}` (`Finset.sum_eq_single x₀`). `gibbsExpectationBC_localObs_dist_le_totalInfluence` — the **uniform locality bound** `≤ siteOsc x₀ f · (1 − α)⁻¹` (independent of `Λ`, `S`, `η`, `η'`), via `∑_{y∈S} R_{x₀ y} ≤ ∑_y R_{x₀ y} = isingTotalInfluence x₀ ≤ (1 − Δ(G)·tanh(βJ))⁻¹` (`dobrushinResolvent_rowSum` + `isingTotalInfluence_le`). The Dobrushin uniqueness/locality estimate. Reference: Glimm–Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304–306. |
| §17.1 | **Exponential distance-decay of the Dobrushin resolvent** (Issue #4214) | **Done (`R_{xy} ≤ α^{d_G(x,y)}/(1−α)`)** | `Dobrushin/ResolventDecay.lean`. The quantitative content of Dobrushin uniqueness: the resolvent entry `R_{xy} = ∑_n (Cⁿ)_{xy}` decays exponentially in the **graph distance**. `isingInfluenceMatrix_eq_smul_adjMatrix` — `C = tanh(βJ) • G.adjMatrix ℝ` (the influence matrix is a scalar multiple of the adjacency matrix). `isingInfluenceMatrix_pow_apply` — entry-wise `(Cⁿ)_{xy} = tanh(βJ)ⁿ·#{length-`n` walks `x→y`}` (`smul_pow` + `adjMatrix_pow_apply_eq_card_walk`). `isingInfluenceMatrix_pow_apply_eq_zero_of_lt_dist` — `(Cⁿ)_{xy} = 0` for `n < d_G(x,y)` (no walk shorter than the distance, via `SimpleGraph.dist_le`). `isingInfluenceMatrix_tsum_shift_apply_le` — the shifted geometric tail `∑'_n (C^{n+d})_{xy} ≤ αᵈ·(1−α)⁻¹` (`d` kept a free variable to avoid whnf-unfolding the noncomputable graph distance). **Headline** `dobrushinResolvent_le_pow_dist` — for `0 ≤ βJ` and `βJ·Δ(G) < 1`, `R_{xy} ≤ α^{d_G(x,y)}·(1 − α)⁻¹` with `α = Δ(G)·tanh(βJ)` the Dobrushin coefficient: shifting off the first `d_G(x,y)` vanishing terms (`Summable.sum_add_tsum_nat_add`) leaves the geometric tail. Combined with the comparison theorem (`Dobrushin/ComparisonTheorem.lean`, #4220) the boundary sensitivity of a local observable decays exponentially in distance. Reference: Glimm–Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304–306. |
| §17.1 | **Dobrushin exponential spatial mixing** (Issue #4214) | **Done (`≤ siteOsc·\|S\|·α^R/(1−α)`, decay → 0)** | `Dobrushin/ExponentialLocality.lean`. The payoff of the comparison theorem: composing the locality bound (#4221) with the resolvent distance-decay (#4222) gives exponential decay of the boundary influence on a local observable in the distance to the differing set. `gibbsExpectationBC_localObs_dist_le_resolvent_pow_dist` — per-distance `\|⟨f⟩^η_Λ − ⟨f⟩^{η'}_Λ\| ≤ siteOsc x₀ f · ∑_{y∈S} α^{d_G(x₀,y)}/(1−α)` (substituting `R_{x₀ y} ≤ α^{d}/(1−α)` into the locality row, `Finset.sum_le_sum`). `gibbsExpectationBC_localObs_dist_le_card_mul_pow_radius` — **headline**: if every `y∈S` has `R ≤ d_G(x₀,y)`, then `≤ siteOsc x₀ f · \|S\| · α^R/(1−α)`, exponentially small in `R` (`pow_le_pow_of_le_one` + `Finset.sum_le_card_nsmul` + `nsmul_eq_mul`); uniform in `Λ`. `gibbsExpectationBC_localObs_dist_le_exponential_spatial_mixing` — same with the disagreement set taken internally as `{y \| η y ≠ η' y}`. `tendsto_dobrushin_radius_bound_atTop` — the bound `c·α^R/(1−α) → 0` as `R → ∞` (`tendsto_pow_atTop_nhds_zero_of_lt_one`), the qualitative decay-of-influence/uniqueness content. `α = Δ(G)·tanh(βJ)`, hypotheses `0 ≤ βJ ∧ βJ·Δ(G) < 1`. (Caveats: the `R → ∞` reading uses a fixed/cardinality-controlled `S`; `G.dist` is the junk value `0` for unreachable pairs, where the resolvent is in fact `0` — not a soundness gap.) Reference: Glimm–Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304–306. |
| §17.1 | **Finite-graph Dobrushin uniqueness — vanishing of the boundary influence** (Issue #4214 §A) | **Done (uniqueness capstone of the §17.1 Dobrushin programme; finite-graph form)** | `Dobrushin/Uniqueness.lean`. The capstone of the single-site Dobrushin comparison programme (#4215–#4223): at high temperature the finite-volume expectation of a local observable becomes independent of the boundary condition as the boundary disagreement recedes from the observable site `x₀` — the decay-of-influence content of the Dobrushin uniqueness theorem (GJ §17.1; Georgii, *Gibbs Measures*, Ch. 8). On the **finite** graph `ι` the disagreement set `{y : η_y ≠ η'_y}` has at most `Fintype.card ι` elements, so the exponential spatial mixing bound (`gibbsExpectationBC_localObs_dist_le_exponential_spatial_mixing`, #4223) is dominated by a fixed-constant radius-bound that vanishes as `R → ∞`. `gibbsExpectationBC_localObs_dist_le_card_univ_pow_radius` — the **cardinality-uniform bound** `\|⟨f⟩^η_Λ − ⟨f⟩^{η'}_Λ\| ≤ siteOsc x₀ f · (Fintype.card ι) · α^R/(1−α)` (bounding `\|{η≠η'}\| ≤ card ι` via `Finset.card_filter_le` + `Finset.card_univ`, then `mul_le_mul_of_nonneg_{left,right}` with `siteOsc_nonneg` and `α^R/(1−α) ≥ 0`), uniform in `η, η', Λ`. **Headline** `gibbsExpectationBC_localObs_boundary_influence_uniform_small` — for every `ε > 0` there is a radius `R` such that for **all** `Λ` and all boundary conditions `η, η'` agreeing on the ball of radius `R` about `x₀` (`∀ y, η y ≠ η' y → R ≤ d_G(x₀,y)`), the boundary influence is `≤ ε`: from `tendsto_dobrushin_radius_bound_atTop` (#4223) with `c = siteOsc x₀ f · card ι`, the radius-bound is eventually `≤ ε` (`Metric.tendsto_atTop` extraction), discharged by the cardinality-uniform bound. `α = Δ(G)·tanh(βJ)`, hypotheses `0 ≤ βJ ∧ βJ·Δ(G) < 1`. Axiom-free. The full `ℤ^d` *infinite-volume* Gibbs-state uniqueness (Issue #4214 §A last bullet) remains research-level — lifting these bounds to the non-`Fintype` lattice loses the `Fintype.card ι` factor. References: Glimm–Jaffe, 2nd ed., §17.1, pp. 304–306; Georgii, *Gibbs Measures and Phase Transitions*, Ch. 8. |
| §17.1 | **Card-free volume-uniform far-field Dobrushin resolvent tail** (Issue #4256 / #4214 §A, ℤ^d uniqueness lift PR 1) | **Done (card-free distance-stratified resolvent bound; ℤ^d lift ingredient)** | `Dobrushin/InfiniteVolumeUniformInfluence.lean`. The first brick lifting the finite-graph Dobrushin uniqueness (`Dobrushin/Uniqueness.lean`, #4229) to the `ℤ^d` infinite-volume setting: the finite result carries a `Fintype.card ι` factor that blows up as `Λ ↑ ℤ^d`; this PR replaces it by a **distance-stratified** bound with **no global vertex-count factor**, so it survives the thermodynamic limit. **Headline** `dobrushinResolvent_farSum_le_resolventTail` — for `d ≥ 1`, `0 ≤ βJ`, `βJ·2d < 1`, fixed `x₀ : ↑Λ`, any `S : Finset ↑Λ`, and `R` with `∀ y ∈ S, R ≤ latticeDistance d x₀.val y.val`: `∑_{y∈S} R_{x₀ y}^{GΛ} ≤ resolventTail d (2d·tanh(βJ)) R`, where `GΛ = inducedGraph (latticeGraph d) Λ` and `resolventTail d α R = ∑'_k 2(2(k+R)+1)^{d-1}·α^{k+R}·(1−α)⁻¹` depends **only on `d, α = 2d·tanh(βJ), R`** — not on `\|Λ\|` or `\|S\|`.
